All financials below are from public English-language earnings-call transcripts and Samsung IR, in **KRW**, with the consensus block auto-injected from BBG. Transcripts are summaries, not verbatim. Byline: transcripts + IR + BBG (KRW) + equity calls.

## Snapshot
Samsung Electronics is the world's largest memory maker and a vertically integrated tech conglomerate spanning four engines: **Device Solutions (DS — DRAM, HBM, NAND + Foundry + System LSI)**, **Mobile Experience (MX — Galaxy smartphones/foldables, networks)**, **Samsung Display (SDC — mobile/large OLED)**, and consumer electronics (DX/VD). The investable story is overwhelmingly DS/memory: DS sales were KRW 81.7tn (~$55bn) in Q1-2026, of which memory KRW 74.8tn, +292% y/y (Q1-2026 call, 2026-04-30). Within memory, Samsung is the #1 DRAM/NAND maker by capacity but the **laggard in HBM** — it trails SK Hynix on the NVIDIA HBM ramp and only began HBM4 mass production in February 2026 (Q1-2026 call). Its Foundry is the credible #2 logic foundry behind TSMC, increasingly winning interest as TSMC advanced-node capacity is pre-booked (briefings, May–Jun 2026). The two debates that move the stock: **HBM4 catch-up vs SK Hynix** and **Foundry share gains vs TSMC**.

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Memory — DRAM/HBM4/NAND (DS memory KRW 74.8tn, the story) · 2) Foundry / System LSI (2nm GAA, 1.4nm, Taylor TX) · 3) Devices — MX (Galaxy) + SDC (OLED panels) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | NVIDIA (HBM4) + hyperscalers for memory; foundry to AMD/Google/Tesla/Qualcomm; smartphone/consumers (Galaxy) — Samsung HBM share ~20% in 2025 |
| **Key suppliers** | WFE (ASML, AMAT, Lam, KLA, TEL — EUV the 2028 supply governor); wafers/materials; substrates (SEMCO) |

## Position in the value chain
Samsung sits in the middle of the AI-memory chain: it buys equipment and wafers upstream, and sells memory (HBM/DRAM/NAND), foundry wafers, and finished devices/panels downstream. Its differentiation: the only player that is simultaneously a top-3 HBM supplier to NVIDIA **and** a logic-foundry alternative to TSMC **and** a device OEM — but it lags SK Hynix on HBM qualification and trails TSMC on foundry yield, so it competes largely on availability and price rather than leadership.

_Foundry-vs-TSMC angle: TSMC advanced-node capacity is pre-booked through 2028+, pushing AMD, Google (Axion ~2028 / TPU), Tesla and others to engage Samsung Foundry on availability despite lagging yields (briefings 2026-05-07 / 2026-06-18). Cross-themes: [HBM / memory super-cycle](themes/hbm-memory.md) · [semicap / WFE](themes/semicap-wfe.md)._

Do not read it against any NAND-bit or HBM share number elsewhere on this page.** ➤ **Samsung's eSSD leadership is intact and it held #1 in a quarter in which the #2 gave 2.0pp to the #3 — the share moved between the two Korean-and-US challengers, not away from Samsung.**
- ⚠️ **THE FORWARD ITEM, AND IT IS AN OPTION RATHER THAN AN EVENT: SAMR's 2021 conditions on SK hynix's acquisition of [[INTC]]'s NAND business BAR it from raising PCIe/SATA eSSD prices in China above the average of the 24 months preceding the approval; the five-year compliance period EXPIRES IN DECEMBER 2026, but the restrictions DO NOT LAPSE AUTOMATICALLY and the expectation that SK hynix will petition for removal is Yonhap's INFERENCE, not a company statement.** If lifted, SK hynix regains freedom to reprice China eSSD for AI-data-centre demand and rising NAND prices on a book fed by **35%–40% of its total NAND capacity in 2026** (TrendForce). ➤ **The mechanism the article itself names is that the caps pressure the price competitiveness and profitability of CHINESE eSSD makers; the same arithmetic argues a lifted cap lets China eSSD prices rise, which is a price tailwind for every non-capped supplier into China, Samsung included — that last step is OUR inference, not the article's. Logged as a watch item; nothing has changed yet and no Samsung number here is affected.**

**🆕 2026-08-27 — THE RUBIN ULTRA 8-Hi DE-SPEC, QUANTIFIED — READ-THROUGH, NOT A COMPANY DATAPOINT.** ⚠️⚠️ **DAMNANG (Substack) — INDEPENDENT AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER: no rating, no PT, no model; the 8-Hi premise is self-described as *"UNCONFIRMED information based on industry checks"* and the revenue figures as *"scenario SENSITIVITIES, not company guidance or shipment forecasts."*** ("What Does Rubin Ultra's 8-Hi HBM Mean?", 2026-08-10 · "HBM Density Peak", 2026-08-20 — full working is on [[SKHYNIX]] and [[NVDA]]) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/What_Does_Rubin_Ultras_8-Hi_HBM_Mean__-_Damnangs_Substack.html) · [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/HBM_Density_Peak__Why_AI_Value_Is_Moving_From_Memory_to_Connectivity.html)
- 🔴 **THE HEADLINE FOR ANY HBM SUPPLIER: HBM REVENUE FALLS FAR LESS THAN THE BIT COUNT, AND MAY NOT FALL AT ALL.** 12-Hi → 8-Hi lifts effective output ~**1.63x** (up to 1.5x stacking time × 1.084x stack yield at 98% per-core-die). If [[NVDA]] absorbs it all into extra GPU shipments, **total HBM revenue lands at ~81.5% of plan at 8-Hi pricing of 50% of planned HBM4E, and ~98% at 60%** — versus the naive bits-based ~73%. The naive number is too bearish because *"the CORE DIE COUNT is 8 versus 12, about two thirds, and the BASE DIE goes in the same"* either way. ➤ **The falsifiable bar: *"if 12-Hi HBM4E could not actually meet more than 81.5% of the required volume, this despec can actually result in HIGHER revenue."***
- 🔴 **THE COMPETITIVE AXIS SHIFTS, AND THIS IS THE PART THAT MATTERS FOR THIS PAGE: *"In the regime where 12-Hi and 16-Hi stack height was itself the AXIS OF DIFFERENTIATION, high-stack qualification and stacking technology carried a LARGE PREMIUM. If the life of 8-Hi extends, **YIELD, CAPACITY, SUPPLY STABILITY and BASE DIE FUNCTIONALITY carry more weight.**"*** ➤ **A durable 8-Hi node moves competition away from the stack-height qualification lead and toward capacity and yield — the dimensions on which the trailing HBM suppliers are closest to the leader. Directionally favourable to a share challenger, unfavourable to whoever was earning a high-stack premium.** ⚠️ **Directional only. No share forecast, no ASP, no volume, and the underlying spec is unconfirmed — three independent sources this week ([[NVDA]] itself via MS, Jefferies' conference recap, and this author) all say the Rubin Ultra configuration is NOT FINAL.**
- **THE CAUSE IS A QUALIFICATION FAILURE, NOT A DEMAND FAILURE:** NVDA has been asking **~16Gbps pin speed on HBM4E**, and *"the HBM4E samples submitted so far by the THREE MEMORY MAKERS are reportedly having a harder time than expected passing qualification at that spec"*, against HBM4 already holding **10-11Gbps vs an 8Gbps JEDEC baseline**. ⚠️ **The author cautions the newly requested HBM4 pin speed *"comes close to the 15 to 16Gbps"* asked of 4E — so the HBM4 / HBM4E label is NOT a clean bandwidth distinction and must not be used as one in a bit or ASP model.** ✅ **Independently corroborated in direction by Jefferies (08-27): the de-spec is *"a means of STRETCHING A CONSTRAINED DIE POOL and INCREASING ACCELERATOR SHIPMENTS… it does not undermine structural bit growth and, if anything, REINFORCES that demand is running ahead of available supply."***

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-26 — WHY SAMSUNG STRUCTURALLY *CANNOT* MATCH [[SKHYNIX]] ON BUYBACKS. IT IS A LEGAL CEILING FROM THE CIRCULAR OWNERSHIP STRUCTURE, NOT A CAPITAL-ALLOCATION PREFERENCE — AND IT IS THE MISSING PIECE UNDER EVERY "SAMSUNG SHOULD RETURN MORE" ARGUMENT ON THIS PAGE.** (Jefferies · **Shawn Park**, answering a direct question, relayed by Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, *"While You Were Sleeping"*, 2026-08-26 — *equity sales & trading product, explicitly NOT research.*)
- The founding family controls Samsung Electronics through **Samsung C&T (028260 KS)**, which controls **Samsung Life (032830 KS)** via a **19.3%** stake.
- Stakes in **Samsung Electronics (005930 KS)**: **Samsung Life 8.6% · Samsung C&T 5.1% · Samsung F&M (000810 KS) 1.5%.**
- 🔴 **Samsung Life + Samsung F&M together exceed 10%, and *"Korea law prohibits FINANCIAL subsidiaries owning more than 10% in an INDUSTRIAL subsidiary."***
- 🔴 **Therefore: *"This circular ownership structure makes Samsung Elec RELUCTANT TO BUY BACK & CANCEL SHARES, which could lead to FORCED SELLING of Samsung Elec by both Samsung Life & Samsung F&M due to the 10% limit, raising speculation DIVIDEND PAYOUT WILL BE THE MAIN TOOL to lift return than direct buyback & cancellation."*** *(Buying back and cancelling shrinks the denominator, mechanically raising Life's and F&M's percentage stakes through the cap.)*
- ⚠️ **THE WORKAROUND BEING SPECULATED LANDS DIRECTLY ON THIS WIKI'S STANDING SAMSUNG SHARE-CLASS TRAP: *"Some speculate Samsung Elec may just do buyback & cancellation MORE FOCUSED ON SAMSUNG ELEC PREF (005935 KS) given NO IMPACT on Sam Life & F&M stakes in Samsung Elec Ord."*** ➤ **A preferred-only cancellation changes the common-vs-preferred share count asymmetrically. Any EPS or payout-per-share series built across the two classes must be checked against which class is actually being retired.**
- **The market is already voting for the indirect route: on 08-26 **Samsung C&T +5.7%, Samsung Life +10.4%, Samsung F&M +5.7%** — *"as street sees INDIRECT METHOD as the way to go for SEC to raise shareholders' return given complicated circular holdings."*** (Samsung Electronics itself +1.8% on the day, SK Hynix +0.6%.)
➤ 🔴 **THIS RECONTEXTUALISES THE 08-25 ENTRY ON THIS PAGE (W15tn of the Aug-Nov buyback already booked as a 2026 expense; the residual W60-80tn decided "mostly in late-Jan 2027"). The constraint on the residual is not management appetite or cash — it is a statutory ownership cap that no amount of FCF relieves. And it sits directly underneath UBS's stated preference for Samsung OVER SK Hynix (below), whose payout policy has no such ceiling.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-27 — UBS KOREA SUMMIT: SAMSUNG IS UBS'S **KEY CALL BUY** AND RANKS ABOVE SK HYNIX, AND THE 2027-ONWARD POLICY IS WHAT MATTERS.** (UBS · **Nicolas Gaudois** / Jimmy Yoon / Luke Yoo / Randy Abrams, 2026-08-27.)
- ***"As for Samsung, we think MORE COULD BE DONE BEFORE YE post the 3Q dividend, and buybacks are part of the toolkit. But **what matters more from here will be the 2027-ONWARDS shareholders return policy.** We believe a **50% FCF return will be the MINIMUM CASE** (and currently our forecast)."***
- **Pecking order: Memory > MLCC > Semiscap > PCB. Stocks: **SAMSUNG (Key Call Buy) > [[SKHYNIX]]** > SEMCO > Eugene Tech > ISU Petasys > Simmtech > Hansol Chem (all Buy); cautious on Neutral-rated LGD and LGE, Sell-rated Hanmi.** ⚠️ *An explicitly stated house preference ranking — NOT a derived CY-sum artefact.*
- ⚠️ **ARITHMETIC FLAG: assuming 50% FCF returns for both names in 2027-28, UBS gets **Won378tn combined in 2027 and Won433tn in 2028 = 14% and 16% of combined market cap.** W378tn at a 50% ratio implies ~W756tn of combined FCF, which cannot be bridged to BofA's ~W170-200tn Hynix FCF figures. **UBS shows no bridge; use the %-of-market-cap figures, not the absolute won aggregate.***
- **CAPEX TIMING — the semicap read on Samsung specifically: **P5-1 receives equipment from JULY 2027 onwards** (SK Hynix's Y1 starts February 2027). ➤ **Consistent with, and dating, the 08-25 Samsung IR line that capex is going into *"shell fab / infrastructure construction rather than equipment"*: the shells are being built now and convert to tool orders in mid-2027.**
- **S/D: *"Memory undersupply will get WORSE in 2027 — whilst this is UBS' view, it also is what BOTH Samsung and SK Hynix expect."* **Current DRAM demand-fulfilment ratio 60%**, with unmet demand spilling into 2027 and captured via LTAs.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-26 — HOT CHIPS 2026: THE FULL THREE-STEP HBM ROADMAP, WITH THE MIDDLE STEP THIS WIKI HAD NOT CARRIED.** (Wccftech / ServeTheHome, relayed by Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26.) **Samsung laid out standard HBM → custom HBM (cHBM) → zHBM:**
- **cHBM: uses ADVANCED LOGIC processes to add SoC-like functionality to the base die (versus a standard base die that *"mainly handles data and test functions"*), *"potentially OFFLOADING WORKLOADS FROM THE xPU and freeing silicon for more compute."* The advanced-logic base die lets Samsung *"replace the conventional HBM PHY with a more compact D2D interface"* — and **from HBM4 to HBM5 Samsung expects this to SIGNIFICANTLY REDUCE the area devoted to PHY and D2D interfaces.***
- **The thermal cost, and the answer: packing more logic into a smaller footprint *"concentrates heat, creating localized thermal hotspots."* Samsung's fix is the **Heat Path Block (HPB) for cHBM4** — **covering 50% of the PHY area and designed to cut peak temperature by MORE THAN 35%.***
- **zHBM: *"vertically stacks HBM DIRECTLY ON TOP OF THE XPU… reduces silicon footprint while ELIMINATING the need for a conventional 2.5D INTERPOSER,"* and by *"eliminating SerDes and reducing data-alignment overhead"* cuts I/O power. Claimed: **70% higher power efficiency, 230% greater DRAM bandwidth vs a standard HBM4e stack, and 8.3% more power freed for the XPU.*** ⚠️ *Vendor claims; no MP date. The zHBM figures match those logged 08-25 — consistent, not new.*
➤ 🔴 **THE ARCHITECTURAL POINT IS NOW BIGGER THAN ANY ONE VENDOR: in the SAME WEEK, Samsung (cHBM → zHBM), [[SKHYNIX]] (I-HBM, and hybrid bonding at HBM4E/HBM5 beyond 20 layers) and [[NVDA]] (NVHBM — its own memory controller INTO the HBM base die, +30% bandwidth / −15% power / +25% XPU die area vs HBM4E, with [[AMZN]] Annapurna as first adopter from Trainium4) all moved logic INTO or eliminated the interposer/base-die boundary. **Bullish base-die logic content and advanced packaging; bearish the conventional 2.5D interposer.** For Samsung specifically, cHBM's *"advanced logic processes"* on the base die is a FOUNDRY pull-through as much as a memory one.**
- ⚠️ **THE COMPETITIVE HAZARD IN NVHBM FOR SAMSUNG: NVDA is establishing *"a STANDARD NVHBM implementation, available from MULTIPLE memory providers,"* explicitly to *"reduce the engineering effort required to integrate and qualify memory across multiple suppliers."* **Standardisation is volume-positive and differentiation-negative** — it is the opposite of the bespoke custom-HBM engineering that Samsung's cHBM roadmap is designed to monetise.**

- 🔴 **PRICING, HARD NUMBER: *"DRAM capacity remains tight with **Samsung raising Q3 prices up to 20%**"*** (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27).

The main driver is Nvidia's procurement strategy of securing a broader range of HBM options to address THERMAL-MANAGEMENT concerns"*** (Zdnet Korea, via @jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-26). ⚠️ **Note this sits against Samsung IR's own 08-25 *"still no HBM de-spec for high-end GPUs."* [[SKHYNIX]]'s 08-26 segmentation (*"de-spec is mainly in PCs/smartphones and low-end GPU/ASIC… high-end including next-gen GPUs, NO de-spec"*) reconciles most of it, but an 8-Hi mix increase in HBM4 shipments TO NVIDIA is not obviously "low-end."**

See [[OPENAI]], [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).**
- 🔴 **AND AN EDA-SIDE CONFIRMATION THAT SAMSUNG'S DESIGN ACTIVITY IS ACCELERATING, FROM OUTSIDE THE MEMORY CHAIN: [[SNPS]] F3Q26 — *"**Korea was a notable outperformer growing 61% y/y in 3Q, driven by HBM engagements and SAMSUNG**"*, with IP strength in *"improving titles like **LPDDR6** and **die-to-die** for AI/HPC designs"*** (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27). ➤ **A leading indicator that runs AHEAD of wafer or capex series: 61% growth in EDA/IP consumption is design starts, and design starts precede the cHBM/zHBM roadmap by quarters.**
- **FOUNDRY / SILICON PHOTONICS: no new datapoint this window beyond the 08-25 IR items (foundry *"appears to be bottoming"*, more new US 2nm customers, second Taylor fab operating in 2030). ⚠️ *Logged as an explicit no-change so the absence is not read as a negative.*
- **PROXY TRADE, for anyone modelling the return story: Samsung C&T **+5.7%**, Samsung Life **+10.4%**, Samsung F&M **+5.7%** on 08-26 (Jefferies · O'Mara) — *the market's chosen expression of Samsung capital return is currently the holding companies, not the operating company.*
- ⚠️ **Trademark housekeeping, logged for completeness only: a **Samsung-Swatch trademark case** appears in Barclays' European retail daily (2026-08-27). No financial detail; no read-through extracted.**



**🆕🔴 2026-08-26 — TWO ITEMS FROM THE NVIDIA Q2 FY27 PRINT. ONE NAMES SAMSUNG DIRECTLY, AND IT IS A FOUNDRY/EDA DATAPOINT RATHER THAN A MEMORY ONE.** (NVIDIA press release + Q2 FY27 earnings call, both 2026-08-26 — company primary. **No Samsung rating, PT or estimate.**)
- **NAMED USE-CASE: *"**Samsung Electronics is using NVIDIA cuLitho to achieve up to 20x greater performance in COMPUTATIONAL LITHOGRAPHY**"*** (Colette Kress, prepared remarks, in the enterprise-vertical section alongside Hudson River Trading, Jane Street and Bristol-Myers Squibb). ⚠️ **ASR decode: the live transcript renders "cuLitho" as "Nvidia Colette" — corrected against the product name. This is a manufacturing-productivity datapoint for the FOUNDRY/mask-shop side of Samsung Electronics, not a memory or HBM datapoint, and no revenue, wafer, node or cost figure is attached. "Up to 20x" is a vendor performance claim on a vendor-chosen workload.** ➜ It belongs next to the existing 2nm/foundry thread on this page rather than to the DS/memory blocks.
- 🔴🔴 **THE COMPLEX-WIDE ITEM: NVIDIA CUT ITS OWN GROSS-MARGIN GUIDE AND NAMED MEMORY PRICING AS THE CAUSE.** *"we are experiencing **EXTREME PRICING CONDITIONS IN MEMORY. The magnitude of the price increase has EXCEEDED OUR PRIOR EXPECTATIONS and are headed even higher into next year. As a result, we are resetting expectations today.**"* — **FQ3 GM 74.0% ±50bp, FQ4 trough 71-72%, FY28 settle 72-73%, from 75.0% actual, recovering only *"as executed price increases take effect in Q1"* [FQ1 FY28].** ➜ **The largest buyer of HBM in the world took a ~300-400bp margin hit and told the market prices go higher into 2027. That is the demand-side confirmation this page's DS thesis has lacked, and it comes from a counterparty with every incentive to talk pricing DOWN.** ⚠️⚠️ **IT DOES NOT ADJUDICATE THIS PAGE'S BIGGEST OPEN NUMBER. The UBS blended-Samsung HBM ASP of **+90% y/y in '27E** versus JPM's industry **+42% y/y** (both logged 08-24, a 48pp gap on genuinely different bases) is untouched by NVIDIA's language — which rules out the low end of the range and confirms direction, nothing more. Nor does NVIDIA name a supplier anywhere in the cost commentary: Kress refers only to *"all three major memory suppliers,"* so there is no Samsung allocation, share or qualification datapoint here.** ⚠️ **Near-term offset: NVIDIA inventory $21.4bn (Jan-26) → $32bn (Jul-26) ahead of the Vera Rubin launch — memory already purchased and sitting in the customer's working capital. A pull-forward, not incremental demand.** [Source — call](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NVDA_conf_call_transcript_ago_26.html) · [Source — press release](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NVIDIAAn.html)

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-25 — SAMSUNG IR, ON THE RECORD, TAKES THE OTHER SIDE OF THE DE-SPEC TRADE ON THE SAME DAY THE SELL-SIDE DESKS SAY THE OPPOSITE — AND SAYS THE 2026 CAPEX IS GOING INTO SHELLS, NOT TOOLS.** BofA hosted a **virtual group meeting with Samsung Electronics IR** as part of its 24-27 Aug memory/Korea tech tour. Seven takeaways (BofA / Simon Woo, *"Simon's tech check (26 Aug): Samsung virtual meeting takeaways"*, 2026-08-25 — *company primary relayed by BofA, not a BofA view*):
- **(1) Buyback accounting:** **W15tn of share-buyback spending (Aug-Nov) has ALREADY been counted as a 2026 EXPENSE** — employee compensation / special bonus using **10.5% of semis-business OP**. ⚠️ *A P&L item, not a capital return, and it lands inside the 2026 numbers the Street is modelling.*
- **(2) The remaining return is a JANUARY-2027 decision:** after **W30tn of 3Q dividends** against total guidance of **W90-110tn**, the **W60-80tn of unpaid FCF "will likely be decided mostly in late-Jan 2027."**
- **(3) LTAs do NOT fix price:** *"memory ASP remains **negotiable on a quarterly basis even under LTAs**"* — LTAs target **60-70% of total wafer capacity**. 🔴 **The company itself capping the "LTAs smooth the cycle" bull leg: volume is locked, price is not.**
- **(4) 🔴 NO HBM DE-SPEC:** *"DRAM supply remains tight but **still no HBM de-spec for high-end GPUs**."* **Logged as the company's position AGAINST the Jefferies / SemiAnalysis / TrendForce checks published the same day (see Debate / thesis) — falsifiable inside 1-1.5 months.**
- **(5) 🔴 CAPEX MIX — SHELLS, NOT EQUIPMENT:** *"aggressive capex is focused mainly on **shell fab / infrastructure construction rather than equipment**."* **Direct read-through against MS's same-day WFE raise, which underwrites ~500kwpm of DRAM greenfield ([[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[TOKYOELEC]], [[ASML]], `themes/semicap-wfe`).**
- **(6) Wafer adds are HBM-only:** *"memory wafer capacity expansion is **mainly for HBM (minimal for conventional DRAM and NAND)**."*
- **(7) Foundry bottoming:** *"appears to be bottoming, with new growth opportunities at the **2nm node (more new US customers)"***; **2nd Taylor US fab operation likely starts 2030**; foundry **not yet profitable after special bonus payments**, profit possible **2028-30**.

**🆕 2026-08-25 — BofA's own overlay on the above (BofA / Simon Woo, same note):** **>10% upside to guided payable FCF (W90-110tn)**; possibility of **another W30tn dividend for 4Q**; buybacks after the 3Q/4Q dividends *"likely far higher than W30tn, as many institutional investors said they prefer buybacks over dividends"*; **memory ASP broadly stable through 2027 after a solid 2H26 rally — DRAM up 20%+ QoQ in 3Q, then high-single-digit% in 4Q**; **DRAM OP margin therefore ~80%+/-**. BofA reads Samsung's **"more disciplined SPE capex (particularly on conventional DRAM and NAND) together with heavy shareholder returns"** as **positive for memory peers via reduced price competition** ([[SKHYNIX]], [[MU]], [[KIOXIA]], [[SNDK]]).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — HOT CHIPS 2026: A THREE-STEP MEMORY ROADMAP ON THE RECORD — standard HBM → custom HBM (cHBM) → zHBM.** (Jefferies Asia TMT sales · Conor O'Mara relaying Wccftech + ServeTheHome, 2026-08-25)
- **cHBM** uses **advanced logic processes on the base die** to add SoC-like function, offloading work from the xPU, and replaces the conventional **HBM PHY with a compact D2D interface**; Samsung expects PHY/D2D area to shrink materially **from HBM4 to HBM5**. Thermal cost handled by a **Heat Path Block (HPB) for cHBM4 covering 50% of the PHY area, cutting peak temperature >35%**.
- **zHBM** stacks HBM **vertically directly on top of the XPU**, eliminating the 2.5D interposer and the SerDes. Claimed: **70% higher power efficiency and 230% greater DRAM bandwidth vs a standard HBM4e stack**, and **8.3% more power freed for the XPU**.
⚠️ **Vendor claims from a conference presentation — no third-party benchmark, no MP date.** 🔴 **Why it matters beyond Samsung: BofA's Hot Chips read is that "memory (not compute) is now the key constraint," that migration to these architectures INCREASES WFE intensity, and that TSV formation, hybrid bonding and process control are the favoured tool steps** (BofA / Vivek Arya relayed by Brian Fenske, 2026-08-25) — the *bull* offset to the same-day de-spec bear on the back-end names ([[BESI]], [[TER]], [[AMAT]], [[KLAC]]).

Logged as a pointer to chase, not a confirmed win.**

- ➜ **THIS RESOLVES THE 08-21 THREE-WAY DISAGREEMENT ON THIS PAGE IN FAVOUR OF THE MORGAN STANLEY / JPM READ.** MS called the package *"slightly below expectations"* against a buy-side **>W110tr** bar; JPM warned the missing buyback *"could be a near-term disappointment to recently elevated investor expectations"*; Citi called the quarterly dividend **sustainable** and reiterated **TP W450,000**. **An 8.7% single-day decline is the sell-the-news outcome MS and JPM described, not the re-rating trigger Citi and KB described.** ⚠️ **Recorded as a market outcome, not as a thesis verdict — every house below kept its rating and its target through the move, and UBS's PT is unchanged at W535,000 against a price that is now 2.08x below it.**

Never blend the two, and never read a preferred yield against a common price (the standing common-vs-preferred trap on this name).**

  - **GS — a MISS versus the press:** *"Based on recent media reports mentioning a potential shareholder return of ~W150tn this year, the guidance of W100tn at the midpoint was likely LOWER than consensus expectations."* But GS immediately reframes it as a floor: **even holding the 50%-of-FCF policy, the 2027E/2028E return pool "will likely increase significantly to W179tn / W232tn respectively."**

That is the strongest quantitative agreement in this entire cluster, and it is about the period nobody has guided yet.**

**🆕 2026-08-24 — THE MARKET'S VERDICT ON FRIDAY'S CAPITAL-RETURN ANNOUNCEMENT WAS NEGATIVE, AND IT IS NOW ON THE TAPE.** **Reuters headline, 2026-08-24: _"Samsung Electronics Shares Fall After Shareholder Return Announcement"_** (via JPM Market Intelligence news links, 2026-08-24). ➜ **This settles the direction of the debate logged this morning between JPM (W30tn = only 25% of 1H26 operating CF less capex, BELOW the stated 50% FCF policy) and BofA (">W110tn payable FCF for 2026… FCF guidance will be raised by more than 10%"): the tape traded JPM's reading, not BofA's, on day one.** ⚠️ **A one-day price reaction is not a verdict on the 2026 FCF question — BofA's claim is about the FY guide, which has not been revised yet. Both stay open.**

**🆕 2026-08-25 (Tue, KST) — BofA RUNS A KOREA MEMORY VIRTUAL TECH TOUR, AND THE SAMSUNG SESSION IS THE ONE THE BUY-SIDE COULD NOT GET INTO.** (BofA Global Research · **Simon Woo**, "Simon's tech check (25 Aug)", 2026-08-24 — *corporate-access schedule*)
- **Four sessions, 25 Aug: EO Technics (039030 KS) 09:00 KST — "laser tech for WAFER CUTTING to get individual dies" plus laser marking on memory/logic; Daeduck Electronics (353200 KS) 10:00 — "PCB for FC BGA logic, LPDDR5 memory board, multi-layer board and plus GLASS SUBSTRATE, CPO"; SAMSUNG ELECTRONICS 14:00; Hana Micron (067310 KS) 16:00 — memory OSAT for Korean chipmakers, DDR5 backend capacity, Vietnam and Brazil operations.**
- 🔴 **The scheduling note is the sentiment datapoint: _"Samsung Electronics – ALREADY FULL… Samsung call already overly crowded – no more extra seats."_ Into a week when the shares just fell on the capital-return announcement, the buy-side is queuing for management access rather than walking away.**
- ➜ **Theme relevance beyond Samsung: the three non-Samsung sessions map the BACKEND/substrate layer of the memory chain — laser dicing, glass substrate + CPO-capable PCB, and DDR5 OSAT capacity. That is the layer this wiki has the least primary coverage of. See [[themes/hbm-memory]] and [[themes/cowos-packaging]].**
- **The mechanics, from the disclosure itself (after the close on 21-Aug), as restated by JPM:** (i) **W90-110tn total for the remainder of the FY24-26E programme**, performance-dependent; (ii) **W30tn special dividend in 3Q26, inclusive of ~W2.5tn of the regular dividend programme**; (iii) the **remaining W60-80tn** mix between buyback and dividend is **under review, with details after the FY26 result is concluded — January 2027.**
- 🔴 **Two exclusions from the FCF payout base that are easy to miss and both flatter the payout ratio: the FCF-based payout does NOT include the LTA-related prepayment amount** (JPM's own view: *"LTA prepayment is not part of operating CF"*) **and does NOT include the PSU purchase amount** (W15tn, largely provisioned already in the 2Q26 result). Separately, management will **purchase 53.3mn treasury shares for the PSU between Aug 24 and Nov 21, 2026.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE PREFERRED-SHARE POINT, STATED EXPLICITLY BECAUSE THIS PAGE HAS A STANDING COMMON-VS-PREFERRED TRAP: preferred shares were NOT included as part of the PSU, and there appears to be NO DPS PREMIUM to the preferred — the CY2Q26 quarterly DPS was IDENTICAL between ordinary and preferred** (JPM, 2026-08-23). That identical-DPS fact is what makes BofA's yield split below arithmetically meaningful rather than a share-class artefact.
- **Why JPM calls it a near-term disappointment despite the headline being in line:** the **W30tn absolute for 3Q26 implies only 25% of 1H26 operating CF less capex — below the stated 50% FCF payout policy.** And: *"It remains unclear why the Samsung management team chose dividend over buyback, as we believe many investors prefer buyback as a more effective option… SEC shares trade at 7.9x L12M EPS or 6.9x on 1H26-annualised EPS."* JPM attributes the **W20tn width of the range** to cash-flow uncertainty from M&A, capex and core operations — including an **FX headwind as the Won appreciated from W1,553/USD in early July to W1,384/USD.**
- 🔴 **BofA takes the OTHER side of the same disclosure and it is the more bullish read on the page: it thinks the FCF guidance is too LOW and will be raised.** *"somewhat conservative FCF guidance (our forecast reveals more than W110tn payable FCF for 2026)… we believe FCF guidance will be raised by more than 10%."* BofA's first take is *"cash-dividend-centric shareholder returns"* — against its own prior expectation of a **60:40 dividend:buyback mix.**
- **BofA's yield arithmetic, carried at both share classes separately (never blended):** the W30tn 3Q26 dividend is a **2.2% QUARTERLY yield on the preferred** and **1.6% on the common**. *"If SEC continues to pay W30tn in cash dividends every quarter, the annualized dividend yield could reach 8.8%"* on the preferred, **6.5% on the common** — *"much higher than yields offered by TSMC or US Big Tech companies."* BofA assumes **at least another W30tn in 4Q**, since unpaid FCF guidance implies **W55-75tn** remaining. **2026E DPS raised W7.6k → W9.9k**; 2027-29 dividend estimates deliberately **left unchanged below W100tn p.a.** because that policy is not yet set. Cross-name framing: **50% of FCF ≈ a high-single-digit % of market cap for BOTH SEC and [[SKHYNIX]]** — i.e. BofA calls the two return programmes *"broadly comparable"* in magnitude despite SEC being dividend-centric and Hynix buyback-centric.
- **Tape context JPM supplies:** since the **June 18 peak the stock is -26%, vs memory peers -30% and the KOSPI -24%** — i.e. SEC has outperformed the memory complex into the announcement. On the day, the desk read was blunt: *"Alibaba & Samsung lead Asian tech down"* (Jefferies · COMARA, "While You Were Sleeping", 2026-08-24) and *"Modern day investing: samsung's down, sell semis"* (@jimcramer, 2026-08-24).

**🆕 2026-08-23 — AT THE JPM ASIA TECH TOUR SAMSUNG PUT ITS LTA COVERAGE TARGET AT A MAXIMUM OF ~70%, THE HIGHEST DISCLOSED FIGURE IN THE MEMORY COMPLEX, WITH CONTRACT DURATION UP TO 5 YEARS.** (JPM · Jay Kwon, "Key Takeaways from JPM Asia Tech Tour (Seoul Part): Memory Semiconductors", 2026-08-23 — SEC, [[SKHYNIX]], [[KIOXIA]] and Nanya hosted Aug 20-21, 50+ investors.) Peer set for comparison: **SKH >50%; Kioxia 50% of volume for '27-28; Nanya 50%+ but <70%.** Pricing terms were not disclosed, but *"memory makers unanimously guided that the contract terms were favorable to the memory suppliers with sizable prepayment terms and stronger binding to both parties."*
- 🔴 **On HBM price negotiation Samsung is the aggressive one and Hynix is the diplomatic one — a genuine strategic divergence, and it is the first time this page can source it:** *"Investors found SEC's HBM pricing and wallet share commentary more aggressive vs SKH prioritizing strategic partnerships and presenting a rather balanced approach."* Both say **2027E HBM ASP negotiations are under review (at the later part of the process)**, weighing (a) market conditions incl. the non-HBM DRAM margin gap, (b) next-gen HBM cost structure, (c) HBM performance. **JPM sees upside risk to its own +43% y/y industry HBM ASP growth assumption** and calls better HBM pricing a source of potential EPS upward revision.
- **Capex discipline was uniform and it is the supply-side bull case:** Korean players reiterated **process-migration-driven bit growth over capacity expansion for the next 12-18 months**, and JPM believes **new NAND cleanroom space for both Korean suppliers arrives no earlier than 2029E — one or two phases of Samsung's Pyungtaek Fab 5 vs SK hynix's M17.** Also: *"Higher wafer capacity allocation towards HBM was a common message from Korean memory suppliers and this is likely to act as a strong source for supply constraints given wafer-to-die penalty."*
- **SIG's rating stack, for the record, because it splits the two Koreans:** **Samsung Positive (px KRW270,000) vs [[SKHYNIX]] Neutral (px KRW1,761,000)** (SIG · Mehdi Hosseini, "Memory Update: Intra-Quarter Pricing Update", 2026-08-24).

**🆕 2026-08-23/24 — HOT CHIPS: SAMSUNG'S "ZHBM" IS THE MOST AGGRESSIVE ARCHITECTURAL CLAIM ANY MEMORY MAKER MADE AT THE CONFERENCE, AND IT WOULD DELETE THE 2.5D INTERPOSER.** (Speaker: Sanghan, Samsung Memory Business, via TMT Breakout "Hot Chips Takeaways", 2026-08-23 and viksnewsletter "Hot Chips 2026: Tuning into Memory Vibes", 2026-08-24 — **conference-coverage newsletters, not broker research**.)
- Samsung laid out a **three-stage roadmap turning the HBM base die from a communications layer into a customised AI platform**, culminating in **ZHBM: vertically stacking HBM directly on the XPU, eliminating the traditional 2.5D interposer.** Removing the lateral PHY/D2D path lets I/O and TSV structures spread across the footprint rather than clustering at the edge.
- **The targets, as presented:** **~0.5 pJ/bit**, **~70% lower total DRAM power versus HBM5**, and **>2.3x higher DRAM bandwidth**. **Absolute DRAM power could fall by >100W, freeing that power for GPU compute.**
- ⚠️ **The constraint that caps the claim, and Samsung stated it itself: thermal limits currently point to roughly FOUR-HIGH stacks, not 12-high or 16-high.** Separately, **moving the memory controller into HBM could reclaim 5-10% of XPU area.** Filed also to themes/hbm-memory.
- **Competitive datapoint from the same week, from the trade press rather than a broker:** *"Samsung's DRAM capacity lead shrinks as SK Hynix expands faster"* (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-24).


**🆕🔴 2026-08-21 (/run-inbox) — THE BOARD ANNOUNCED, THE PRESS RANGE RESOLVED AT THE LOW END, AND THE THREE HOUSES THAT PUBLISHED ON IT THE SAME DAY OFF THE SAME W281,500 CLOSE DO NOT AGREE ON WHETHER IT IS A BEAT.** The remaining 2026E shareholder return is **W90-110tr**, framework **UNCHANGED** (50% of 2024-26E cumulative FCF), with **~W30tr of cash dividends in 3Q26E** plus a separate **W15tr / 53.3mn-share employee buyback running 24 Aug → 21 Nov**. **CITI reiterates BUY, TP W450,000** and calls the ~W30tr quarterly dividend **SUSTAINABLE** (⇒ ~W120tr annualised, 6.4% yield on the common); **MORGAN STANLEY calls it "slightly below expectations"** versus the recent buy-side **>W110tr** bar and leaves **OW / PT W381,000 UNCHANGED**; **KB SECURITIES** runs the growth-and-dividend case and carries **no explicit TP in this note**. ➤ **W381,000 vs W450,000 on identical facts — an 18% target gap and opposite signs on sentiment. The disagreement, not the number, is the alpha here.** (Citi · Peter Lee (AC) / Jayden Oh, "Unveiled ~W100tr Shareholder Return Plan for 2026E", 2026-08-21 07:54 ET, 12pp; Morgan Stanley · Shawn Kim w/ Ryan Kim / Duan Liu / Cindy Huang, "W90-110tr Capital Returns, Framework Unchanged", 2026-08-21 11:01 GMT; KB Securities · Jeff Kim / Changmin Lee, "A Gem of Growth and Dividends", 2026-08-21, **produced by KB Securities, distributed by Jefferies — NOT Jefferies research**.)
- 🔴 **THE DISCLOSURE, AND IT IS NOW COMPANY FILING (Dart) RATHER THAN PRESS — THE FACTS BOTH HOUSES AGREE ON.** Remaining 2026E shareholder return **W90-110tr** under the unchanged policy of returning **50% of 2024-26E cumulative FCF**; **~W30tr of cash dividends in 3Q26E, inclusive of the regular quarterly dividend**; a **separate W15tr buyback of 53.3mn common shares** for employee stock-based compensation, executed **24 August → 21 November**. Samsung has **already returned W29.3tr during 2024-25 — W20.9tr cash dividends + W8.4tr buybacks/cancellation — and that is DEDUCTED to arrive at the remaining figure**, alongside a **W9.8tr annual regular-dividend commitment** (MS). ⚠️ **The FCF definition is where the number is actually decided, and Citi spells it out: in calculating cumulative FCF, BOTH (i) upfront payments related to memory LTAs AND (ii) cash outflows related to employee stock-based compensation are DEDUCTED.** ➤ **That is the mechanism by which the LTA deposits this page has been tracking since 08-02 REDUCE the distributable pool — the LTA prepayments are a use of the same cash the 50%-of-FCF policy divides. Read it directly against the W15tr employee buyback, which is the SBC leg of the same deduction.** (Citi 08-21; MS 08-21.)
- 🔴🔴 **THE SHARPEST AND MOST TRADEABLE DISAGREEMENT IS NOT THE SIZE — IT IS THE MIX OF THE REMAINING ~W60-80tr, AND THE TWO HOUSES GUESS OPPOSITE WAYS.** **Citi expects the ~W100tr to break down as ~W60tr DIVIDENDS + ~W40tr BUYBACKS**, with **~W30tr paid in 3Q26E and the remaining ~W30tr in 4Q26E** — i.e. **dividend-heavy**. **Morgan Stanley expects the opposite: "the remaining W60-80tr of the indicated 2026 return is likely to be allocated in the form of BUYBACK/CANCELLATION, in our view."** MS then states the consequence plainly: **"a meaningful buyback would provide a more structural EPS/share-count benefit, while a dividend-heavy package would emphasize near-term cash yield."** ➤ **This is a clean, dated, binary fork with a decision date (see `## Catalysts`), and it maps onto two different stocks: Citi's version is a ~6.4% yield instrument, MS's is a share-count-shrink instrument. It also runs straight into the BofA mechanic already on this page — BofA's ~60:40 DIVIDEND:buyback call (08-20) sides with CITI, on the "10% rule" (Samsung Life / Samsung Fire must sell down against the government's 10.0% guideline) and >4.0mn individual shareholders. So it is 2-1 for dividend-heavy, with MS the outlier.** (Citi 08-21; MS 08-21; BofA · Simon Woo 08-20, already logged.)
- ⚠️ **WHY THE SAME NUMBER IS A BEAT FOR ONE HOUSE AND A MISS FOR THE OTHER — THE ANSWER IS THE BUY-SIDE BAR, NOT THE COMPANY.** MS: **"Big capital returns, slightly below expectations — a significant step-up in absolute shareholder returns, although largely expected and slightly below buy-side recent >W110tr estimate."** Adding W90-110tr to the W29.3tr already returned puts **cumulative 2024-26 shareholder returns at roughly W120-140tr** on MS's arithmetic. Citi, from the identical disclosure, frames the programme as **"~W100tr"** and reiterates Buy with **expected share-price return +59.9%, dividend yield 0.6%, total return +60.5%** off **W281,500 (21 Aug, 15:45)**, market cap **W1,645,727,428M / US$1,180,262M**. ➤ **Note carefully what the W120-140tr figure IS — it is MS's CUMULATIVE 2024-26 total, NOT an annual run-rate. Do not read it against Citi's ~W120tr ANNUALISED DIVIDEND: those two similar-looking numbers are on completely different bases and netting them would be a units error.** ➤ **The genuinely useful read is that the marginal buyer had already discounted >W110tr, so the announcement is a sell-the-news setup on MS's framing and a re-rating trigger on Citi's. The stock mark to judge it against: W281,500 on 21 Aug, versus the KRW300,000 level KB says the yield could let the shares "settle above."** (MS 08-21; Citi 08-21; KB 08-21.)
- 🔴 **AND HERE IS THE QUANTIFIED VERSION OF THAT SPLIT, WHICH IS THE SINGLE MOST USEFUL THING IN THIS CLUSTER: MS'S OWN PUBLISHED DIVIDEND YIELD IS 1.5% FOR FY26e. CITI'S IMPLIED YIELD ON THE SAME CLOSE IS 6.4%. FOUR TIMES.** Citi: assuming the **W30tr quarterly dividend is sustained** — "backed by secular memory market outlook underpinned by LTAs" — the **annualised dividend is ~W120tr, implying 6.4% on the common and 8.7% on the preferred** at the 21-Aug close. Cross-check the other way: W120tr ÷ 6,607mn = W18,163/share ÷ W281,500 = 6.45%, which reproduces Citi's 6.4% exactly.** ➤ **So MS's yield row and DPS line are pre-announcement and have not been marked to the 08-21 filing, while its PT and rating were explicitly reaffirmed. This is the recurring broker-note internal-inconsistency pattern on this page: trust the note's disclosed EVENT facts, do not trust its un-refreshed model rows. The open question this creates is worth carrying: IF the ~W30tr quarterly cadence is real, MS's own PT is being set on a dividend stream roughly a quarter of the size Citi assumes.** (Citi 08-21; MS 08-21; MS model · Shawn Kim, published 2026-08-07.)

The author's verdict on the four claims the market cites: **① the "30% discount" is a MIX ARTEFACT** — it compares company-wide ASPs and roughly two thirds of CXMT's revenue is low-priced mobile memory; **like-for-like the gap is 5-10% per CXMT's own IPO prospectus, and in SERVER products the sign FLIPS** (a July-2026 64GB DDR5 server module on CXMT silicon sold for **RMB18,999** in Chinese online retail vs **RMB18,595** for the same spec on Samsung/SK hynix silicon — CXMT **2.2% HIGHER**; in contract, CXMT was reported quoting **above Samsung's supply price of US$1,240/module**); **② it CANNOT sell cheap** — CXMT spends **>30% more than the incumbents** to produce the same memory, its dies are **~40% larger in area** and its yield falls short of the **85-90% industry standard**; **③ the "8% share" is a REVENUE number** (US$7.31bn of 1Q26 DRAM revenue = 7.7% of the world market) — on output it puts **13% of the world's DRAM WAFERS into production but converts them into only 6% of memory CAPACITY**; **④ its 1Q26 revenue +71% y/y was PRICE, not volume** — q/q, capacity shipped grew **11%** while ASP rose **57%**. ➤ **⚠️ THIS CONTRADICTS A NUMBER ALREADY ON THIS PAGE and both should stand rather than be netted: Wells Fargo (Aaron Rakers, 07-14) carries a **~24% CXMT ASP discount**; DAMNANG's argument is that any company-average discount of that shape is a product-mix artefact. It is corroborated on the physics by the SemiAnalysis STEEL teardown already logged (CXMT ~0.3 Gib/mm², i.e. materially larger die).** ➤ **The Samsung-specific verdict is two-sided and worth carrying verbatim in substance: Samsung "sells the largest share of its output to Chinese smartphone and PC manufacturers," so it is "hit first and hardest" if Chinese procurement directives extend to memory, AND what CXMT can push outside China is commodity mobile/PC DRAM rather than server product, so **Samsung meets that price competition first as well**. AGAINST that, at **4.32x forward** on 27 July — versus **SK hynix 4.69x, Micron 6.42x, CXMT ~30x** — Samsung was **the cheapest of the three, so most of the risk already sat in the price**. The author's own conclusion: the memory correction is more likely to END after the CXMT listing than to continue.** ⚠️ **NOT a broker: independent Substack, no rating, no PT.** (DAMNANG (Substack), "After CXMT's Listing, Do the Memory Three Have Further to Fall?", 2026-07-27.)

This is the mechanic behind the MS Sales read already on this page that Hynix's programme is more EPS-accretive than Samsung's.**

- 🔴 **READ THE DS LINE FIRST, BECAUSE IT IS THE WHOLE COMPANY NOW.** JPM has **DS operating profit of W370.3tn against GROUP operating profit of W373.2tn in CY2026 — i.e. essentially 100% of group profit comes from semiconductors**, with Display, DX (mobile/consumer) and Harman netting to roughly nothing. **DS revenue is W527.2tn of W719.3tn group (73%), and DRAM alone is W352.5tn (49% of group revenue).** ➤ **This decomposition answers the recurring "Samsung is a conglomerate, not a memory pure-play" objection on this page: on JPM's model it IS a memory pure-play for profit purposes from CY2026 onward. The 51.9% group operating margin that looks impossible for a company selling phones and appliances is simply DRAM/NAND margin diluted by revenue that earns nothing.**
- 🔴 **THE CY2026 EPS GAP IS BELOW-THE-LINE, SAME PATTERN AS [[SKHYNIX]], AND THAT PARALLEL IS ITSELF THE SIGNAL.** JPM is **−0.02% on revenue and −1.5% on EBIT** yet **+9.5% on EPS**. Operations cannot produce that — it is tax, non-operating income, minorities or share count. **The identical shape appears in the same analyst's SK Hynix model (−1.1% EBIT / +14.1% EPS).** ➤ **Two Korean memory models from one house, both on consensus at the operating line and both ~10-14% above it on EPS, points to a systematic house assumption below the operating line rather than two coincidences. If it is right, Street CY26 EPS is too low across Korean memory on essentially identical operating forecasts. Worth pulling the published notes to identify the line.** ⛔ **WITHDRAWN 2026-08-20 — SHARE-COUNT BASIS ARTEFACT. JPM's headline EPS is common-shares-only; BBG's consensus is a preferred-inclusive blend. At NET INCOME, JPM is 3.6% BELOW consensus. There is no below-the-line gap at Samsung and no cross-name pattern. See `## BBG annual-line correction (2026-08-20)`.**
- ⚠️ **THE CY2027 EPS "−3.8%" INVERTS ONCE OUR OWN KNOWN DATA BIAS IS REMOVED.** This wiki's CY figures are calendar-year sums of quarterly consensus, and **Korean EPS on that construction runs above BBG's annual line — ~+6.8% for Samsung.** De-biasing (W75,198 ÷ 1.068 ≈ **W70,410**) puts **JPM at +2.7%, i.e. slightly ABOVE consensus rather than below.** ➤ **Use the de-biased figure; the raw −3.8% is a sign-flipping artefact.** ✅ **BIAS CONFIRMED LIVE 2026-08-20 at +6.8% — but the EPS comparison is basis-contaminated either way; use the net-income line (−3.6%).**
- 🔴 **THE GENUINE OUT-YEAR DIVERGENCE IS THE CY2027 TOP LINE, AND IT SURVIVES EVERY ADJUSTMENT: JPM revenue −5.0% and operating profit −5.9% BELOW consensus (W941.0tn vs W990.7tn; W549.3tn vs W583.7tn).** That is roughly **W50tn of revenue and W35tn of profit**, and it is NOT a margin call — margins are within 50bp. **JPM simply has less Samsung revenue in CY2027 than the Street.** ➤ **This is the cleanest tradeable divergence in the file, and it sits on the out-year where the memory debate actually lives. Note the direction against the EPS point above: JPM is more conservative on 2027 OPERATIONS while being more generous below the line — those partly cancel at EPS, which is why the headline EPS delta looks small while the underlying disagreement is not.** ✅ **HOLDS, RESIZED 2026-08-20 to −3.5% revenue / −3.8% EBIT (~W34tn / ~W21.5tn), and now CORROBORATED at net income (−3.6%). Strongest surviving row. Note the "partly cancel" caveat no longer applies — with the EPS row withdrawn, JPM is uniformly below consensus on CY2027.**
- 🔴 **AND THE ONE WITH THE WIDEST READ-THROUGH: JPM IS ABOVE CONSENSUS ON SAMSUNG CAPEX IN BOTH YEARS — +4.2% CY26 (W79.1tn vs W76.0tn) and +8.3% CY27 (W99.3tn vs W91.7tn), on a path to W107.3tn in CY2028.** ➤ **It runs OPPOSITE to the same analyst's SK Hynix capex, which is 3-4% BELOW consensus. So JPM's Korea view is more Samsung spend and less Hynix spend than the Street — a MIX call within Korean WFE rather than a level call, and directionally a positive read for tools levered specifically to Samsung. The Samsung-over-Hynix MIX call DOES NOT EXIST and the semicap read-through is CANCELLED. See correction block.**
- **Historicals the model anchors on (consistent with the quarterly grid):** FY2024 revenue **W300,871bn**, EBIT **W32,726bn** (10.9%), EPS **W5,433**, capex **W51,406bn**; FY2025 revenue **W333,606bn**, EBIT **W43,601bn** (13.1%), EPS **W7,258**, capex **W47,522bn**. ➤ **The operating-margin path 10.9% → 13.1% → 51.9%E is the most consequential assumption on this page, and consensus independently sits at 52.7% — so this is not a JPM outlier, it is the Street's base case.**

### BBG annual-line correction (2026-08-20)

⚠️ **The consensus column in the table above is this wiki's CY-SUM (quarterly consensus added up), not BBG's annual line — and separately, Samsung's EPS carries a share-count basis trap. Placed correctly (live pull 2026-08-20), two of the five readings above are withdrawn.**

| Metric | JPM (workbook) | BBG **annual line** | Δ | Δ as printed above (vs CY-sum) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| CY2026 revenue | W719,274bn | W729,874bn | **−1.5%** | −0.02% |
| CY2026 EBIT | W373,163bn | W384,640bn | **−3.0%** | −1.5% |
| CY2026 net income | W297,408bn | W308,535bn | **−3.6%** | n/a |
| CY2026 EPS (headline, common-only) | W50,797 | W48,041 | +5.7% ⚠️ *basis mismatch* | **+9.5%** |
| CY2026 capex | W79,137bn | W78,475bn | **+0.8%** | **+4.2%** |
| CY2027 revenue | W941,029bn | W975,122bn | **−3.5%** | −5.0% |
| CY2027 EBIT | W549,257bn | W570,790bn | **−3.8%** | −5.9% |
| CY2027 net income | W436,712bn | W452,973bn | **−3.6%** | n/a |
| CY2027 capex | W99,300bn | W98,325bn | **+1.0%** | **+8.3%** |

- ⛔ **THE "BELOW-THE-LINE EPS GAP" AT SAMSUNG IS A SHARE-COUNT ARTEFACT AND IS WITHDRAWN.** JPM's headline EPS (W50,797) is struck on **common shares only** — its own net income implies **5.855bn shares**, and the workbook carries a second line, *"EPS (W) inc. pref. Shares" = W44,857*. BBG's consensus EPS implies **6.422bn shares** (`BEST_NET_INCOME` ÷ `BEST_EPS`), i.e. a **preferred-inclusive blend**. Comparing the two measures a share count, not a forecast. ➤ **At net income — which contains no share count — JPM is 3.6% BELOW consensus, entirely consistent with its −1.5% revenue and −3.0% EBIT. There is no anomaly to explain.**
- ⛔ **AND THEREFORE THE CROSS-NAME "PATTERN" IS GONE.** The claim that two JPM Korea models share a systematic below-the-line assumption does not survive: **[[SKHYNIX]] has a real (if smaller) non-operating gap of +4.9% at net income; Samsung has −3.6%, i.e. the opposite sign.** ➤ **One name, one mechanism — not a house-wide Korean assumption. Do not carry the paired framing.**
- ✅ **THE CY2027 TOP-LINE CALL HOLDS AND IS NOW THE ONLY LIVE DIVERGENCE ON THIS PAGE.** −3.5% revenue / −3.8% EBIT (~W34tn / ~W21.5tn below the Street, not ~W50tn / ~W35tn), **corroborated independently at net income (−3.6%)**, with the margin gap only **−17bp** — so "not a margin call, JPM simply has less Samsung revenue in CY2027" survives and tightens. ⚠️ **The "partly cancel against the EPS point" caveat is void now that the EPS row is withdrawn: JPM is uniformly below consensus on CY2027 at revenue, EBIT and net income alike.**
- ⛔ **THE CAPEX MIX CALL IS OVERTURNED.** On the annual line JPM is **+0.8% CY26 / +1.0% CY27 on Samsung capex — i.e. ON consensus, not 4-8% above** — and **+4.0% ABOVE on SK Hynix CY2026 capex**. So JPM is above consensus on **both** names in 2026, **more so on Hynix**, the opposite of the claimed tilt. 🔴 **"More Samsung spend, less Hynix spend than the Street" is not in the models. The read-through to [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[ASML]], [[TOKYOELEC]] and [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md) is CANCELLED — nothing should be positioned on it.**
- ⚠️ **Methodology note: our CY-sum capex for Samsung runs 3.2% (CY26) and 6.7% (CY27) BELOW BBG's annual line, while for [[SKHYNIX]] it runs 7.8%/4.0% ABOVE.** Opposite signs on the two names is exactly what manufactured a fictitious "mix" call. ➤ **For Korean names place broker annual models against `1FY`/`2FY`, never the CY-sum.**

See [reconciliation-2026-08-19](_meta/reconciliation-2026-08-19.md) §BBG resolution._

Same-day BBG consensus (52.7%) overturned that at the reconciliation step, and the DS-is-all-the-profit decomposition above is the explanation that was missing. See `_inbox/_ingest-log.md` (2026-08-19) and [## Changelog](#changelog).** ⚠️ **Mechanical hazard: forecast columns are headed by STRINGS (`2026E`) while historicals are numeric — numeric-only detection returns 2024/2025 only, or lands on growth-rate rows and yields nonsense.**


**🆕🔴 2026-08-19 — SAMSUNG FOUNDRY IS RAISING PRICES BY UP TO 15%, AND THE PRICE MAP IS GEOGRAPHIC IN A WAY THAT IS ITSELF THE STORY: CHINESE CUSTOMERS ARE ACCEPTING THE LARGEST INCREASES, AND TAIWAN — [[TSM]]'s HOME MARKET — THE SMALLEST.** (Reuters, 2026-08-19, "two people familiar with the matter"; relayed by @jukan05, @dnystedt, @firstadopter.)
- **The disclosed grid:** **SF4 (4nm) prices up 10-15% month-over-month in July vs June for US and China customers; Taiwan customers up only 5-10%. SF5 (5nm) up 10-15%.** Increases apply **to new orders**.
- **The capacity fact underneath the pricing:** **the SF4 line at Pyeongtaek has been full since late last year**, and demand from **China is "particularly strong."**
- 🔴 **Why the geographic split matters more than the headline percentage: charging Taiwanese customers the least is the behaviour of a supplier competing hardest where TSMC is strongest, while extracting the most where the customer has the fewest alternatives (China, post-export-control). Read it as evidence of a genuinely tight leading-edge market rather than of restored Samsung pricing power per se — the discount is aimed at the accounts Samsung most wants to take from [[TSM]].**
- **Same-day capacity datapoint (Korean media via @jukan05, 2026-08-19): Samsung plans to increase the number of FLOORS at its new Pyeongtaek fab from the originally planned two to THREE — specifically P5 Plants 1 and 2, which together are described as becoming the world's largest semiconductor manufacturing facility at a single site.**
- ⚠️ **Sourcing caveat, stated plainly: the pricing story is Reuters on two unnamed sources and the P5 floor-count is Korean media — neither is a company disclosure. Both are logged as reported, not as confirmed.**

KB has published a thesis with exactly one variable, which makes it easy to falsify: monthly contract prints are the whole test.**

- **2026-08-19 · Foundry pricing and capital-return comparison added.** New reported (not disclosed) datapoints: **SF4 +10-15% m/m for US/China, +5-10% for Taiwan; SF5 +10-15%; SF4 Pyeongtaek line full since late 2025; P5 Plants 1-2 redesigned from two floors to three.** Capital-return framing shifted by the [[SKHYNIX]] W40trn buyback of the same day — the page now carries the **employee-compensation dilution asymmetry** (Samsung buybacks partly not cancelled vs Hynix's 100%-cash bonuses) as the relative-value argument, superseding the previously undifferentiated "both Korean memory names face capital-return pressure" framing.
- 🔴 **THE VALUATION CLAIM, WHICH IS THE WHOLE NOTE: Samsung trades at 1.49× NTM P/BV, which discounts a long-term ROE of 13.9% — BELOW the pre-AI 2012-22 average of 15.2%, and against UBS's own estimate of 31.0%.** The stock is **down 37% from its June 19th peak**, on an average **'27-29E FCF yield of 33.9%**. ➤ **Stated plainly: the market is pricing Samsung as though the AI memory cycle leaves it structurally WORSE than the decade before AI existed. That is the cleanest statement of the bear-case-in-the-price on this page, and it is the argument to test rather than the target.**
- **The cut itself is small and specific: '26-30E operating profit lowered ~3% on average, on lower NAND ASP forecasts (running "fractionally below current contract negotiations") and slightly lower HBM bit shipments, partly offset by higher HBM ASPs.** Post-revision UBS is still **16% above '27E consensus OP.** EPS: **12/26E KRW43,532 (cons 48,296) · 12/27E KRW73,903 (cons 69,649) · 12/28E KRW84,988 (cons 68,932).** ➤ **Note the crossover — UBS is BELOW consensus for 2026 and 23% ABOVE for 2028. Every house in this run (KB, Bernstein, UBS) is below or in line near-term and above in the out-years; the memory disagreement is uniformly a duration disagreement, not a level disagreement.**
- 🔴 **THE HBM SHARE CALL, AND IT IS THE ONE TO RECONCILE ACROSS THIS PAGE: UBS forecasts Samsung SURPASSING [[SKHYNIX]] on HBM bit share in 2027 — Samsung 40%, SK Hynix 38%, [[MU]] 22%** — on lowered Samsung HBM bit shipments of **22bn Gb (+95% y/y) in 2027E, cut from 24bn prior**, with **HBM ASPs now +90% y/y in 2027 (was +68%)**. ➤ **Two houses in this run now put Samsung #1 in HBM: UBS at 40% of BITS in 2027, KB at 44% of HBM4 specifically. Different metrics, same conclusion, and it is a reversal of the multi-year SK Hynix leadership this wiki has carried. Treat "Samsung takes HBM leadership in 2027" as a live consensus-forming claim rather than a house view — and note it is being made while UBS simultaneously CUTS Samsung's HBM bit volumes, i.e. the share gain is coming from a shrinking-forecast denominator as much as from Samsung's own ramp.**
- 🆕 **DEMAND / SUPPLY FRAME, net-new numbers: UBS now models DRAM bit end-consumption +40% y/y in 2027 versus +21% in 2026E, and NAND +26% from +23%** — an ACCELERATION into 2027, driven by agentic AI pulling DDR5/LPDDR5 for conventional servers and AI-server CPU head nodes plus NAND for KV-cache and storage. **"Almost all of the incremental wafer capacity addition for DRAM is going to HBM, and no new capacity ex-China is being added to NAND."** Front-end DRAM capacity dedicated to HBM reaches **500k/690k wpm by end-2026/2027 = 24%/30% of industry capacity.** ⚠️ **The risk UBS names is not supply — it is AFFORDABILITY: "memory industry revenues are approaching US$1.61tn in 2027E."** ➤ **That $1.61tn figure is the single most useful sanity check in this run: it is the number the whole complex's 2027 estimates have to be paid for out of, and it belongs in any hyperscaler-capex cross-check.**
- **Execution / capacity: Samsung has improved HBM4 yields and performance, though thermal issues may persist — UBS believes Samsung is trying to PULL IN the 4F2 DRAM chip design rollout to 2028. It retains the ability to deploy more new wafer capacity than peers on remaining P4 floor space, plus a pull-in of P5 to first clean-room readiness by 3Q27.** **Shareholder returns: policy of returning 50% of FCF ex-M&A with a potential KRW9.8tn annual dividend floor; on UBS FCF forecasts of KRW248tn (2026E) and KRW450tn (2027E), that implies additional returns of ~KRW108tn in 2026E and ~KRW215tn in 2027E.** ➤ **UBS's implied return is roughly the MIDPOINT of KB's KRW100–200tn range, derived independently from a formula rather than from an expected announcement. Two houses, two methods, overlapping answers — the strongest form the return thesis has taken on this page.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260807_UBS_005930-KS_Key_Call-_Samsung_Electronics_-Implying_ROE_below_pre_AI.html)

**🆕 2026-08-11 — SAMSUNG PUSHES HIGH-NA EUV OUT TO ~1nm (≈2030), ABANDONING THE 2nm/1.4nm PLAN (TrendForce, via JPM / Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-11).** Rationale: *"technical hurdles and cost pressures mean the tech is still expensive and immature."* Framed as **mirroring TSMC's cautious approach** — *"[[TSM]] has only bought High-NA gear for R&D and says its 2029 A12/A13 nodes won't need it"* — leaving *"only one outlier in early High-NA EUV adoption"* (i.e. [[INTC]]). ➜ **For Samsung this is a capex-discipline positive and a foundry-competitiveness question at once: it de-risks the 2nm/1.4nm cost curve but concedes the leading-edge-tooling race to Intel on paper. Read alongside the 4nm LPU breakeven above and the Taylor-fab qualification push (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-11: "Samsung races to qualify Taylor fab as 2nm demand turns US capacity into a strategic asset").** Negative read-through logged on [[ASML]]'s page: High-NA volume becomes a single-customer book into ~2029–30.

On product, **Samsung HBM4 yield is reported to be nearing 80%** as the production race with SK hynix intensifies (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10) — the qualification datapoint that matters most for the 2027 HBM4 price negotiation, since a credible second source is precisely what caps SKH's pricing power.
