**Q2-2026 (reported 2026-07-30) — record again, by a wide margin, and management guided the market to stay UNDERSUPPLIED. Stock finished ~flat.** **Operating profit KRW 89.5tn (~$62bn)** — "a more than **19-fold increase** from a year earlier, and **nearly all of it came from its semiconductor business**, which benefited from rising chip prices driven by demand for AI servers and increased shipments of advanced high-bandwidth memory chips." **Revenue KRW 171.5tn (~$119bn), also an all-time high.** AP's headline framing: "**Samsung Chip Profit Soars 250-Fold During Relentless AI Buildout**." Reported one day after [[SKHYNIX]] also posted record earnings. (AP via GS TMT Today, 2026-07-30)
- **⚠ THESIS-DRIFT — the record marks on this page step up hard: operating profit KRW 57tn (Q1-2026) → KRW 89.5tn (Q2-2026); revenue KRW 134tn → KRW 171.5tn.** Prior values moved to the Changelog. For the durability debate on this page, this is one more quarter against the ex-Micron VP's "cycle at the top / HBM commoditizes" call and against GS's observation that 5-6x earnings "disbelieves the durability."
- **Guidance — management is now the most explicit source on this page for the undersupply call:** "expects demand for its memory products to **remain strong in the second half**, driven by continued expansion of AI infrastructure and broader adoption of **agentic AI**. **Demand for server chips is expected to accelerate further, keeping the market undersupplied.**" Consistent with the intra-quarter flow that had been building the same case (@QQ_Timmy: server DRAM C3Q26 +30-40% q/q; Jefferies: a shortage with "no way out" for years). (AP via GS TMT Today, 2026-07-30)
- **⚠ The non-memory side is now paying for it — and it is the cross-read that matters:** an **operating loss in the mobile, TV and home-appliance division, "partly due to higher component costs."** Samsung is booking the cost side of its own memory upcycle inside its device business — the mirror image of [[QCOM]]'s "challenging memory and supply environment" hitting handsets and [[ARM]]'s FY27 royalty growth cut to high-teens on memory cost-induced smartphone demand destruction, both on 2026-07-29. The set-maker P&L is where the memory shortage is being absorbed. (AP via GS TMT Today, 2026-07-30)
- **Market reaction — record numbers are no longer a share-price event:** the stock **finished ~flat** on the print and traded **-1%** in the Asia session, with [[SKHYNIX]] **-4%**, [[KIOXIA]] +3%, [[TSM]] +20bps and the KOSPI **-1.23%**. Rothschild's technicals read frames why: the Korean leveraged unwind may be complete (**the 3x leveraged Hynix ETF is down 97% — "no more room to drop"**) but "the market trend of 'Long AI' is MUCH bigger than Korean leveraged ETFs" — **SOXL took +$4.4bn of inflows in July, on track for the largest monthly inflow ever** (AUM ~$10bn → ~$33bn → $17bn now), SMH still carries **$65bn AUM**, and CTA/systematic flows are now sellers "in almost any tape." Same morning: **UBS initiated [[SKHYNIX]] at Buy**, and JPM flagged "**finally, a case for Memory on P/B**" with "Korea positioning: unwind largely done." (GS TMT Today, 2026-07-30; Rothschild & Co Redburn · Ben Denney via Rowan Adley, 2026-07-30; JPM · Matthew See, 2026-07-30)

**Q1-2026 (reported 2026-04-30) — record quarter on AI memory.** Revenue **KRW 134tn** (+43% q/q); operating profit **KRW 57tn** (+185% q/q); operating margin **43%** (from 21%); net profit KRW 47tn; EPS KRW 7,123 (Q1-2026 call). The OP ramp over four quarters frames the inflection: **KRW 4.7tn (Q2-25) → 12.2tn (Q3-25) → 20.1tn (Q4-25) → 57tn (Q1-26)** (transcripts).
- **DS KRW 81.7tn (~$55bn), +225% y/y**; memory KRW 74.8tn, +292% y/y (Q1-2026 call).
- **DRAM:** bit growth low-teens % q/q, ASP up "low 90% range" q/q. **NAND:** bit +low-20s %, ASP +high-80s % q/q (Q1-2026 call). Note: conventional DRAM currently *more* profitable than HBM (Q1-2026 call; corroborated wccftech 2026-04-30) — the cycle is being led by commodity DRAM, not HBM, at Samsung.
- **NAND:** Samsung is the **#1 NAND maker, ~33% revenue share (2Q25e)**, with structurally higher OPM than peers on scale and a lower QLC mix (eSSD-skewed); MS has it as a **Top Pick (OW)** beneficiary of the AI-NAND upcycle, alongside KIOXIA/SanDisk (MS AI NAND overview). Industry NAND supply seen growing only **15-20% y/y in 2026** as Samsung/SK/Micron divert capex toward HBM and smaller players are cash-constrained — supportive of pricing (MS AI NAND overview; Phison 2Q25).
- **HBM:** first to mass-produce/ship **HBM4 in February 2026**; "demand for HBM4 fully booked"; HBM4 >50% of HBM sales from Q3; HBM sales **>3x y/y in 2026**; HBM4E samples in Q2 (16 Gbps, 4.0 TB/s) (Q1-2026 call; Q4-2025 call reiterated "HBM triples in 2026"). Supply-chain checks size Samsung HBM share at **~20% in 2025, set to rise in 2026**, with **1c-node HBM4 test yields ~50-60%** (ramp framed "on track") and a claimed **11 Gbps** HBM4 (some bins higher); Samsung is taking the riskier swing of jumping straight to **1c DRAM** for HBM4 vs SK Hynix/Micron staying on 1b (Q425 Techknowledge takeaways; SemiAnalysis HBM). Mix to ASICs is a relative strength — SK Hynix is capacity-capped on M15x and steering non-NVIDIA HBM demand (Google/AMD ASICs) toward Samsung, with NVIDIA also "encouraging Samsung to ramp" given conventional-DRAM tightness (Q425 Techknowledge takeaways).
- **Foundry:** double-digit y/y revenue growth, earnings down q/q on seasonality; **Taylor TX** equipment move-in Apr 23, ops 2026 / mass production 2027; 1.4nm on track; 2nm GAA ramping (first-gen new products from Q4-25, Q3-2025 call). Foundry capability is also a structural HBM differentiator: only Samsung can in-house its own advanced-logic **HBM4 custom base die**, whereas SK Hynix/Micron must outsource to TSMC — though customers are reluctant to tape out Samsung-compatible base dies given roadmap-delivery doubts (SemiAnalysis HBM). **Backside power (BSPDN):** Samsung targets adoption in **2027** (Intel end-2025, TSMC late-26/early-27) via Direct Backside Contact (vs Intel's simpler Power Via); Bernstein models BSPDN reaching ~45% of Intel/TSMC/Samsung volume by 2030 (Bernstein Semis deck, May-2025).
- **MX:** revenue KRW 37.5tn; MX+Network OP KRW 2.8tn; Q2 profitability decline "inevitable" on memory cost. **SDC:** mobile weak on seasonality + memory cost; premium/QD-OLED strategy.
- **Capex/return:** Q1 capex KRW 11.2tn (DS 10.2tn); 2026 "substantial y/y increase." Quarterly dividend KRW 372/sh; KRW 9.8tn annual minimum payout (2024–26); share cancellation ~KRW 14.6tn (Q1-2026 call).
- **DRAM wafer capacity (the structural #1):** Samsung exits 2026 with the largest DRAM wafer cap at **~740K wafers/month** (vs SK Hynix ~610K, Micron ~365K), adding only ~45K in 2027 (clean-room space, not equipment/demand, is the binding constraint through end-2028); P5L phase-1 capacity **won't be ready until mid-2028**, and P5L phase-2 construction was **just announced (within the prior week) with earliest output ~2030** — so Samsung's 2027 supply growth is "very limited." Korea contractors carry longer build lead times than SK Hynix (paid less). At end-2026 ~180K of the 740K is HBM wafer cap (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23).
- **NAND→DRAM conversion / losing NAND share:** Samsung (and SK Hynix) are converting NAND wafer capacity to the more-lucrative DRAM, driving global NAND wafer cap **−3% in 2026**; Samsung is **losing NAND market share** as it prioritizes DRAM/HBM (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23).
- **HBM4 catch-up — possible #1 in 2027:** Samsung is "doing a better job" on HBM4; the analyst flags a **real possibility Samsung overtakes SK Hynix as the #1 HBM supplier in 2027** (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23) — corroborated by Conor, who notes the HBM4 price was still not finalized two weeks ago "despite Samsung already shipping," expected +90-100% vs last year's LTA (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **First-ever hybrid-bonding disclosure:** at the meeting Samsung disclosed **for the first time that it has a hybrid-bonding project** and is "willing to offer it at a premium" (i.e. for higher HBM stacks / HBM4E) — read bullishly by Besi holders (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **Anthropic angle — now a foundry lead, not just a memory customer (The Information, 2026-07-02):** [Anthropic](ANTHROPIC.md) has begun early-stage work on its **own AI chip** and held talks with **Samsung as a potential manufacturing (foundry) partner** — the first explicit confirmation of the in-house-ASIC that was previously only *inferred* from the HBM-2028 memory deals. Read-through for Samsung: a prospective advanced-logic foundry win from a top-3 AI lab, layering onto the Google TPU V10ax / AMD / Tesla availability-driven engagement narrative — and a customer that would also take Samsung HBM. Prior (still valid): Anthropic pursuing an HBM (2028) deal with Samsung as well as SK Hynix — same structure as its Micron deal (server DRAM + eSSD 2027 / HBM 2028) (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23; The Information, 2026-07-02).
- **ECTC 2026 — Samsung the most-cited memory maker on HBM4E packaging (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02):** at ECTC (Samsung had **11 papers**, the second-most after Intel's 12, vs TSMC's 3) Samsung presented two HBM4E-packaging solutions that bear directly on the high-stack roadmap the page already tracks: (1) an **8-layer silicon interposer for HBM4E at up to 12 Gb/s** that cuts the layer count ~20% vs the estimated requirement (HBM4E otherwise needs ~2x the interposer layers of HBM3E and ~5x HBM2; power +86% vs HBM3E / +5.6x vs HBM2), using ultra-high-density capacitors and a balanced two-signal/one-ground routing scheme; and (2) **hybrid-copper-bonding (HCB) vs thermal-compression-bonding (TCB) thermal validation** for 2.5D GPU packages — HCB cuts **stack-level thermal resistance ~19%** (rising to ~29% at 4x pad density), enabling ~1-2°C higher inlet temp or ~4% more package power at constant temperature — quantifying the payoff of the hybrid-bonding project Samsung first disclosed at the Redburn Asia meeting (06-23). Read-through: concrete evidence Samsung is investing hard in the HBM4E/20-24-hi-stack packaging problem where taller stacks and higher base-die power (esp. for custom HBM) shift the thermal bottleneck (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_2_july.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_2_july.html)
- **BofA Global Memory weekly — 2Q OP slight miss + Meta/CXMT/Korea de-risking (BofA · Simon Woo/Dai Shen/Vivek Arya, 2026-07-02):** three takeaways bearing on Samsung: (1) **Samsung's 2Q OP likely to miss slightly** due to a **special employee bonus** (a one-off cost, not a demand signal) — echoes the JPM union/bonus provision flag; (2) **Meta will NOT cut memory orders** — chipmakers/supply-chain contacts (NAND controller, substrate) say Meta's long-term memory/component orders are getting *stronger* (more HBM, LPDDR5, eSSD for its AI DCs), and the "Meta Compute = excess capacity" Street fear is "groundless" — a direct rebuttal of the [META](META.md) memory-demand bear that drove the 07-02 memory selloff; (3) **CXMT's DRAM shipment to Apple likely low** on quality/patent issues, and **Korea's new memory cluster won't have a major impact** — de-risking two China/oversupply overhangs (BofA Global Memory Tech, 2026-07-02).
- **Samsung Display 2H foldable-iPhone growth (Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-07-02):** JEF Asia morning flags **Samsung Display will see strong 2H26 growth on the foldable iPhone** — a positive read-through for the SDC panel segment tied to Apple's foldable launch (Jefferies Asia TMT, 2026-07-02).
- **Preliminary Q2 2026 results — ahead of consensus (reported Mon 2026-07-06, via Vital Knowledge):** **sales KRW 171T (vs Street KRW 169.2T)** and **operating income KRW 89.4T (vs Street KRW 84.2T)** — both ahead of consensus. Vital Knowledge cautions the knee-jerk reaction could be "modest disappointment" since the beat isn't large for a stock sitting at the epicenter of the memory boom (Vital Knowledge, 2026-07-06). Reinforces the DRAM up-cycle read; full detail (segment mix, HBM) awaits the late-July print. **07-07 tape confirmed the "hurdle" caution: stock fell ~5.8-10% intraday (closed −7%) despite the record print**, triggering a KOSPI circuit breaker (the 6th of 2026); OP +~1,810% YoY / +129% YoY revenue, and Samsung **overtook NVIDIA (and AAPL) as the world's most profitable company** — the sell-off read as "the market's hurdle for the memory names has risen dramatically" (@ParadisLabs / @aleabitoreddit / Mohamed El-Erian @elerianm, 2026-07-07). A record quarter met with a −7% reaction is itself the signal — expectations, not results, are now the binding constraint on the memory longs.
- **Broker read on the prelim print — the beat is understated by a big one-off provision (2026-07-07):** the headline **W89.4trn OP was struck after recognizing ~W15+trn of labor-cost provisions covering the two quarters of 1H26** — so **underlying 2Q OP is ~W110-120trn (~$78bn) ex-provisions**, with the chips (DS) division alone ~W90trn (memory ~W91-93trn / logic-foundry ~−W2 to −W3trn) — "actually better than most recent sell-side reports" (JPM · Jay Kwon; BofA · Simon Woo; JEF/Shawn Park local color, all 2026-07-07). **JPM (Jay Kwon) reiterates OW, PT W480,000** — SEC trades at **5.2x FTM P/E, the cheapest memory stock globally**; NAND blended ASP **+>70% Q/Q** (above JPMe +53%), DRAM below JPMe's high-50s% on mix; **4nm HBM4 base-die entered full-scale mass production**. **BofA (Simon Woo) — "no need to worry about downturn," W400-500trn annual OP sustainable into 2027-28**; 2Q memory OPM ~80% DRAM / ~65% NAND (even after the bonus provision); models 3Q OP **>W100trn for the first time** and a 2028 "soft-landing" (ASP −10-12% YoY → sub-5% EPS decline; 2028 EPS still 26% above 2026). Positioning, not fundamentals, drove the sell-off: **offshore institutional ownership only ~46%**, but elevated retail-ETF/leverage/vol-trader positioning + SEC's 10-day realized vol forcing VAR-driven position cuts (JPM · Duncan Wagner, 2026-07-07). Full segment/HBM detail at the **July 30** call.
- **Foundry backlog ₩50tn ($32.7B) + Meta 2nm MTIA / Anthropic interest (media via @dnystedt, 2026-07-07):** Samsung Foundry's order backlog has reportedly risen to **₩50 trillion (~US$32.7B)** after landing Tesla last year and, more recently, **interest from Meta and Anthropic** — Meta said to be seeking Samsung's **2nm process for its MTIA AI chips** (previously TSMC-only). Firms up the availability-driven foundry-optionality narrative (Google TPU V10ax / AMD / Tesla) with a concrete backlog figure and two new prospective AI-lab/hyperscaler logic wins. Read-through to [custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md) and [META](META.md)/[ANTHROPIC](ANTHROPIC.md).
- **CXL 3.1 mass production delayed (@jukan05, 2026-07-07):** Samsung delayed mass production of its **CXL 3.1-based memory module (CMM-D 3.0)**, following the postponed launches of CXL 3.1-supporting CPUs from **Intel and AMD** — the CXL-memory-fabric ramp is gated by the host-CPU roadmap, not Samsung. A modest push-out of the CXL optionality (not the DRAM/HBM core). Read-through to [INTC](INTC.md)/[AMD](AMD.md) and [cxl-memory-fabric](themes/cxl-memory-fabric.md).
- **CMM-D validated as a KV-cache tier — first-party benchmark (Samsung white paper "Optimizing KV Cache Offloading to CMM-D in a CXL Switch-based Memory Pool," June 2026):** on an NVIDIA Blackwell system (8× RTX PRO 6000) with a **CXL 2.0 switch-based memory pool** running vLLM + LMCache, Samsung's **CMM-D** used as the LMCache backend achieves **DRAM-comparable performance at single-GPU (TP=1) and ~92% of DRAM at 8-GPU (TP=8)**, sustaining a high cache-hit rate (avoiding KV recompute) across cache sizes — after host-level optimizations (custom interleave kernel, LMCache NUMA-interleave, CXL memory pinning). Concrete performance evidence under the CMM-D/CXL optionality the page tracks (a data point on the product's viability, not the DRAM/HBM core). Read-through to [cxl-memory-fabric](themes/cxl-memory-fabric.md).
- **HBM4/HBM4E primer — Samsung's foundry-integrated angle (Atlas Peak Research "HBM4 and HBM4E: The Memory Wall Becomes the System Architecture Problem," 2026-04-03):** JEDEC finalized HBM4 in April 2025 (2048-bit / 32-channel / 2.048 TB/s per stack at 8 Gb/s — ~2x HBM3E; HBM4E → 16 Gb/s / ~4.096 TB/s ~2027). Samsung-specific: **began commercial HBM4 shipments in early 2026, confirmed mass-production status in March 2026 (GTC)**, on a **4 nm logic base die** built in-house (its structural differentiator — SK hynix/Micron must outsource the base die to TSMC); **customers include NVIDIA Vera Rubin and AMD MI455X**; **custom-HBM samples targeted to begin 2027**. The next competitive frontier is exposing the base die as a semi-custom integration layer for hyperscalers/ASIC vendors — where Samsung's in-house advanced-logic capability is an edge, offset by customers' reluctance to tape out Samsung-compatible base dies given execution doubts (Atlas Peak, 2026-04-03).
- **The HBM4 pin-speed bake-off — a two-tier HBM4 market, and the clearest statement yet that the Rubin HBM4 rework is done (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** AMD's MI455X ships **12 stacks of HBM4 = 432GB per package** at **23.3 TB/s** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks = 288GB**, Rubin at **22 TB/s** — a near-tie on bandwidth *despite AMD running a 50% wider bus*, because AMD clocks its HBM4 at **7.6 Gbps while Nvidia targets 10.7 Gbps, 40% faster**. SemiAnalysis is explicit this was deliberate and supplier-facing: "**Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification. This change was precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X.**" The cost fell on the memory makers — the change "**was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin but as of today these issues have been finally resolved**" — and the standing implication is that **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD** for the same generation. AMD and Nvidia are the only two shipping HBM4 this year. **Read-through for Samsung, specifically:** the page's central open question is HBM4 *price and yield* (the qualification question having resolved — HBM4 in mass production since Feb, shipping to both **NVIDIA Vera Rubin and AMD MI455X** per Atlas Peak above). This note reframes that question as a **binning** question: HBM4 is now effectively **two markets**, an easier **7.6 Gbps AMD socket** and a premium **10.7 Gbps Nvidia socket**. Two consequences worth holding: (1) falling short of Nvidia's spec locks a supplier out of the *premium bin*, not out of HBM4 — which is a materially softer bear case than the HBM3E-era "fails qualification, discounts unqualified inventory" episode this page carries, and gives the **already-confirmed AMD MI455X socket** more strategic weight than a second-customer datapoint would normally carry; (2) it raises the bar on the page's live 1c-yield debate, since an above-JEDEC target means tighter binning and lower effective yield on the top bin. **SemiAnalysis does not say which supplier wins the 10.7 Gbps bin — no such claim is made here**, and the page's existing bear datapoints (1c test-wafer yields "still extremely low"; HBM4 ES pin speed ~10 Gbps but with higher jitter and weaker package execution than SK hynix's, per "Memory Mania" 02-06) remain the counterweight. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **Price up, content down — the 2027 HBM-pricing call and the offsetting de-spec, from the buyer's own BOM (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** The bull leg: SemiAnalysis explicitly expects "**a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM**" — i.e. commodity-DRAM margin is the *anchor* HBM is being repriced toward, which is exactly the inversion this page already carries from the other direction (Q1-2026 call: conventional DRAM currently **more** profitable than HBM; SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania": it "turned bullish" on Samsung *for* its commodity-DRAM exposure precisely because Samsung's HBM margin is the lower one). If HBM reprices toward commodity-DRAM margins, the specific bull leg that made Samsung's HBM-laggard status *less* costly narrows — worth watching as a two-sided read rather than a clean positive. It is modelled as strong enough to **compress the accelerator vendors themselves**: AMD's ASPs must rise but it cannot pass the full memory increase through, because "most of AMD's volume goes to large buyers with real negotiating leverage, and those buyers have more alternatives than they used to," so HBM cost passes through "**at a lower incremental margin than their corporate margins, dragging down gross margins on the MI455X package.**" Baseline: "**HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025**" (SemiAnalysis Memory Model / Accelerator and HBM Model). **The bear leg is in the same note.** Tight supply is already destroying attach: the original 2025 MI455X BOM estimate carried "a staggering **138TB in DRAM across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM**"; the current estimate has **LPDDR5X entirely removed** plus a **significant DDR5 de-spec to a far lower DDR5 RDIMM content** — the roadmapped **up to 1TB of direct-attached LPDDR per MI455X accelerator on the EAM module has disappeared**, attributed directly to tight memory supply. SemiAnalysis's summary: "**much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs.**" Net for Samsung — **price-led revenue with content as a live offset**, and given Samsung is the largest DRAM/LPDDR supplier by wafer capacity, the LPDDR line item is the one to watch. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **Kirin 9030 teardown — Samsung 1a LPDDR5X inside Huawei's flagship, but dual-sourced against CXMT in the 16GB SKU (SemiAnalysis STEEL teardown, "Is SMIC N3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14).** SemiAnalysis's own teardown lab found the **Huawei Kirin 9030 Pro carries 12GB of Samsung DRAM in two stacks of four dies, identified as the K4L2E165YD — a 12Gb LPDDR5X-9600 device on Samsung's 1a node**, the fourth generation of its 10nm-class DRAM after 1x/1y/1z, in volume since 2022, "**so this is current memory rather than older-node inventory**" — i.e. a current-node Samsung part designed into a flagship Chinese handset, not a legacy-inventory clear-out. **The competitive tell is in the 16GB Pro Max variants, which were found with both CXMT and Samsung packages.** The CXMT part is marked **CXDD7JEDM**, two stacks of four dies, **packaged in week 45 of 2025**, with X-ray CT-inferred die dimensions consistent with a known density of **~0.3 Gib/mm² for the CXMT G4 process, roughly equivalent to other manufacturers' 1z**. That is **dual-sourcing inside a flagship Chinese handset, with CXMT one node-generation behind Samsung on density** — the most concrete *measured* datapoint on this page for the China-DRAM-substitution debate, which has otherwise been argued from capacity forecasts and BofA/WF share estimates. It cuts both ways and should be read that way: CXMT is genuinely designed in at the flagship tier (not just low-end consumer, as the roadshow/BofA framing has it), but it is doing so a generation behind on density and only as a *second* source alongside Samsung. Separately from the same report, Samsung appears as a **foundry density reference point** — a theoretical **SMIC N+4** (198nm cell height, 54nm CGP → 137.8 MTr/mm²) would land **on par with Samsung SF4** — one bounded read on where domestic Chinese logic sits versus Samsung's own node ladder. (SemiAnalysis "Is SMIC N3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **MS reaffirms Samsung as its DRAM Top Pick; DRAM > NAND (Morgan Stanley "NAND Industry Outlook", 2026-07-02):** in the Global Perspectives, MS (Shawn Kim) keeps **OW on Samsung Electronics as the Asia Tech team's Top Pick in DRAM** "for its market leadership and potentially stronger shareholder returns," modeling **>20-30% DRAM price hikes in 3Q26** (enough to keep the YoY rate of change accelerating), with pricing power translating into earnings revisions that support P/E stability at **5.2x 2027e earnings**. MS is mindful of the rate of change: **YoY DRAM pricing likely plateaus in 4Q26** vs supply-discipline visibility only clearing in 2028, so the stocks "might lack near-term cyclical catalysts until 2028+" — though multiples could keep re-rating on favorable LTAs. Tactically MS **prefers DRAM over NAND** (better LTA terms, higher demand visibility, EUV-capped supply discipline, and a potential **HBM4E capacity squeeze**). MS order-of-preference table PT: **KRW381,000** (bull KRW430,000 / bear KRW190,000) (Morgan Stanley "NAND Industry Outlook", 2026-07-02). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nand_-_MS.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nand_-_MS.html)
- **Korea "Three Mega Investment Projects" — MS economist framing (Morgan Stanley "South Korea: A Landmark Industrial Strategy", 2026-07-01):** MS Chief Korea/Taiwan economist Kathleen Oh details the June-29 plan (a distinct read from the JPM framing above) — **"Three Mega Investment Projects" of ≥KRW2,000tn (~US$1.5tn)** across (1) semiconductor clusters, (2) AI data centers, (3) physical AI & robotics, led by Samsung and SK Group, who together announced **an additional KRW1,500tn on top of already-committed plans over the next 10-15 years**. For Samsung specifically: it **extended its pre-existing chip-production investment plan both geographically and in product base (HBM)** as part of a broader **KRW2,450tn** investment plan — including a humanoid-robot line in Gumi, **new HBM fabs in Cheonan/Onyang**, KRW625tn to Honam-region AI-chip/robot/battery/IT projects, and a digital-twin appliance hub in Gwangju. Plan target: **Korean memory capacity to double by 2031**; execution the key risk (2027 budget draft end-July is the first checkpoint) (Morgan Stanley "South Korea: A Landmark Industrial Strategy", 2026-07-01). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260701_Morgan_Stanley_035420-KS_South_Korea-_A_Landmark_Industrial_Strategy_Unveiled_.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260701_Morgan_Stanley_035420-KS_South_Korea-_A_Landmark_Industrial_Strategy_Unveiled_.html) _(Reconciliation note: this KRW2,000tn/$1.5tn "Three Mega Projects" figure is the government-plan headline; not to be conflated with the JPM ~$3.1tn combined-through-2040 corporate-capex figure or the DIGITIMES KRW2,655tn Samsung-domestic-only number already on the page — different scopes, all logged.)
- **Wafer-shortage acknowledgement:** met in Korea on the trip, Samsung said the buy-side were "the first to ask about a wafer shortage" next year and that if one materializes it would **optimize product mix — pushing conventional DRAM at the expense of HBM** (TSV backend penalty), further limiting HBM capacity (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **Management color at Susquehanna Asia tour (2026-06-12 / SIG note 2026-06-18):** Samsung told SIG its **foundry division has moved beyond the difficult last two years** and that **advanced-node yields have "improved significantly" and reached "stable levels"** — management's own rebuttal to the yield-lag bear (contrast the ex-TSMC exec's "volume/pipe-cleaner" framing); most advanced process is 2nm, with **Tesla's 2nm adoption a "major validation point"** attracting more customers. On HBM: **HBM4E ramps 2027** and is seen a *larger* opportunity than HBM4 (adoption extends beyond GPU vendors to ASIC/custom silicon), with **HBM4E data rates up to 16 Tbps**; HBM4 share expected "materially higher" than its HBM3E share; **server memory now >50% of Samsung's total memory revenue**. Also disclosed it has **secured a meaningful silicon-photonics customer** (intends to expand), and partners with **Amkor** while building its own 2.5D packaging; on storage it is favoring **TLC eSSD** for AI KV-cache offload (QLC still under evaluation) (SIG "Asia tour", 2026-06-18).
- **Hyperscaler affordability ceiling (demand-durability frame):** at the Jefferies Asia Forum, Samsung itself put the memory bill hyperscalers "can bear" at **30-35% of their total operating cash flow** — vs ~**20% of combined hyperscaler OCF in Q1** (DRAM+NAND); a ~100% ASP move would already put the industry at that 30-35% ceiling, the key check on whether 2H ASP gains can match 1H (Jefferies/Conor equity call, 2026-04-07). This is the demand-side bear that pairs with the supply-side bull (StoneX wafer-cap constraint).
- **EUV is the hard cap on 2028 DRAM supply:** ASML's ~**90 EUV tools/yr by end-2027 is insufficient** — Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron each need to add ≥**100K DRAM wafers/month from 2028**, requiring ~**20-25 tools each for Samsung/Hynix** (Micron 10-15) while TSMC already absorbs ~**40 tools**; the math leaves "no easy" industry capacity add, structurally tight (JPM "Memory" equity call, 2026-05-28) — corroborating the page's clean-room-constraint thesis with the tool-level governor.
- **New AI-CPU memory pull:** NVIDIA Vera standalone-CPU demand ~**500K (2026) → 3M (2027)**; JPM models AI-CPU + head-node attach lifting **total server DRAM demand by >15-20%** vs prior view (server is ~60-65% of total DRAM), with LPDDR5X SO-DIMM content draining mobile-DRAM supply — Samsung/Hynix said they could **fill their fabs without supplying any smartphone DRAM** (JPM "Memory" equity call, 2026-05-28).
- **HBM ASP for 2027 +~30% blended:** JPM models **~+30% blended HBM ASP into 2027** on Samsung/Hynix — ~**10% like-for-like + mix** (HBM4 bit mix skewed 2H/2027 as the ramp slipped) — vs only a high-teens like-for-like on conventional DDR5; HBM4 price negotiation seen starting **July** post-Q2 (JPM "Memory" equity call, 2026-05-28).
- **Losing HBM share at Google (near-term):** Samsung's HBM capacity is growing **slower than Google's own TPU demand**, so its share at Google falls to **~low-20s%** this year→next; the two raised capacity-expansion plans should let it **re-match TPU consumption by 2028** (JPM "Memory" equity call, 2026-05-28).
- **Foundry gap quantified (ex-TSMC sales exec):** Samsung is **~1 year behind TSMC** in logic (vs Intel ~1.5-2yr); TSMC ~**70% foundry share**, Samsung "a little higher than Intel's <10%." The yield gap is a **volume/pipe-cleaner problem** — too little wafer throughput to mature yields — and **over half of Samsung's semiconductor capex is memory, not foundry**; the customer-conflict overhang (Samsung competes with its own foundry customers) remains a structural deterrent (ex-TSMC exec equity call, 2026-04-14).
- **Cost-structure edge = consolidated Korea footprint:** the ex-Micron VP attributes Samsung's superior memory cost structure to its **almost-all-Korea manufacturing footprint** (fewer sites/management layers/shipping) vs Micron's globally dispersed fabs; he is skeptical the **HBM4E customer-supplied custom logic base die** "really takes off" given warranty/liability/yield issues — a caution on the de-commoditization optionality the bulls lean on (ex-Micron VP equity call, 2026-06-10).
- **1c DRAM node yield/output detail; P4 fab phasing; industry-wide 1c adoption; HBM3E pricing power (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06):** Samsung's **1c DRAM node delivers ~70% higher bit output per wafer vs the 1a node** — the underlying process win behind its 2026 capacity ramp. On the P4 fab: **Phase 1 and Phase 3 drive most of the 2026 incremental capacity**, Phase 4 likely comes online later in 2026, and **Phase 2 could pull forward from 1Q27 but material output still lands in 1H27**. By **end-2026, Samsung + SK hynix combined are expected near 30% of DRAM wafer production on the 1c node** — i.e. the industry-wide leading-edge transition is still only partial. On HBM pricing, Samsung **priced HBM3E below both SK hynix and Micron in 2025**; SemiAnalysis sees a **10-15% price increase achievable in 2026** as HBM3E 12-hi performance/shipments improve. SemiAnalysis "turned bullish" on Samsung specifically (Nov note) because it has the **greatest exposure to commodity DRAM among the three** — and **commodity DRAM margins are now on par with or exceeding HBM margins** since late 4Q25, "particularly the case for Samsung, which has lower margin in producing HBM." 2026 DRAM WFE capex modeled **+26%** (vs SK hynix +34%, Micron +20%) (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania: How a Once-in-Four-Decades Shortage Is Fueling a Memory Boom", 2026-02-06).
- **HBM4 front-end competitiveness on power efficiency (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06):** Samsung's HBM4 **front-end (1c) is competitive on performance/power efficiency**, at times running at **lower power than SK hynix's** part — though SK hynix retains the signal-integrity/jitter edge (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06).
- **Root cause of the HBM struggle, quantified; the MI300X episode (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11):** SemiAnalysis traces Samsung's HBM underperformance to a **poor front-end DRAM process plus packaging** — bad yields, an underperforming product that didn't meet Nvidia's requirements. Share-loss timeline: Samsung was healthy through **HBM2E**; shortfalls surfaced at **HBM3** (lost Hopper-era share to SK hynix), and it only ever qualified HBM3 for **Nvidia's H20**. The starkest episode: on **AMD's MI300X**, Samsung was the **sole HBM supplier** (the first mainstream 12-Hi HBM product) — which "proved to be a big mistake": Samsung's HBM was a **significant contributor to above-average GPU failure rates**, and the episode **damaged AMD's relationship with Microsoft**. On **HBM3E**, Samsung's part is built on the **1a node (designed for mobile)** vs. SK hynix/Micron's superior 1b — falling further behind on speed/thermal envelope; AMD **shifted significant HBM3E supply to Micron**, and hyperscaler ASIC customers are **moving to SK hynix where they can**. **Amazon's Trainium2e is the one socket where Samsung retains most of its HBM3E share** (Amazon more willing to trade performance for cost). On Nvidia qualification, Samsung has had **repeated failures to qualify HBM3E**, and is still attempting a 12-Hi HBM3E redesign for **Blackwell Ultra**; market chatter is more optimistic, but SemiAnalysis's own understanding is that **Samsung has not yet passed full qualification** — it is offering **steep discounts on its unqualified inventory stockpile**. On HBM4, Samsung is taking the **bigger risk of jumping to the 1c node** (vs SK hynix/Micron staying on 1b) to try to close the performance/yield gap; recent **test-wafer yields are "still extremely low,"** and even if the ramp gets back on track, **meaningful volume lands only "later into 2026"** — a pattern SemiAnalysis compares to Intel/Samsung Foundry's history of failed aggressive leapfrogging. The offsetting structural advantage: Samsung has **in-house advanced-logic/foundry capability** (unlike SK hynix/Micron), letting it build the HBM4 custom base die internally — though customers remain reluctant to tape out Samsung-compatible custom base dies given execution concerns, which raises the switching costs of multi-sourcing away from Samsung (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: The Rise and Roadmap of HBM", 2025-08-11, p.40/45-49).
- **Samsung developing an AI-PC accelerator, "GAIA" (@jukan05, 2026-07-09):** Samsung is developing **GAIA**, an AI accelerator for **AI PCs** on a **4nm process**; it has already supplied **prototypes to major customers including Lenovo (China) and HP (US) for performance validation**, with mass production potentially **as early as next year**, and is pushing to **integrate it with processing-in-memory (PIM)**. A net-new compute/product-optionality datapoint (unconfirmed by Samsung). (@jukan05, 2026-07-09)
- **Courting Big Tech foundry clients at Sun Valley (TrendForce, 2026-07-09):** Samsung reportedly courting **Big Tech foundry clients at the Sun Valley conference this week** — incremental color on the availability-driven foundry-optionality narrative. (TrendForce, 2026-07-09)
- **2nm foundry-share reality check — TSMC ~90-95%, Samsung + Intel only ~5-6% in the first two product waves (JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08).** A cap on the availability-driven foundry-optionality bull case: JPM's Gokul sees TSMC holding **~90-95% of 2nm share in the first two product waves (2028-29), with only ~5-6% going to Intel + Samsung combined "if they can execute"** — because TSMC's 2026/27 capex ramp largely closes the 28-29 capacity gap, and "adjusted, TSMC is still going to be cheaper than all these other foundries even though their sticker price is lower." Share loss to Samsung/Intel only materializes if 2028 demand runs 30-40% above TSMC's aggressive build plan. Goldman Sachs's TSMC preview corroborates the dominance backdrop — very strong N2/N3 demand (AI ASIC / Google TPU), 2027 capex revised up to ~$78bn, CoWoS to ~220k/mo by end-2027 — implying limited near-term 2nm leakage (Goldman Sachs "TSMC preview", 2026-07-09). Bear on the Samsung-foundry-share thesis (JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_08_gokul_preview_tsmc_2q26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_08_gokul_preview_tsmc_2q26.html) · [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_09_gs_on_tsmc.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_09_gs_on_tsmc.html)
- **US ADR exploration — Bloomberg report, official denial, then a reported "behind-the-scenes" review (@jukan05 / @business/Bloomberg, 2026-07-14 → @jukan05, 2026-07-15):** Bloomberg reported Samsung is **exploring a potential US ADR/share sale** — preliminary talks with banks, no decision yet, and it will "monitor the volatile memory chip stocks" as part of the call — following [SK Hynix](SKHYNIX.md)'s blockbuster Nasdaq ADR listing on 07-10 ($149/sh, ~$26.5bn raised, the largest-ever by a foreign company). Samsung **officially denied** it is considering a US listing, but Korean media (@jukan05, 07-15) reports it has **internally launched a review of ADR scenarios and procedures** — finance/IR teams assessing costs/benefits and even **requesting SK Hynix's listing playbook** (SK Hynix likewise distanced itself before formalizing). An ADR listing is one of the Jefferies-flagged catalysts (07-08); a potential liquidity/re-rating catalyst — though Jim Cramer framed it as yet more memory "supply" for the market to absorb (@jukan05 / @business, 2026-07-14; @jukan05, 2026-07-15).
- **New DRAM fab at Giheung, ~100K wafers/month (@jukan05 "Exclusive", 2026-07-14):** Samsung will build a new DRAM fab at its **Giheung** campus (Yongin), **converting the former "SR5" R&D-center site** (its historic 1987 R&D landmark, demolished late-2025/early-2026 — the R&D-tower plan reversed in favor of production) into memory output — **~100,000 wafers/month**, "tens of trillions of won," internal org already formed, initial construction possibly **as early as 3Q26**. Added capacity color: **P4 (Pyeongtaek) is installing a new 100K-wpm line capable of HBM4 DRAM**; the Yongin next-gen cluster targets first ops **2029**; two Gwangju fabs carry **KRW400tn**. Framed as demand-driven (Counterpoint cited: global DRAM revenue **$150bn 2026 → up to $210bn**) — a bull signal for the up-cycle but also incremental DRAM supply feeding the 2027-28 durability debate (@jukan05, 2026-07-14).
- **Samsung Foundry has reportedly secured Anthropic (Korean media via @jukan05, 2026-07-14):** an industry source close to Samsung says **"Samsung Electronics' foundry division has agreed to manufacture Anthropic's custom AI chips"** — upgrading the prior "in talks" framing (The Information, 07-02; reconfirmed 07-03) to a reported **agreed 2nm foundry win**. A follow-on report (@jukan05, 07-15) lists **Anthropic + DeepX** among newly secured 2nm customers alongside Google and Tesla, with TSMC's capacity-constrained overflow "flowing to Samsung." A marquee AI-lab foundry customer that would also take Samsung HBM. Read-through to [ANTHROPIC](ANTHROPIC.md). (Korean media via @jukan05, 2026-07-14/15)
- **Tesla A15 tape-out at Taylor, TX — 2nd US 2nm chip (@lithos_graphein / @trendforce, 2026-07-13):** Samsung's **Taylor (Texas) fab completed tape-out for Tesla's A15/AI5 chip** — the **second 2nm chip made in the US**, just behind Intel's 18A (Musk confirmed design completion in April). A concrete Taylor-fab 2nm proof point on the availability-driven foundry narrative. Read-through to [TSLA](TSLA.md). (@lithos_graphein / @trendforce, 2026-07-13)
- **Google 2nm TPU I/O die — Samsung to fab it, sounding out back-end-design partners (@jukan05, 2026-07-15):** Samsung is set to **manufacture the I/O die of Google's 10th-gen 2nm TPU (codenamed "Icefish," ~MP 2028) on its 2nm process** (while TSMC makes the compute die on 1.4nm); short on internal staff amid surging 2nm demand, it is **sounding out DSP partners (ADTechnology, Gaonchips, Alphachips) to outsource the back-end design** — the outsourcing is a workforce-bottleneck tell (orders exceeding internal capacity), not a loss. Concrete evidence of Samsung 2nm involvement in Google's TPU program. Read-through to [custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md) and [GOOG](GOOG.md). (@jukan05, 2026-07-15)
- **Two bullish broker reads into the correction (2026-07-13):** **Jefferies (Silk Road desk · Cristina Titu) upgraded Samsung to BUY (07-13)** and reiterated **Samsung & SK Hynix BUY (07-15)** — the same desk that flagged the 07-01 selloff now turning constructive (desk-level; the house Jefferies/Jeff Kim rating is already BUY, TP W600k). **BofA (Simon Woo, 07-13):** the **memory correction is overdone**; **DRAM ASP +50-60% QoQ**, and Korea's lower 2Q blended ASP (~low-mid 40%) is a **2Q-specific mix effect, not a 3Q trend** — supportive of Samsung memory earnings into the July 30 print (Jefferies · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-13/15; BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-07-13).
- **Into the Thursday (July 30) print — MS restates the prelim and, more usefully, spells out why the buyback everyone is waiting for may not arrive on this call (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28).** On the prelim: "**you had 85 trillion operating profit as a consensus. We came in at 89. But we have to factor in the provision from labor cost. They could have been close to 100 trillion if that was the case. Didn't matter. The stock sold off 7% that day.**" Two reconciliation notes against what this page already carries: (1) Sean's **~₩85tn consensus** sits slightly above the **₩84.2tn Street** figure logged from Vital Knowledge (07-06) — immaterial, but the actual prelim OP on file is **₩89.4tn**, so read Sean's "we came in at 89" as consistent with the print rather than as a separate MS estimate; (2) his **"close to 100tn ex-provision" implies a provision of roughly ₩11tn, materially smaller than the ~₩15+tn / ₩110-120tn underlying-OP figures the page carries from JPM (Jay Kwon) and BofA (Simon Woo) on 07-07** — a real dispersion in how large the 1H26 labour provision was, not a restatement, and worth resolving at the print. **The buyback mechanics are the net-new part, and they temper the expectation this page has been carrying** (BofA's desk looking for a "massive buyback + earlier dividends" at the call, logged 07-23/24): Samsung's **three-year shareholder-return policy — 50% of free cash flow — expires this year**, so "there should be some sort of a clarity on how much of that 50% free cash flow will be in the form of special dividends or buybacks." But **historically Samsung announces these with the Q4 results, "because they do need a board to actually look at the amount of free cash flow at year end before making that decision"** — i.e. the structural announcement window is October-to-January, not July. Two further constraints: **near term Samsung could be funding ~₩40tn of employee bonus which will be paid in stock, and "that one should not be canceled because it will be left for the employee bonus"** (so treasury shares earmarked there are not available for cancellation); and **"historically, the company tends to buy back stocks when the stock hits book value"** — a valuation trigger the stock is nowhere near after this year's move. Sean is on the bull side of the debate itself ("that's kind of the bull case, and I'm on that camp on buybacks that could potentially re-rate these stocks, especially when you have visibility on LTAs"), and notes investors "can basically buy the entire business in 35 years" at current valuations — but the timing mechanics argue the catalyst is a **year-end** event, not a July-30 one. He also confirms the print date: **"we do have earnings for Hynix tomorrow. And we have Samsung on Thursday"** (= 2026-07-30), which resolves the "7/29 print" reference in the BofA desk row below as belonging to [SKHYNIX](SKHYNIX.md), not Samsung. On the government capex announcements: "those things to me are **more something that is not quite important**. If you talk to companies, they're more focused on what's visible in the next year or so… those numbers will actually be based on where the overall demand lies" — a mild deflation of the ₩2,000tn/₩2,450tn/$3.1tn plan figures the page carries across three different scopes. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html)
- **P4 second-floor cleanroom named as a source of incremental industry DRAM bits (Morgan Stanley webcast · Sean, 2026-07-28).** Asked whether the mega-capex announcements matter, Sean's answer pivoted to what actually shows up in bits: TrendForce now has **industry DRAM bit growth tracking ~31% this year (up from a 25% type of growth) and ~24% next year**, and he attributes part of that to **"Samsung P4 second floor clean room"** plus [SK Hynix](SKHYNIX.md) running **HBM3E rather than HBM4** (less total HBM, therefore more commodity DRAM produced). Read against this page's standing supply framing this is a **nuance, not a reversal** — the StoneX/TrendForce cleanroom-constraint thesis (Samsung ~740K wpm exiting 2026, only ~45K added in 2027, P5L phase-1 not ready until mid-2028) is about *2027-28*; the P4 second-floor cleanroom is *this year's and next year's* incremental bits, and it is one of the concrete reasons the industry bit number moved up 6pts. On the NAND side the same dynamic runs through YMTC (third line, **50,000 wafers** next year), taking NAND bit growth from high-teens to **20-25% next year**. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html)

## Debate / thesis
- **Bull:** Three engines firing from 2H26 — memory + foundry + robotics (Jefferies/Jeff Kim, 2026-05-11: Q2-26 OP **KRW 100tn** vs his KRW 84tn; "cheapest it will be" at 4.7x '27 P/E). The DRAM shortage has "no way out in coming years"; foundry optionality is incremental upside as hyperscalers diversify from TSMC (@techfund1, briefing 2026-06-18). Memory complex tape-positive: Bernstein has long held Samsung as its **top pick in memory** on improving HBM position + a never-stretched valuation (never sustainably above 1.5x P/B), arguing the "lost-leadership" perception drove an abnormal P/B discount vs SK Hynix/Micron since mid-2023 (Bernstein Semis deck, May-2025). MS likewise carries Samsung **OW / Top Pick** across both NAND (AI-NAND upcycle) and AI semis (MS AI NAND overview; MS Asia AI Factories). Korea tape repeatedly rips on the name (Samsung +8.9% 2026-05-21; +4.8% overnight 2026-06-17 on Apple memory pass-through).
  - **SemiAnalysis turns bullish specifically on Samsung's commodity-DRAM exposure (2026-02-06):** the mechanism is the inverse of the usual HBM-purity framing — Samsung has the **greatest exposure to commodity DRAM among the three** memory makers, and SemiAnalysis "turned bullish" on Samsung in its November note precisely because **commodity DRAM margins are now on par with, or exceeding, HBM margins** since late 4Q25, "particularly the case for Samsung, which has lower margin in producing HBM" than SK hynix/Micron. Reinforcing detail: Samsung's **1c DRAM node delivers ~70% higher bit output per wafer** than 1a, and it **priced HBM3E below both SK hynix and Micron in 2025**, leaving room for a **10-15% HBM3E price increase in 2026** as 12-hi shipments/performance improve. On HBM4, Samsung's **1c front-end is competitive on power efficiency** (sometimes beating SK hynix), even though SK hynix keeps the signal-integrity/jitter edge (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania: How a Once-in-Four-Decades Shortage Is Fueling a Memory Boom", 2026-02-06).
  - **Jefferies' #1 pick — "not just memory" (Conor, 2026-04-07):** Jefferies made Samsung its top Asia-tech idea on a three-legged story: (1) **memory** (Jeff Kim's KBI note: Samsung earns more OP than Nvidia next year at ~1/5 the market cap); (2) **foundry turning profitable** — if Samsung takes ~30% of the HBM market next year that volume runs through a Samsung-tailored fab with its own base die, plus wins cited include a Sony wide-angle image sensor for Apple, Tesla, the Groq chip (~3M units) and active talks with Qualcomm and AMD — foundry has been valued at zero-to-negative EV, so profitability is a "potential valuation kicker"; (3) **panels** — ~10M foldable iPhones this year = ~20M Samsung panels off Apple. Sentiment tailwind: **foreign ownership of Samsung sat at a 10-year low** as of the call (Jefferies "Asia tech", 2026-04-07). BofA's TSMC expert corroborated the foundry-leverage angle: Samsung is "leveraging its memory business" (HBM4 leadership) to win foundry designs, e.g. pressing AMD to fab on Samsung in exchange for HBM4 supply (BofA "Best ideas", 2026-04-09).
- **Bear:** Samsung is the **HBM laggard** — HBM4 qualified but volume not ramped, product still trails SK Hynix on power/speed; SK Hynix is the relative-performance long (JPM memory-China, 2026-05-28; [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md)). SemiAnalysis is the sharpest bear: Samsung's HBM struggles stem from poor front-end DRAM (1a node, mobile-derived) + packaging, yielding "even worse" than peers and an inferior product NVIDIA won't fully qualify; HBM3E share has bled to SK Hynix/Micron (AMD MI300X failures, ASIC sockets migrating, Amazon Trainium2e the main retained socket); and **HBM4 1c test-wafer yields remain "extremely low"** with meaningful volume only later in 2026 — so the bullish 50-60% yield checks (Techknowledge) and SemiAnalysis "still very low" framing directly conflict (SemiAnalysis HBM vs Q425 Techknowledge takeaways). The ex-Micron VP frames HBM as commoditizing once 2nd/3rd vendors qualify, but notes **Samsung's cost structure is actually better than Micron's** — relatively advantaged in a snap-back but still riding a cyclical top (2026-06-10). Foundry yields lag TSMC; wins are availability-driven, not leadership. Union strike risk: JPM flagged 7–12% downside to 2026 OP (briefing 2026-05-08).
  - **The MI300X episode, in full (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11):** the AMD MI300X detail behind the generic "AMD MI300X failures" reference above — Samsung was the **sole HBM supplier** on MI300X (the first mainstream 12-Hi HBM product), which SemiAnalysis calls "a big mistake": Samsung's HBM was a significant contributor to **above-average GPU failure rates** and the episode **damaged AMD's relationship with Microsoft**. SemiAnalysis's own read is that Samsung has **not yet passed full HBM3E qualification at Nvidia** despite more optimistic market chatter, and is offering steep discounts to move its unqualified inventory stockpile. Samsung's HBM4 1c leapfrog is explicitly compared to **Intel/Samsung Foundry's history of failed aggressive leapfrogging** (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: The Rise and Roadmap of HBM", 2025-08-11).
  - **The commercial cost of that episode, now quantified — three years and two full AMD generations (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** The page already carries the MI300X sole-supplier episode and the "damaged AMD's relationship with Microsoft" conclusion. What the Advancing AI note adds is the **concrete commercial consequence**, stated flatly: "**In 2023, Microsoft dropped AMD as a customer after MI300X due to unreliable Samsung 2023 HBM memory & poor software quality & skipped both MI325X and MI355X.**" Microsoft has **only now returned**, with an announced **MI455X Helios** commitment (SemiAnalysis believes mainly for OpenAI as the end customer on Azure). So the reputational damage from a single sole-source HBM decision cost the *accelerator vendor* its largest cloud customer across **two entire product generations** — the sharpest available illustration of why hyperscalers and accelerator vendors now dual-source HBM by design, and of the asymmetry Samsung is still working against in qualification decisions. Attribute precisely: SemiAnalysis puts the blame on **both** unreliable Samsung 2023 HBM **and** AMD's software quality — this is not a Samsung-only causation claim. Read constructively, the same note also shows Samsung supplying HBM4 into the MI455X generation (Atlas Peak, 2026-04-03, above), i.e. the AMD relationship survived; read bearishly, it is the clearest evidence on this page that HBM reliability failures carry multi-year commercial tails rather than one-quarter ones. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **Where the sell-side/buy-side stands:** Jefferies (Jeff Kim) raising estimates, Q2-26 OP KRW 100tn, 4.7x '27 P/E (2026-05-11). Bernstein OW (top memory pick, valuation-discount thesis), MS OW/Top Pick (NAND + AI semis); SemiAnalysis the contra on HBM execution. SK Hynix ~5.3x vs Samsung ~5.9x fwd P/E; FinTwit memory books underweight Samsung vs SK Hynix on HBM/yield edge (@paradislabs, 2026-05-07). Net: consensus likes the memory cycle, prefers SK Hynix for HBM purity, owns Samsung for the cheaper multiple + NAND scale + foundry/robotics optionality — the swing factor remains whether 1c HBM4 yields are genuinely on track (Techknowledge) or "still very low" (SemiAnalysis). **Arete (Nam Hyung Kim) Buy, PT W350,000 (2026-07-21)** — constructive on the P5 NAND greenfield (~70-80k wpm) within a still-tight NAND market, and flags Samsung's **12-Hi (48GB) HBM4E sampling** as the datapoint behind the Rubin Ultra 384GB scenario (see the 07-21 Intra-quarter row + [hbm-memory theme](themes/hbm-memory.md)).
  - **PT-raise wave through May–Jun 2026 (rating actions):** **UBS switched its top memory pick TO Samsung** (from SK Hynix), upgrading conviction with **PT W400k (from W275k), Buy, added to APAC Key Call List**, on the thesis that Samsung reaches **equal HBM share with SK Hynix by 2027 (~40% each)** and its scale lets it deploy capacity/secure multiple revised LTAs — arguing memory stocks (Samsung especially) do not price a "New Normal" of structurally higher through-cycle ROE (+2ppt) and FCF (+3ppt) (UBS "APAC Focus — Global I/O Memory Semis", 2026-05-20). **Barclays reiterated OW, PT to $4,250 (from $4,000)** on the GDR at an unchanged **2.5x P/book**, modeling the guided HBM tripling and much-stronger-than-expected DRAM pricing (2026E rev W667.6tn, EPS W38,566; but cutting Mobile units −12% in 2026) (Barclays "Samsung & SK hynix — Pricing Tailwinds Intact", 2026-05-03). **BofA reiterated Buy, lifting common-share PO to W520,000 (from W440,000)** — preferred PO W355k, GDR PO to $8,800 (from $7,400) — on **2027-28E EPS up 14%/17%** (7%/10% higher DRAM ASP) post its Korea conference; BofA sees the global memory market topping **US$1.0tn in 2027** (DRAM ~$0.8tn, NAND ~$0.4tn) for the first time, with chipmakers set to raise **HBM prices aggressively in 2027** now that conventional DRAM price has exceeded HBM (BofA "Global Memory Tech", 2026-06-22).
- **Chris Wood (Jefferies GREED & fear) adds +1ppt across three portfolios (2026-06-25).** Christopher Wood is increasing Samsung Electronics exposure by +1ppt in his global long-only, international long-only, and Asia ex-Japan portfolios (selling Alphabet and Alibaba to fund). Thesis: "The story that the DRAM industry has changed structurally, and that the companies should now be valued on a price to earnings basis rather than on a price to book basis, looks to GREED & fear an increasingly powerful argument. And by this measure these companies still look cheap." He frames Korean memory/DRAM as a "picks and shovels" play on the AI compute cycle, citing Jevons Paradox: as GLM-5.2 and cheaper Chinese models cut token costs, the resulting demand explosion drives DRAM prices higher. Bull signal (Jefferies / Christopher Wood "GREED & fear: The mother of all cycles", 2026-06-25). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Chris_Wood.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Chris_Wood.html)
  - **UBS makes Samsung a "Key Call" Buy, PT KRW 550k (UBS "Memory Semis Monthly", 2026-07-03):** in the July '26 monthly UBS (Nicolas Gaudois) carries **Samsung as a "Key Call" Buy with PT KRW 550,000** — a step up that supersedes the prior UBS-line PT of **KRW 400k (05-20, when UBS first switched its top memory pick to Samsung; moved to Changelog)**. UBS believes **Samsung is progressing in completing by 3Q more revised LTA agreements with another handful of large customers**, focused on **DDR5**, targeting **50-70% of volumes tied to LTAs**; Korean makers fix ~60-70% of volume at fixed pricing over 5-yr contracts (vs Micron's floor/ceiling structure), and UBS reiterates **"Meta not an issue"** for HBM demand. Industry backdrop: DRAM 3Q26 **+32% QoQ** / 4Q26 +18%; undersupplied to at least 2Q28; memory-industry revenue **$992bn 2026 / $1,763bn 2027**. Bull signal (UBS · Nicolas Gaudois, "Memory Semis Monthly", 2026-07-03). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_memory.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_memory.html)

- **🆕 ARCHIVE BACKFILL — **DAMNANG (Substack)** (independent Korean-semis Substack; also publishes as **Damnang2** and, on one earlier ingest, under the publication name **“The Optical Edge”**, launched 2026-03-15, ~9k subscribers, PAID). ⚠️ NOT A BROKER: no rating, no price target, no house model. These pieces are 2-5 MONTHS OLD and are recorded as HISTORICAL FRAMEWORK ONLY — none of them supersedes any current number, rating or thesis above.**
  - **The HBM4 architecture case FOR Samsung, from the ISSCC paper itself** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “How Samsung Overtakes SK hynix in HBM4 — An ISSCC 2026 Paper Review”, **2026-03-20**; review of Samsung's ISSCC 2026 paper *“A 36GB 3.3TB/s HBM4 DRAM with Per-Channel TSV RDQS Auto Calibration and Fully-Programmable MBIST”*). **The structural argument the page's “HBM laggard” bear has to answer: Samsung moved the BASE DIE to its own 4nm FinFET LOGIC process AND jumped the core die two DRAM generations (1a → 1c, skipping 1b) SIMULTANEOUSLY** — VDDQ down 32% (1.1V → 0.75V), **4× the TSVs of HBM3E** (16 → 32 channels, DQ TSVs per channel doubled), microbump pitch 96×110μm → 70×110μm, 12-Hi 36GB at **3.3TB/s per cube vs 1.3TB/s for HBM3E**, and a measured **13.0Gb/s per pin against a JEDEC HBM4 baseline of 6.4Gbps**. The system-level number that matters more than pin speed: **per-channel RDQS auto-calibration lifts max speed at tCCDR=2nCK from 7.8Gb/s to 9.4Gb/s (~+20%)** — tCCDR being the die-switching penalty AI workloads pay constantly. ➤ **The author's own strategic read, and the reason this is on the SAMSUNG page rather than a technology page: the logic base die “only makes sense if you have memory and foundry under the same roof — SK Hynix would need to source that logic process externally (e.g. from TSMC).” And his own open questions are the right ones and remain open five months later: Samsung Foundry 4nm YIELD, 1c stability in volume, and reproducibility of paper numbers in mass manufacturing.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-20_-_How_Samsung_Overtakes_SK_hynix_in_HBM4-_An_ISSCC_2026_Paper_Review.html)
  - **Hybrid bonding — Samsung is the AGGRESSOR, and the motive is explicit** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Hybrid Bonding, Fully Dissected”, **2026-03-28**; based on the author's interviews with engineers at Samsung, SK hynix and Micron). **“Samsung is the most aggressive”: it has signalled hybrid-bonding adoption starting at 16-layer HBM4E, and its equipment subsidiary SEMES has produced 16-layer HBM samples with hybrid bonding and confirmed they function** — the author's framing being that Samsung “hasn't been able to catch SK Hynix on TC NCF-based bonding [so] hybrid bonding is a RESET OF THE GAME BOARD.” Against that: **SK hynix is “the most conservative,” with 12-layer HBM4 already MP-ready on MR-MUF and 16-Hi HBM4 showcased; [[MU]] has disclosed no timeline and is “most likely to be last.”** Timing: **JEDEC relaxing HBM4 package thickness 720μm → 775μm pushed hybrid bonding out one generation; from HBM4E at 20 layers, TC bonding “deteriorates sharply.”** ⚠️ **The engineers' own caveat, carried: they target HBM4E for initial adoption but “given the cost challenges, it may realistically not happen at scale until HBM5.” This is a technology-roadmap signal and a sample demonstration — NOT a production commitment.** (Also, from the same author's “Why HBM Is So Hard to Make”, **2026-03-20**: the current split is **SK hynix on MR-MUF — best thermal, most production-proven — vs Samsung on NCF + thermocompression, better precision at fine pitch**.) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-29_-_Hybrid_Bonding_Fully_Dissected.html)
  - **CXMT — the argument that Samsung is the MOST exposed of the three, which is the mirror image of the SK-hynix conclusion this wiki already carries** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Is CXMT a Threat or an Illusion?”, **2026-04-11**). *“For Samsung, CXMT is the opponent it faces across the WIDEST FRONT… losing HBM ground to SK hynix from above, commodity ground to CXMT from below, and facing a 3D DRAM R&D race from the front.”* Because Samsung is No.1 in DRAM share but trails in HBM, it **“relies on commodity revenue to shore up the bottom line”**, so CXMT's DDR5/LPDDR5X volume growth in China hits Samsung's China-facing commodity revenue most directly. Supporting detail: **CXMT wafer output ~40kwpm in 2020 → ~720,000 wafers per QUARTER by end-2025 across three 12-inch fabs; G4 node feature size ~16nm per TechInsights (a ~20% cell-size reduction vs G3), which the Big 3 reached in 2018-19 — a five-to-six-year gap, achieved with DUV multi-patterning and NO EUV.** ➤ **The author's constructive half for Samsung: it is “moving the most aggressively among the Big 3” on 3D DRAM — the transition where he thinks the competitive rules actually change.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-04-11_-_Is_CXMT_a_Threat_or_an_Illusion-.html)
  - **Vera Rubin's SOCAMM cut, and why HBM4 was not what got cut** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Vera Rubin's DRAM Cut in Half: Is the Memory Cycle Really Over?”, **2026-06-08**). ⚠️ **ROUTING CORRECTION: the ingest router mislabelled this piece “SemiAnalysis” — it is DAMNANG, discussing a SemiAnalysis report.** The Samsung-relevant facts: **Samsung referenced HBM4 and SOCAMM2 productisation for the Vera Rubin generation in its 1Q26 results and at GTC 2026, and referenced PCIe Gen6 eSSD and KV-cache storage demand in the same results.** The framework: **GB300 NVL72 carried 20.7TB of HBM3e at 576TB/s; Vera Rubin NVL72 carries 20.7TB of HBM4 at 1,580TB/s — capacity FLAT, bandwidth ~2.7×.** What was cut was CPU-side SOCAMM (192GB → 96GB modules, ~54TB → ~28TB per rack, rack cost ~$7.6m → ~$6.8m per the SemiAnalysis figures he cites), and his structural point is that **SOCAMM is SOCKETED, so “the reduction of soldered memory is CUT demand; the reduction of socketed memory is closer to DEFERRED demand.”** ➤ A useful precedent for reading the 2026-08 HBM de-spec debate above: the same author distinguishes state that is CAPTIVE in HBM (hot KV, intermediate) from state that is ABSORBED into the DRAM family (LPDDR/SOCAMM) from state that LEAKS to storage. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-06-08_-_Vera_Rubins_DRAM_Cut_in_Half-_Is_the_Memory_Cycle_Really_Over-.html)
  - **Two smaller archive marks, logged for the record rather than the number.** (i) **“Samsung had a quarter where CONVENTIONAL DRAM PROFITABILITY EXCEEDED HBM”** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Will the Memory Stock Rally Keep Going?”, **2026-05-11**) — the earliest dated statement of the HBM-vs-commodity margin inversion that management itself confirmed on the 2026-08-25 IR call. (ii) **Rambus announced its HBM4E Controller IP (16 Gbps/pin, 4.1 TB/s per device) in March 2026 “developed in collaboration with SAMSUNG FOUNDRY”** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Is the Market Reading Rambus Right?”, **2026-03-24**) — a small but concrete Samsung Foundry AI-IP engagement predating the 2nm hyperscaler wins management described on 08-25. [Sources](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-05-12_-_Will_the_Memory_Stock_Rally_Keep_Going-.html) · [Rambus](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-24_-_Is_the_Market_Reading_Rambus_Right-.html)


## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕🔴🔴 THE RUBIN-GENERATION STACK-HEIGHT LADDER — A SPEC, NOT A FORECAST, AND THEREFORE SETTLEABLE.** As of 2026-09-06 the wiki carries **Rubin at 12-Hi HBM4** (Jefferies Asia TMT specialist SALES · Conor O'Mara, 09-06 — ⚠️ SALES DESK COMMENTARY, NOT RESEARCH, relaying TrendForce / ET News) **against UBS's own house view that NVIDIA already moved VR/R300 to HBM4 8-Hi** (UBS Korea Summit first read, 2026-08-25) — and **Rubin Ultra at 8-Hi HBM4, "around one-third fewer than 12-Hi in Rubin"** (Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan et al., 09-06; ⚠️ MS's own wording is "Rubin Ultra **CPU**", ambiguous, not corrected here). **WATCH, IN PRIORITY ORDER: (1) an NVIDIA primary platform spec or a memory-maker qualification disclosure fixing the Rubin and Rubin Ultra stack heights — it would also settle whether management's 08-25 "no de-spec at the high end" and the DS spokesperson's "it is being considered" were describing different generations; (2) the END-SEPTEMBER 2026 HBM contract-price round — the direct test of whether ASP HOLDS while stack height FALLS, which is the leg on which Samsung's claimed HBM4 SPEED leadership would actually pay under @jukan05's pin-speed thesis; (3) whether the HBM4 BASE-DIE unit count and HBM4e migration offset the step — and, uniquely for this company, whether any of that logic content lands at SAMSUNG FOUNDRY rather than at [[TSM]]; (4) whether Morgan Stanley restates the 48,618mn Gb 2027 AI HBM demand figure its 08-24 model built on a 12-Hi Rubin Ultra.** ⚠️ **Explicitly NOT asserted: @jukan05's UNCONFIRMED question (09-06) that **Feynman** was "also lowered to 8-High" — it contradicts Mizuho's 768GB/GPU HBM4e 16-Hi Feynman mark. Both stand.**

- **🆕🔴 JANUARY 2027 (4Q26 result) IS NOW THE HARD DATE FOR THE REST OF THE CAPITAL RETURN — the W60-80tn buyback/dividend split is explicitly deferred to that board meeting.** JPM: *"January is when Samsung has historically disclosed a new shareholder return program policy."* BofA adds the mechanism: *"the method of returning any unpaid FCF (after 3Q W30tn dividends) will be decided at the end-Jan 2027 board meeting."* (JPM · Kwon; BofA · Woo, both 2026-08-23)

- **🆕 2026-08-24 → 2026-11-21 — the 53.3mn-share PSU treasury purchase window is open.** A mechanical, non-discretionary bid in the market for three months; note it is **excluded** from the FCF payout base. (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23)
- **🆕 2026-08-27, 09:00 HKT — BofA hosts a Samsung C&T SMR / nuclear-power call.** Relevant here because **C&T is a key stakeholder of Samsung Electronics** and constructs SMR/nuclear plants — BofA is using it as a **data-centre demand check** (BofA · Woo, 2026-08-23). Same tour: Nanya Tech President (08-26), EO Technics (08-24), Samsung SDS (08-25), Wonik IPS (08-28).

- **🆕🔴 DATED — BOARD FINALISES THE REMAINING ~W60-80tr ALLOCATION: LATE OCTOBER 2026, WITH A SECOND GATE AT END-JANUARY 2027.** MS: **"remaining ~W60-80tr allocation to be finalized by the board in late October"**, and its own watch-list splits the event in two — **(1) end-October confirmation of the ~W30tr 3Q dividend; (2) the END-JANUARY decision on the remaining W60-80tr AND the dividend/buyback MIX; (3) the 2027+ capital-return framework**. Citi frames the same calendar from the company side: **details finalised at the board meeting scheduled for end-October 2026E, and "following the 3Q dividend, Samsung plans to disclose the remaining amount and specific execution method by end-January 2027E."** ➤ **This is the resolution date for the Citi-vs-MS mix fork in `## Current state` (dividend-heavy ~W60tr/~W40tr vs buyback/cancellation-heavy) — a binary with a known date and two different equity stories attached. MS expects "the market to shift focus to higher capital returns as the next catalyst."** (Morgan Stanley · Shawn Kim, 2026-08-21; Citi · Peter Lee, 2026-08-21.)
- **🆕 DATED — W15tr / 53.3mn-SHARE EMPLOYEE BUYBACK EXECUTES 24 AUGUST → 21 NOVEMBER 2026: a ~3-month mechanical bid on ~0.9-1.0% of shares out, but ⚠️ NOT the EPS-accretive kind.** The filing states the purpose as **employee share-based compensation and "does not specify an expected holding period"**; MS reads it as earmarking profits for employee performance bonuses and **"unlikely for immediate cancellation."** Size discrepancy to note: **MS puts 53.3mn shares at ~0.9% of shares outstanding, Citi at ~1%** (both on the 21-Aug close) — immaterial, same disclosure. ➤ **Track it as a flow/technical event, not as share-count shrink. This is exactly the mechanic in the 08-19 MS Sales point already on this page — that a portion of Samsung's buybacks funds employee compensation and is therefore NOT cancelled, making the programme less EPS-accretive than [[SKHYNIX]]'s. That call is now CONFIRMED by the filing.** (Morgan Stanley · Shawn Kim, 2026-08-21; Citi · Peter Lee, 2026-08-21.)
