- **Q2-2026 print — Thursday 2026-07-30** (date confirmed by MS's Sean on the 07-28 webcast: "we do have earnings for Hynix tomorrow. And we have Samsung on Thursday"): test of Jefferies' KRW 100tn OP; DRAM ASP trajectory (server DRAM C3Q26 +30-40% q/q per @QQ_Timmy, 2026-06-16); and the size of the 1H26 labour-cost provision, where the ex-provision underlying OP estimates on this page **disagree materially — Sean/MS "close to ₩100tn" vs JPM/BofA "~₩110-120tn"** (07-07).
- **Shareholder return — the structural window is year-end, not this print (Morgan Stanley webcast · Sean, 2026-07-28).** The **three-year shareholder-return policy (50% of FCF) expires this year**, so clarity on the split between special dividends and buybacks is genuinely due — but Samsung **historically announces with the Q4 results**, since the board needs year-end FCF visibility before deciding. Two additional constraints on the near-term buyback the desk consensus is looking for: **~₩40tn of employee bonus is to be paid in stock and "should not be canceled,"** and **historically Samsung buys back "when the stock hits book value"** — a trigger far below the current price. Watch the **October-to-January** window as the real catalyst date, and treat any July-30 announcement as an upside surprise rather than the base case.
- **Union strike** resolution / production impact (announced May 21–Jun 7 window; foundry/LSI unresolved per trendforce 2026-05-16).
- 1d (7th-gen) DRAM mass production, earliest 1H27 (@jukan05, briefing 2026-06-17).
- **US ADR listing** — Bloomberg reports preliminary bank talks (07-14); Samsung officially denied but reportedly launched an internal ADR-scenario review (@jukan05, 07-15), following SK Hynix's 07-10 Nasdaq ADR ($26.5bn). A potential liquidity/re-rating catalyst (and one of Jefferies' flagged catalysts, 07-08). Watch for board approval / SEC filing as the formalization signal (@jukan05 / @business, 2026-07-14/15).
- **🆕 Korea's "three mega investment projects" — the state-backed capacity leg, and the one component worth tracking is CHUNGCHEONG AS AN ADVANCED-PACKAGING HUB (MS · Salvatore / Blake / Byrd et al, "AI Sovereignty: Policy, Market, and Economic Implications", 2026-08-19).** MS describes the programme as the largest domestic investment effort undertaken by any Korean administration to date, and says it **includes semiconductor clusters from both Samsung Electronics and [[SKHYNIX]]**. Samsung's named components: **expanding chip clusters in Yongin and Gwangju; a production line in Gumi; an additional allocation to Honam-region projects focused on AI chips, robots, batteries and IT components; and investment in the Chungcheong region to develop it as a key hub for advanced semiconductor packaging.** ➤ **Why the Chungcheong item is the one to watch: this page's own 08-17 evidence says the HBM constraint is BACK-END stacking capacity and floor space, not wafer starts (the NRD K2 R&D→foundry repurposing to free room for 2nm HBM base dies). A state programme aimed at advanced packaging is pointed at exactly that bottleneck.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE KRW MAGNITUDES ARE NOT USABLE AS PUBLISHED AND ARE NOT RECORDED AS FACTS. The note's exhibit extracted as "approximately KRW 2,450 trillion by December 2040, including KRW 2,100 trillion in semiconductors" plus "an additional KRW 625 trillion" for Honam — roughly an order of magnitude above Samsung's actual capex run-rate and above publicly reported Yongin mega-cluster figures, and this note's two-column exhibits are demonstrably interleaved by the PDF text extractor elsewhere. Marked EXTRACTION-UNCERTAIN; verify against the original exhibit before any of it is used. What to check first: whether the figures belong to Samsung at all, and whether the unit is trillion or billion KRW.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USELECTION_20260819_1049.html)
- **New DRAM-capacity milestones** — Giheung SR5-site DRAM fab (~100K wpm, construction possibly 3Q26); P4 100K-wpm HBM4-DRAM line; Yongin cluster first ops 2029 (@jukan05, 07-14) — incremental 2027-28 supply to watch against the tightness thesis; plus **labor-union opposition** to the Honam mega-project (84% of surveyed members opposed, tabled as a 2027 CBA item — @zerohedge, 07-13) as an execution risk.

## Risks
- **HBM share loss to SK Hynix** — laggard on NVIDIA qualification/yield; HBM3E share already lost to SK Hynix/Micron (AMD MI300X reliability issues, ASIC sockets migrating; SemiAnalysis HBM; JPM 2026-05-28). 1c HBM4 a high-risk leapfrog with low test yields. Concrete case: Samsung was the sole HBM supplier on AMD's MI300X — a "big mistake" per SemiAnalysis, since Samsung's HBM contributed to above-average GPU failure rates and damaged AMD's relationship with Microsoft; SemiAnalysis's own channel read is Samsung has still **not passed full HBM3E qualification at Nvidia**, despite more optimistic market chatter, and is discounting unqualified inventory to move it (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11).
- **Commodity-DRAM margin reversal** — the bull case (SemiAnalysis "turned bullish" on Samsung's commodity-DRAM exposure since commodity margins now match/exceed HBM margins, esp. for Samsung given its lower HBM margin) is a double-edged sword: if commodity DRAM pricing rolls over first (it is the more cyclical, less differentiated product), Samsung's greater relative exposure to it becomes the bear case instead of the bull case (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06).
- **Memory commodity snap-back** — current cycle led by conventional DRAM ASP (+~90% q/q); new fab capacity (Samsung/SK/Micron) landing ~CY27-28 could break pricing (ex-Micron VP, 2026-06-10). Custom HBM4 base dies raise switching costs, disproportionately hurting roadmap-laggards like Samsung (SemiAnalysis HBM).
- **De-spec / content destruction in the accelerator platforms — distinct from consumer demand elasticity (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-21).** The platforms are cutting DRAM and HBM attach *because* memory is scarce and expensive, so **bits per accelerator** — which most models hold flat or growing — is the exposed variable rather than price. On AMD's MI455X the **up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier has been removed outright** and DDR5 de-spec'd to a far lower RDIMM content vs an original 2025 BOM of **138TB of DRAM per platform across LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM** — "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs" (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24). Sharpest for Samsung as the largest LPDDR supplier by wafer capacity: the deleted line item is **LPDDR**, and the page already carries the offsetting LPDDR5X SO-DIMM/SOCAMM demand pull from NVIDIA's Vera CPU — these now point in opposite directions and need to be netted, not stacked. In the custom parts it is a direct HBM-content cut: **Meta's custom MI450/MI455 halves the compute silicon (8 dies → 4), cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 per package, and downgrades the HBM4 from 12-Hi to 8-Hi**, on a program SemiAnalysis argues "is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin" — doubly negative (fewer stacks on a program that may itself shrink) (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21; restated 2026-07-24). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
- **China DRAM substitution reaching the flagship-handset tier — now a measured datapoint, not a forecast (SemiAnalysis STEEL teardown, 2026-06-14).** The **16GB Huawei Kirin 9030 Pro Max variants were found with both CXMT and Samsung packages** — the CXMT part marked **CXDD7JEDM**, packaged **week 45 of 2025**, with X-ray-CT-inferred die dimensions consistent with **~0.3 Gib/mm² for the CXMT G4 process, roughly equivalent to other makers' 1z** — i.e. **dual-sourced against Samsung inside a flagship Chinese handset, one node generation behind on density**. That is harder evidence than the capacity-forecast and share-estimate arguments this page's China bear leg has rested on (BofA "high-end DRAM stays mostly ex-China," WF "~8% DRAM rev share / HBM 2-4yr behind", roadshow "CXMT ~20% wafer / ~6-7% bits"), and it sits *above* the low-end-consumer tier those framings imply. The offsetting facts from the same teardown: the 12GB Pro carries **Samsung's K4L2E165YD, a 12Gb LPDDR5X-9600 part on the current 1a node** ("current memory rather than older-node inventory"), and CXMT appears only as a *second* source, a generation behind. Watch item on mobile-DRAM share in China, not a near-term server/HBM threat. (SemiAnalysis "Is SMIC N3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **HBM4 top-bin qualification, not just qualification** — Nvidia's above-JEDEC **10.7 Gbps** HBM4 pin-speed target (vs **7.6 Gbps** on AMD's MI455X) forced suppliers to rework HBM4 and means Nvidia "must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD," so the risk concentrates in **yield on the premium bin** and in the widening quality/price spread between the three suppliers — directly on top of the page's live 1c-yield debate (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24). Two-sided: it also means missing Nvidia's spec locks a supplier out of the *premium bin* rather than out of HBM4 entirely. SemiAnalysis names no bin winner.
- **Foundry yield gap vs TSMC** — wins are availability-driven; profitability/utilization remains the swing.
- **Korea-specific:** union strike (7–12% OP downside, JPM 2026-05-08); concentrated chaebol governance.
- **Macro:** tariffs (mainstream-memory pricing sustainability tariff-sensitive per Q425 Techknowledge takeaways), FX (KRW), China demand; memory cost inflation pressuring own MX/SDC margins (Q1-2026 call).
- **Capex intensity:** "substantial" 2026 increase into a potentially cyclical top.

## Intra-quarter — calls, commentary & reports (since the last print)
_Q2-2026 · Jul 30, 2026 → open · sell-side / expert calls / reports since the 2026-07-30 print. Prior window (Q1-2026, Apr 30 → Jul 20) archived under `## Changelog`. Timeline visual: [timeline.html](timeline.html)._

**Signal vs management** — what management said on the last call × what the intra-quarter flow is saying (✓ confirms · ⚠ nuances · ✗ contests):

_**Síntese — update after the conference call and the full sell-side wave (2026-07-30 PM):** the call itself moved the stock twice in opposite directions, and the reason is the single most important new disclosure of the memory reporting season. Samsung rallied from down to **+4.6%** on foundry (**named Broadcom, 2nm design wins to more than double y/y**) and on management saying the memory shortage **worsens in 2027 and stays tight through 2028** ("we will not see any meaningful increase in supply through 2028"), then **faded to close -3.7%** when it disclosed that **60-70% of future DRAM *and* NAND capacity will sit under LTAs** — the highest LTA share of any memory maker. Jefferies' O'Mara has the buy-side logic: "that **limits the optionality on the upside in pricing** as we go into next year. Investors from the US said **Micron's comments on LTAs capped its upside and maybe that is happening to Samsung too**." So the LTA framework is simultaneously the bull case (five-year terms, floor prices, five CSP contracts done + five more in the pipeline, **>25% of total prepayment already collected**) and the cap on it. Second key fact: Samsung **out-executed SK hynix on both DRAM bit shipments (+low-teens q/q vs high-single-digits) and DRAM ASP (+mid-40% vs +30%)** — Susquehanna reads this as genuine high-value share capture, while the BTG former-SK-hynix expert argues it is mostly mix (Samsung ≤25% of capacity on HBM vs SK hynix ~40%, so more of Samsung's base caught the conventional DRAM spike). Both are partly true; the discriminator is **HBM4 yield, where the same expert puts Samsung at ~60% vs SK hynix 80-85%** — which is the real reason to doubt the "HBM share converges to DRAM share in 2027" guide. PT wave, note the direction: **MS OW W381,000 · JPM OW ▼W400,000 from W480,000 (multiple reset 8x→6x F12M P/E) · Susquehanna Positive ▼W725,000 from W850,000** — i.e. everyone raised numbers and cut targets, on de-rating not de-rating of the business._

_**Síntese do quarter** (opened 2026-07-30): the durability bears lost the quarter on the numbers — **record operating profit of ₩89.5tn (~$62bn), up more than 19x y/y, on all-time-high revenue of ₩171.5tn (~$119bn), with nearly all of the profit from semiconductors** — and management explicitly guided the market to stay **undersupplied**, with server-chip demand "expected to accelerate further" in 2H on AI infrastructure and agentic AI. That is a direct confirmation of the archived window's HBM/pricing bulls (and of GS's observation that the market "disbelieves the durability" at 5-6x). Two honest caveats: the non-memory side is now paying for it — an **operating loss in mobile/TV/appliances partly on higher component costs**, the mirror image of [[QCOM]]'s and [[ARM]]'s memory-driven handset demand destruction the night before — and the stock **finished roughly flat** on the print (-1% in the Asia session), i.e. the tape is fully aware and the debate has moved entirely to 2027-28 supply. **Update 2026-08-03 (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur, hosted by Jefferies — the primary supply-chain call): the industry half of the thesis got harder confirmation and Samsung's own half got a caveat.** Confirmation: **HBM4 to NVDA re-marked to $30-32/GB from $17-18/GB on HBM3-class parts** (~1.8x; ⚠ the transcript dates it "in 2026" in the presentation and "in 2027" in Q&A — ambiguity recorded, not resolved), a **Rubin rack BOM of $7-8m vs $3-3.5m for a GB200-class rack**, a **Rubin GPU at $78-80K on 75-80% assumed NVDA chip GM** — and, most usefully for the durability debate, **ODM procurement desks refusing the de-spec: cutting the memory module "was discussion" but is "unfeasible for AI server," with no 2027 spec change seen "including the Google, AWS," and purchasing teams raising capex to fund buy-and-sell of GPUs.** Caveat, and it lands squarely on this page's weakest leg: **Fubon sees "only a SMALL VOLUME of Micron and Samsung's HBM" on Rubin, with SK hynix HBM4 the bigger-volume ramp from late-Aug/Sep** — the platform's 200-300K → 700-800K unit step is paced by [[SKHYNIX]], not by Samsung. **Early qualification, small volume: the bear line on this page ("HBM4 qualified but volume not ramped") is corroborated, and SemiAnalysis's ~30% of first-12-month Rubin HBM supply becomes the number to defend in Sep-Dec rather than a settled mark.** Two offsets worth carrying: the surviving **two-compute-die Rubin Ultra at "around 384 GB"** (the four-die ">1,024 GB" part is cancelled) is the **second independent arrival at this page's 384GB scenario**, after Samsung's own 12-Hi 48GB HBM4E sampling (Arete, 07-21) — so the 12-Hi choice reads as a platform read rather than a capability ceiling; and **"the HBM base die issue," one of the two named causes of the ~two-month Rubin push-out**, is the rare HBM problem where Samsung's in-house advanced-logic base-die capability is an argument in its favour._

The BTG-hosted session, in the company's own words, says something **narrower and far more useful**: de-spec **IS being evaluated by customers** (*“a lot of key [customers] are… considering lowering HBM [stack] layer, or lowering the commodity density”*), the **cause is SUPPLY CONSTRAINT rather than weak demand** (*“not a big technology issue or other issues, other than the demand issue… if they insist on same stacking layer like twelve or sixteen layer, they may not get enough HBM volume”*), **demand shows no slowdown** (*“we haven't seen any kind of slowdown signal so far”*), **fulfilment is only 60-70%**, and **unmet 2026 demand is deferred into 2027**, which is why S/D gets *tighter* in 2027 and 2028. ➤ **The two are COMPATIBLE — ‘no de-spec has been adopted in a shipping high-end GPU spec’ and ‘customers are actively considering it because they cannot get enough supply’ can both be true — but they carry OPPOSITE trading implications. The flat denial is a falsifiable point forecast that the Jefferies/SemiAnalysis/TrendForce checks can break inside 1-1.5 months. The primary's version is close to UNFALSIFIABLE-BY-DE-SPEC: if content per GPU falls, management has pre-framed it as evidence of shortage, not of demand destruction — which is exactly the mechanism Mizuho/SemiAnalysis quantify as ~20-30% MORE GPU/ASIC shipments off the same DRAM. DO NOT COLLAPSE THEM — the affordability driver is a different claim from the supply driver and cuts the opposite way for the bull case.** **(b) HBM SHARE / PRICE: SemiAnalysis puts Samsung's 2027 HBM4 at ~$3.5-3.9/Gb versus [[SKHYNIX]] ~$3.0-3.4/Gb — a ~15% premium — with Nvidia-bound price step-ups of +98% for Samsung vs ~+70% for SK hynix and +89% for [[MU]], and the technical reason named: Samsung's 1c DRAM + SF4 logic base die is *"superior to (and more expensive than) SK Hynix's combination of 1b and N12,"* with Samsung's front-end HBM yield *"materially improved"* versus its laggard HBM3E 12-Hi. It supports the two houses that put Samsung #1 in HBM in 2027 with a mechanism, and it lands next to UBS's +90% Samsung blended HBM ASP for '27E against JPM's +42% industry — corroborating the high side of that 48pp spread without closing it.** ⚠️ **SINAL UNCHANGED on this row: management's own two-sided record on de-spec is what makes it unresolvable, and a fourth outside view does not resolve it. The price/product leg is net-positive but is an estimate from a house with no rating, no PT and no model.** **➕ 09-06 — THE ROW FINALLY GETS THE MISSING GENERATION LABEL, AND IT IS WHAT MAKES MANAGEMENT'S TWO-SIDED RECORD SURVIVABLE. SINAL STILL UNCHANGED.** **Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan, Daniel Yen, Daisy Dai, Tiffany Yeh, Lucas Wang, Henry Zhao, Ethan Jia ("Greater China Semiconductors: SEMICON Taiwan 2026 key takeaways — we need more", 2026-09-06): *"our recent supply chain checks suggest that **Rubin Ultra CPU is highly likely to use 8-Hi HBM4, around one-third fewer than 12-Hi in Rubin**. But by **increasing HBM4 base-die units and migrating to HBM4e, plus some software optimization**, total memory bandwidth should be sufficient for a token output increase."*** ⚠️⚠️ **MS's own wording is "Rubin Ultra ***CPU***" — stack height is a GPU/package attribute and Rubin Ultra's CPU is Vera. QUOTED VERBATIM, NOT CORRECTED; THE AMBIGUITY IS UNRESOLVED.** ➤ **THE CUT LANDS AT RUBIN ULTRA, NOT AT RUBIN — which is exactly the distinction that lets BOTH of management's 08-25 statements stand: "still no HBM de-spec for high-end GPUs" (BofA relay) can be true of the CURRENT platform while the DS spokesperson's "it IS being considered, driven by SUPPLY CONSTRAINT" is true of the NEXT one. Is this the start of the memory downcycle?"* 🔴 **WHY HE REVERSES:** the three HBM yield limits are (1) stacking-stage packaging yield, (2) larger die area → fewer KGD per wafer, (3) TSV wafer-process loss. Only (1) is elastic — *"stacking 4-High or 8-High is a far more mature process than stacking 12-High or 16-High, so simply LOWERING THE STACK HEIGHT YIELDS MORE HBM. That leads straight to MORE ACCELERATOR SHIPMENTS."* Net: *"if giving up capacity means selling HBM4 and HBM4E at 8-High, it also means securing higher packaging yields. That means more HBM, and in a larger sense MORE RACKS SHIPPED, so TOTAL HBM DEMAND ACTUALLY INCREASES compared with the world where everyone kept insisting on high capacity HBM."* **THE DEMAND-SIDE MECHANISM: the need to keep everything in HBM is shrinking — models distributed across many GPUs, prefill/decode disaggregated, cold KV cache and model state moving down to **LPDDR, CXL and NAND tiers**; quantization and MLA offset weight/KV growth. Past the minimum working set *"the additional value comes MORE FROM BANDWIDTH THAN FROM CAPACITY"* — and higher-bandwidth HBM **consumes MORE wafers**, because *"only a limited share of dies on a wafer passes SPEED BINNING."* So buyers trade capacity for bandwidth, and DRAM tightness is not relieved.** ⚠️ **THE CYCLE CLAIM, which is the testable one: *"Thanks to HBM, memory has become LESS CYCLICAL. Even if a downturn comes, HBM demand will not shrink. And: *"the logic camp, [[NVDA]] included, does not want the memory makers' role to expand. | **08-29** | 🔴 **CORPUS (Hot Chips) — SAMSUNG PRESENTS A THREE-PHASE HBM BASE-DIE ROADMAP ENDING IN "zHBM": MEMORY STACKED DIRECTLY ON THE XPU, WITH THE 2.5D INTERPOSER ELIMINATED** ⚠️ **conference-presentation relay in Chinese via the corpus; vendor-claimed figures, no independent verification** | produto | **bull** | 🔴🔴 **THE MOST DETAILED FORWARD ARCHITECTURE ON THIS PAGE AND IT IS A DIRECT ANSWER TO THE "HBM IS A COMMODITY" BEAR CASE. Samsung's stated framing: first use advanced logic to rebuild the base die (B-die), then upgrade it from a *"data relay station"* into an *"intelligent partner"* of the AI system, and finally with **zHBM stack memory VERTICALLY DIRECTLY ON the GPU/TPU-class XPU, ELIMINATING the 2.5D interposer**.** |
**THE CONSTRAINT IT IS SOLVING: bandwidth is doubling per generation — **HBM4 already >3TB/s per stack, HBM4E ~4TB/s, HBM5 doubling again with capacity above 60GB** — against limits on **TSV density, PHY I/O power and area**. Samsung says it has already moved HBM4 to **D1c DRAM plus a 4nm logic process**, targeting lower power and smaller effective area.**
**PHASE 1 — RECLAIM XPU AREA: replace the conventional PHY with compact **D2D/T2D interfaces built in advanced logic**, add a **Heat Path Block** cutting peak temperature by **>35%**, and offload the **memory controller and SRAM fine-grained repair onto the B-die** — estimated to **reclaim 5-10% of XPU area for a 10-20% performance gain**. **PHASE 2 — FUNCTION EXPANSION (cHBM/AHBM):** use the freed space for **telemetry, on-die test, near-memory capacity expansion and selective processing elements**, reducing data movement and thermal power. **PHASE 3 — zHBM 3D INTEGRATION:** distributed I/O, **wafer-to-wafer and hybrid bonding**, shortening transmission distance and **removing the SerDes entirely**. Versus HBM4E, Samsung claims **~70% better power efficiency, ~230% more bandwidth, up to 100W saved per module, and ~8.3% more power available to the XPU**. (@QQ_Timmy, corpus, 2026-08-29.)** ⚠️⚠️ **EVERY NUMBER ABOVE IS VENDOR-CLAIMED AT A TECHNICAL CONFERENCE, WITH NO DATE, NO CUSTOMER AND NO PRODUCTION COMMITMENT. Treat as roadmap intent.** ➜ **BUT IT IS STRATEGICALLY LOAD-BEARING FOR THIS PAGE FOR ONE REASON: every phase increases the LOGIC content of an HBM stack, and Samsung is the only DRAM maker with captive advanced logic. It is the same argument as the NVHBM customised-logic-die question above, stated as a product roadmap. Corroborating third-party framing: UBS's Hot Chips takeaway that *"efforts around NEW MEMORY ARCHITECTURES are very important to watch"* and that *"memory is just as important – maybe more so – than compute for AI inference"* (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-08-31), and SemiAnalysis listing *"Samsung's HBM4 comeback"* among its Jalapeño-episode topics (2026-08-29).** |

Logged on both counts.** **Flow item: in the MSCI August review effective at the 2026-08-31 close, Samsung is not among the named largest TMT buys or sells; the named TMT buys are [[SNDK]], [[KIOXIA]], GOOGL and Hynix, and the sells [[MU]] and TYL (UBS TMI, 2026-08-31).** |
| 08-28 | 🔴 **Bernstein · Mark Li et al. — "Global Memory: An AI memory primer"** + **Wells Fargo · Rakers weekly (08-21)** | produto | **mixed** | **SAMSUNG'S OWN ANSWER TO THE HBM PACKAGING WALL IS zHBM, AND BERNSTEIN IS OPENLY SCEPTICAL ON THE PHYSICS.** **What it is: today HBM stacks connect to the XPU through an interposer beneath them, making the approach *"2.5D"*. **With zHBM Samsung aims to take it to "3D" by putting the HBM stacks ON TOP OF the XPU**, shortening the HBM-XPU communication path further. To bond the DRAM dies Samsung plans **wafer-to-wafer hybrid bonding**, which Bernstein reads as partly a thermal fix (*"hybrid bonding indeed requires no gaps in between dies and can dissipate heat faster"*).** ⚠⚠ **THE SCEPTICISM, VERBATIM AND UNRESOLVED: *"we wonder HOW THE DRAM DIES IN zHBM CAN WITHSTAND THE HEAT DISSIPATED FROM XPU IN THE BOTTOM and keep its operations within the designed specifications."* Putting DRAM directly on top of the hottest component in the system is the whole risk, and the covering analyst does not consider it answered.** ➤ **Logged against this page's HBM-leadership debate: zHBM is a genuine architectural differentiation attempt, but it is a physics bet, and Bernstein is not underwriting it.** ⚠️ **TICKER TABLE — CONFIRMS: Outperform, PT KRW 440,000 (price KRW 256,500 at the 28-Aug close); preferred 005935.KS PT KRW 374,000 (price KRW 186,800); SMSN.LI PT $7,350.** ⚠⚠ **EPS TRAP, PER THIS WIKI'S STANDING RULE — DO NOT RECONCILE THESE AGAINST BBG: Bernstein prints the SAME EPS against BOTH the common and preferred lines (2026E KRW 48,393; 2027E KRW 77,273). Broker headline EPS and BBG differ on the common-vs-preferred share count. Place SAMSUNG at NET INCOME for any consensus comparison.** **From the Wells Fargo weekly: Samsung held the leading CLIENT SSD ship share at **27.5% in 2Q26, up from 22.9%** in the prior quarter; and Samsung Ventures is an investor in Rebellions, whose Rebel 100 accelerator is built on **Samsung 4nm** NPU chiplets.** (Bernstein · Mark Li / Mark C. Newman / Edward Hou / Phoebe Sun, "Global Memory: An AI memory primer", 2026-08-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_SNDK_Global_Memory_An_AI_memory_primer.html) |
| **08-27** | 🔴🔴 **BofA · Simon Woo, CFA (Merrill Lynch Seoul) w/ Dai Shen, Vivek Arya, Mikio Hirakawa, Matt Shin** — "Global Memory Tech: Weekly theme: NVIDIA’s 70% growth vs memory, tour takeaways, Sept spot upside" (industry overview, 2026-08-27) ⚠️ **industry note — no SAMSUNG rating/PT stated in the summary; company detail is from BofA's own virtual tech tour** | **demanda / supply / capital** | **bull** | ★ 🔴🔴 **THE MOST DIRECT ANSWER YET TO THE DE-SPEC BEAR, AND IT COMES FROM SAMSUNG ITSELF ON A HOSTED TOUR: *"**NO DE-SPEC FOR HIGH-END GPU/CPU**"* — alongside *"only **50-60% SUFFICIENCY RATIO (supply-to-demand) EVEN NEXT YEAR**."*** ⚠️⚠️ **READ THIS PRECISELY, BECAUSE IT DOES NOT CONTRADICT THE DE-SPEC THESIS THIS WIKI INGESTED ON 08-26/27 — IT LOCATES IT. The SK hynix-sourced reconciliation logged this week was rationing DOWN THE TIER STACK; Samsung is saying the HIGH END is not being de-spec'd. Those are the same claim seen from two ends: scarce bits are being held at the top and withheld further down. Both stay on the wiki, unnetted.** 🔴 **THE HEADLINE CALL: *"Memory industry growth can be HIGHER THAN NVIDIA's (e.g., 80% vs 70% in 2027) due to shortage, mix and ASIC growth themes."* BofA's current global DRAM sales forecast is **+48% (ASP +24% × bit growth +19%)**; on a **10% QoQ ASP increase through 1Q-4Q27**, 2027 DRAM sales grow **80%+ (ASP +46% × bit +19%)**.** ⚠️ **The +80% is a SCENARIO built on a uniform 10% QoQ ASP path, not BofA's base case (+48%). Do not carry it as a forecast.** **Three stated drivers: (1) NVIDIA's own 70% guide *"is ALREADY BASED ON memory shortage"*; (2) *"the **ASIC/TPU camp is pursuing HBM, DRAM and NAND MORE AGGRESSIVELY and is WILLING TO PAY HIGHER PRICES THAN NVIDIA**"*; (3) scope for 10%+ QoQ ASP in 1H27/2H27 if NVDA and hyperscalers absorb the new big-3 capacity — with *"[[AAPL]], China OEMs and Taiwan module makers"* possibly paying above-LTA 2027 prices.** ➜ **Driver (2) is the one that matters most for this page and it inverts a standing assumption: the custom-silicon camp is described as the PRICE-SETTING buyer, not the price-taking one.** **SAMSUNG-SPECIFIC TOUR TAKEAWAYS: **cash-dividend-centric shareholder return** (vs [[SKHYNIX]]'s buyback-centric); **new P4 fab MAINLY FOR HBM**; **60-70% of capacity under LTA**; *"quite big portion of capex spend used for **SHELL FAB CONSTRUCTION**"*.** ➜ **Shell-fab capex is optionality, not supply — it is spend that does NOT add bits until tools go in, which is the reconciliation between record capex and a 50-60% sufficiency ratio.** **Sept spot: module makers and white-box OEMs *"cannot get sufficient amount of commodity DRAM/NAND … due to the preference toward Big Tech names"*; BofA's channel check shows **sub-50% sufficiency for Sept spot** and it sees **10-20% spot price upside in September** *"due to shortage and peak seasonality"* — even though *"current spot price suggests NEGATIVE MARGINS for most IT products."* Korea-complex reads from the same tour: **Samsung C&T** — SMR *"more critical for data center power but current construction mostly EUROPE-CENTRIC instead of US"*; **Samsung SDS** — data-centre capacity **110MW+ (2025) → 230MW+ (2029) → 800MW+ (2031) including 500MW DBO**. |
| **08-25** | 🔴🔴 **SAMSUNG ELECTRONICS INVESTOR RELATIONS — COMPANY PRIMARY. BTG Pactual-hosted investor call; Michael Randolph (IR) on corporate/capital, a DS/memory spokesperson on semis; “Dongbin” and “Patrick” named as present.** ⚠️ **RAW ASR TRANSCRIPT — proper nouns and one buyback figure garble; anything ambiguous is flagged inline and NOT quoted as a number** | **capital / supply / produto / competicao / guidance** | **bull (operations) · mixed (capital)** | **THE OPERATING HALF, ITEM BY ITEM.** ① **Supply/demand: fulfilment rate “about 60 to 70 percent” across products including HBM; unmet 2026 demand pushed into 2027; “tighter in twenty seven, even twenty eight… very tight… expected to continue AFTER twenty eight.”** ② **HBM: 30-40% share in 2H26 on HBM4, “mid-thirty to high-thirty percent” in 2027 (all-HBM) but “mid- to high-twenty percent” in the HBM4E answer — SEE THE NEW SINAL ROW; both logged, neither reconciled.** Growth rate held at **“at least 50 to 60 percent”** for the next few years; **HBM4E and larger die drive “much higher blended ASP and wafer consumption”**; capacity allocated to HBM **“regardless of the profitability”** to stay a major supplier to **NVIDIA, AMD, Broadcom and AWS**. ⚠️ **UNIT GUARD: “blended ASP” here is Samsung's own product-mix ASP, NOT an industry $/Gb series — it is NOT comparable with the $/Gb marks on this page and does NOT bear on the open UBS +90% vs JPM +42% 2027 HBM ASP dispute, which stays open.** ③ **HBM margin, and this is the most useful new disclosure of the call: HBM margin is TODAY “way lower than the commodity DRAM” because the 2026 HBM contracts were fixed at end-2025 before the commodity price spike; 2027 contracts are “in progress” and management is “quite confident that we can raise the prices much higher… at least a similar level of the commodity DRAM,” so “the margin gap… will be narrowed significantly next year.”** ➤ That is the company's own confirmation of the “HBM-vs-commodity paradox” this page has carried since April. ④ **LTAs — THE 30-40% RESIDUAL FINALLY HAS A STATED PURPOSE, WHICH IS A DIRECT ANSWER TO THE JEFFERIES “LTAs CAP THE UPSIDE” BEAR ON THIS PAGE: “we are decided… we will intentionally allocate sixty to seventy percent toward the LTA. The reason we left thirty to forty percent of the capacity is to address the mobile, PC, and non-LTA customers… in order to take more opportunities, especially more price uptrend, we inevitably have to leave some capacity to address this market.”** The un-LTA'd third is deliberate price-upside optionality, not residue. ⑤ **NAND: essentially ALL new production growth comes from XI'AN (“process migration to Green Nine and Green Ten”) because “in Korea, we don't have enough spaces… in Korea we are mostly allocating the premium spaces toward the D[RAM].” PRODUCT MIX IS TLC, NOT QLC: “we are only working on T[L]C SSD, not QLC” because KV-cache offload from HBM to storage means “not only NVIDIA, but also ASIC and data center customers are placing very high volume orders for TLC SSD” — QLC acknowledged as a larger future market but “in the near term… T[L]C momentum is much stronger.”** ⑥ **FOUNDRY: 2nm HVM for TESLA next year; “multiple hyperscalers” and “multiple AI leading company” orders on 2nm; 4nm NPUs; the Broadcom MOU re-stated by management (“a very meaningful MOU with Broadcom”) — which retires the “unverified analyst aside” flag this page raised on 08-20 as to EXISTENCE, though still NO size, scope, node split or duration is disclosed. “Because of this increase, the volume and backlog, we are CONSIDERING TO BUILD ADDITIONAL CAPACITY.”** Mature node re-purposed toward **silicon photonics** and image sensors; management concedes profitability needs “execution and utilization on [the] marginal” node. ⑦ **3D HBM — NET-NEW PRODUCT DIRECTION AND THE STRONGEST STRUCTURAL ARGUMENT FOR SAMSUNG ON THIS PAGE: stacking HBM DIRECTLY ON TOP OF THE GPU/CPU logic die to cut interconnect, “recently… provided this concept to our customer, and some leading GPU and AI players are quite interested.” Management's claim is vertical integration — “we have the memory division, foundry and advanced packaging division” — and the strategic conclusion is that customised HBM makes memory behave like foundry: “if they start a design and place an order, definitely they cannot change the vendor… the memory industry will have much less… volatil[ity].”** ➤ This is the same architectural thesis [[SKHYNIX]]'s CPO/CXL work points at, arrived at from the opposite end (in-package 3D vs out-of-package optical). ⑧ **CXMT: “we are fully aware and do not dismiss this challenging competitor” and “Chinese… entrant is the biggest threat to existing vendors,” but competitiveness “cannot be evaluated on the capacity alone”; response is “double down on our R&D” plus the solution/server business.** ⑨ **DOWNSIDE PROTECTION — the mechanism named: a “SHELL FIRST” strategy (“we actually prepared the infrastructure AHEAD of bringing in the equipment”), so cleanroom can be filled or not as demand dictates; plus the Applied Materials long-term agreement, which management calls “a SAFETY NET… [to] respond to this market change.”** ⑩ **MX/DX — the cost side of the memory upcycle, worse before better: “we reported a LOSS for the first time in the MX business… memory pricing and component pricing is very burdensome… it's probably going to get WORSE BEFORE IT GETS BETTER.” Offsets: growing UNIT volume and share (A-series, filling a void left by “some C[hina] brands” pulling back), services/AI monetisation, and aggressive cost/headcount management; appliances moving to JDM/ODM and a simplified line-up.** ➤ Direct confirmation of the standing “non-memory drag” Sinal row. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Samsung_IR_call_BTG_raw_notes_25_ago_26.html) 🔴 **RELATIONSHIP TO THE BofA-RELAYED 08-25 ROW BELOW — STATED SO IT IS NEVER DOUBLE-COUNTED: different host (BTG vs BofA), different Q&A roster, and each has items the other lacks → read as TWO SESSIONS ON ONE IR ROUND. This transcript is the FULLER PRIMARY and owns the body; the BofA relay keeps the items only it has (the W15tn buyback expensed at 10.5% of semis OP, 2nd Taylor ~2030, foundry profit 2028-30).** **Where the primary CORROBORATES the relay and adds the mechanism — which is the part worth having:** • **SHELL-FIRST CAPEX.** BofA relays *“aggressive capex is focused mainly on shell fab / infrastructure construction rather than equipment.”* The primary supplies the strategic reason, as downside insurance rather than as guidance: *“we are having the [shell]-first strategy… we actually prepared the INFRASTRUCTURE AHEAD OF BRINGING IN THE EQUIPMENT. It means that if we have more [cleanroom] spaces, we can adequately and FLEXIBLY ADJUST to market dynamics”* — paired with the Applied Materials long-term agreement, which management calls *“a SAFETY NET… [to] respond to this market change.”* ➤ **This makes the Samsung-vs-MS-WFE tension sharper, not softer: shells are being built for capacity that management explicitly reserves the right NOT to equip.** • **HBM-FIRST WAFER ADDS.** BofA relays *“memory wafer capacity expansion is mainly for HBM (minimal for conventional DRAM and NAND).”* The primary gives the geography behind it: *“in Korea, we don't have enough spaces to allocate the capacity toward the [NAND]… in Korea we are mostly allocating the premium spaces toward the D[RAM]”*, with essentially all NAND bit growth coming from **Xi'an process migration** instead. • **LTA PRICE OPTIONALITY — TWO DIFFERENT MECHANISMS, SAME CONCLUSION, AND THEY ARE COMPLEMENTARY NOT DUPLICATIVE.** BofA relays that *“memory ASP remains negotiable on a quarterly basis EVEN UNDER LTAs”* (price optionality INSIDE the contracted book). The primary supplies a second, independent source of the same optionality: the deliberately un-contracted **30-40% residual** kept for mobile/PC/non-LTA customers *“in order to take more opportunities, especially more price uptrend.”* ➤ **Together they retire the Jefferies ‘LTAs cap the upside’ bear more completely than either does alone.** |

| 07-27 | ⚠️ **DAMNANG (independent Substack) — "After CXMT's Listing, Do the Memory Three Have Further to Fall?" ⚠️ DATED 2026-07-27, ~25 DAYS STALE, swept late — price marks are HISTORICAL, the value is the FRAMEWORK. NOT a broker: no rating, no PT** | competicao / valuation | **bull (bear case largely rejected)** | **READ-THROUGH ONLY, but it is the most systematic takedown of the CXMT bear case this page carries.** The event: mid-July 2026, CXMT confirmed its listing date → **Micron −8% and a trillion dollars of market cap lost, Samsung and SK hynix sold off alongside**; on 27 July CXMT **opened RMB49.50 (+472% vs the RMB8.66 IPO price), RMB3.31tn mcap = the most valuable A-share listing at that price, RMB54.65 by midday (+531%)**, with **Samsung and SK hynix both weak that day and FOREIGN INVESTORS NET SELLERS**. The author tests four market claims and rejects them: **① the "30% discount" is a MIX artefact** (company-average ASPs; ~2/3 of CXMT revenue is low-priced mobile memory) — **like-for-like 5-10% per CXMT's own prospectus, and in SERVER the sign FLIPS: a July-2026 64GB DDR5 module on CXMT silicon sold at RMB18,999 vs RMB18,595 for Samsung/SK hynix silicon (CXMT 2.2% HIGHER), and CXMT reportedly quoted ABOVE Samsung's US$1,240/module contract price**; **② it CANNOT go cheaper — >30% higher cost for the same memory, ~40% larger die area, yield short of the 85-90% standard**; **③ the "8% share" is REVENUE (US$7.31bn 1Q26 DRAM = 7.7% of the world market) — on output, 13% of world DRAM WAFER STARTS converting into only 6% of memory CAPACITY**; **④ 1Q26 revenue +71% y/y was PRICE not volume — q/q capacity shipped +11% while ASP +57%**. ⚠️ **CONTRADICTS Wells Fargo's ~24% CXMT ASP discount already on this page (Rakers, 07-14) — both stand, not netted; corroborated on physics by the SemiAnalysis STEEL teardown (CXMT ~0.3 Gib/mm²).** **Samsung-specific, two-sided: Samsung "sells the largest share of its output to Chinese smartphone and PC manufacturers" → "hit first and hardest" if Chinese procurement directives extend to memory, and what CXMT can push outside China is commodity mobile/PC DRAM, so Samsung meets that competition first too; AGAINST that, at 4.32x forward on 27 July (vs SK hynix 4.69x, Micron 6.42x, CXMT ~30x) Samsung was the CHEAPEST of the three, so most of the risk already sat in the price.** Author's conclusion: the memory correction is **more likely to END after the CXMT listing than to continue**. |

Exhibit 12 ("AI Sovereignty & Semi Localization: Top 50 global stocks by materiality and market cap >US$3bn", data as of 12-Aug-2026), MS analyst Shawn Kim, materiality "CORE TO THESIS", mcap US$1,355.8bn on BOTH rows:** <br>• **005930.KS — SAMSUNG ELECTRONICS (COMMON): OVERWEIGHT, last KRW 268,000, PT KRW 381,000 → base +42%, bull +60%, bear −29%.** <br>• **005935.KS — SAMSUNG ELECTRONICS (PREFERRED): OVERWEIGHT, last KRW 187,800, PT KRW 304,800 → base +62%, bull +83%, bear −19%.** <br>⚠️ **SAME market cap, SAME rating, DIFFERENT prices, DIFFERENT targets. This is the ratings-screen analogue of the common-vs-preferred SHARE-COUNT trap this page already documents on EPS (broker headline EPS = common only; BBG = preferred-inclusive; place Samsung at NET INCOME, never EPS).** ✅ **NOTHING SUPERSEDED ON THE COMMON: OW / PT KRW 381,000 is exactly the mark this page already carries from MS · Shawn Kim (07-27 "Playing the AI Infrastructure Dip" and the 07-30 post-print note), and the screen's bull/bear reconcile to the 07-02 "NAND Industry Outlook" scenario values (bull ≈ KRW 430,000, bear ≈ KRW 190,000) — so the whole MS scenario set is internally consistent across seven weeks. **THE PREFERRED LINE (PT KRW 304,800) IS NET-NEW TO THIS PAGE.**** 🔴 **Samsung's +42% base case (common) is the HIGHEST of the ten semis/semicap names in the screen. ⚠️ Hold that against the 08-06 positioning fact already on this page — MS Asia tech replaced Samsung with SEMCO as its Korea Top Pick — i.e. the same house has the largest absolute upside and a lower relative preference. Both are retained.** 🔴 **THE OTHER NET-NEW ITEM IS THE STATE-BACKED CAPACITY LEG, AND IT COMES WITH A DATA-INTEGRITY WARNING. MS describes KOREA'S "THREE MEGA INVESTMENT PROJECTS" as the largest domestic investment programme undertaken by any Korean administration to date, and says they INCLUDE SEMICONDUCTOR CLUSTERS FROM BOTH SAMSUNG ELECTRONICS AND [[SKHYNIX]]. Samsung's named components: expanding chip clusters in YONGIN and GWANGJU, plus a production line in GUMI; an additional allocation to HONAM-region projects focused on AI CHIPS, ROBOTS, BATTERIES AND IT COMPONENTS; and investment in the CHUNGCHEONG region to develop it as a key hub for ADVANCED SEMICONDUCTOR PACKAGING.** ⚠️⚠️ **DO NOT ASSERT THE KRW MAGNITUDES. The exhibit's text layer extracted as "Samsung Electronics plans to invest approximately KRW 2,450 trillion by December 2040, including KRW 2,100 trillion in semiconductors" and "an additional KRW 625 trillion" for Honam. Those figures are roughly an ORDER OF MAGNITUDE above Samsung's actual capex run-rate and above the publicly reported Yongin mega-cluster numbers, and this note's two-column exhibits are demonstrably interleaved by the PDF text extractor elsewhere. The QUALITATIVE facts above are recorded; the numbers are marked EXTRACTION-UNCERTAIN and NEED VERIFICATION against the original exhibit. They have deliberately NOT been placed in the body of this page as facts.** ➤ **The genuinely new and usable piece is CHUNGCHEONG AS AN ADVANCED-PACKAGING HUB: it maps straight onto the HBM back-end bottleneck this page identified on 08-17 (NRD K2 possibly repurposed from R&D to foundry to free capacity for 2nm HBM base dies), i.e. the constraint is stacking/floor space, not wafer starts — and now there is a state programme pointed at exactly that.** **The macro frame, for completeness: restrictions create winners and losers across the stack but *"the broader outcome is a larger and longer-lasting global AI capex cycle"*; multipolarity *"drives a larger global TAM, in particular in the US and China"*; sovereign capacity requires *"additional domestic and regional data centers, greater redundancy, more power generation and transmission, localized cloud and networking infrastructure, and in some cases, parallel technology stacks."* MS's house figure is **US$860bn of AI capex "in the US this year alone"** with a call for further federal support. ⚠️ **US scope — do NOT net against the $877bn 2026 GLOBAL figure from MS's "Power Struggle" (2026-07-14); different bases, not a revision.** **China context relevant to the CXMT threat this page carries: a state-procurement rule evaluating a qualifying "local" product **as if its price were 20% lower**; >RMB 35tn of Six Networks capex in the 15th Five-Year Plan (2026-2030), >RMB 1tn of it compute; an AI-agent framework (May-2026) carrying **domestic-chip compatibility requirements**; "AI Plus" penetration targets of >70% by 2027 / >90% by 2030; and a cybersecurity-review toolkit whose cited precedent is **the 2023 review of Micron** — the closest available analogue for how a foreign memory supplier gets excluded from the China market.** (Morgan Stanley, 2026-08-19) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USELECTION_20260819_1049.html) |

His conclusion is deliberately unflattering to technology narratives: on individual competitiveness "it's difficult to say which is better… each strengths comes from the VOLUME, how much they can produce. So in volume wise, SAMSUNG IS LEADING."** ⚠️ **That is the pro-Samsung version of the scale argument and it cuts against reading the HBM4 yield gap as decisive — both legs now sit on this page, unreconciled.** **(2) THE SPOT-VS-CONTRACT GAP QUANTIFIED, which is why Korean reported ASPs looked weak against the trackers: TrendForce expected 2Q DRAM +55% and NAND +70%, but "those numbers are more for the SPOT MARKET… not the CONTRACT prices."** **(3) LTA SCALE RESTATED WITH THE REVENUE CONVERSION: Samsung "will apply 60 to 70% of their wafer capacity" to LTAs, which he converts to "their revenue is like 80%… of revenue will go to LTAs" — and the product inside them is CUSTOMISED ("it is not like a commodity product where they can sell to the others"), which is his core reason oversupply is harder to trigger than in prior cycles.** **(4) A BEAR-SIDE HBM PRICE CEILING: [[NVDA]] adjusted HBM prices +20% in 1Q, and against the "HBM4 doubles from ~$550 to $1,200-1,400 next year" bull case he expects only +20-30%, because Nvidia protects ~75% system gross margin and "do not increase the BOM… very much."** **(5) On hyperscaler ASIC programmes — asked whether custom silicon is a threat: net POSITIVE for memory, "each players they using HBM and high performance advanced DRAM HBM, so it will increase the sales procurement."** (BTG Pactual-hosted expert call · Hongkyo Jung, former SK Hynix executive, "Beyond the Earnings: What SK Hynix & Samsung Tell Us About the Memory Cycle", 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_30_btg_former_hynix_expert_30_07_26.html) |

Against this page's standing marks that is a risk to size and not yet a miss: the bear line above ("HBM4 qualified but volume not ramped") is directly corroborated, and SemiAnalysis's model of Samsung at ~30% of first-12-month Rubin HBM supply (vs SK hynix ~60%) is now the number that has to be defended in the Sep-Dec window. Those are different businesses and not contradictory, but they are the two halves of how Samsung is spending, and only one of them has a customer book behind it today.** **② THE YIELD TRIANGLE IS NOW QUANTIFIED BY A NAMED ANALYST, and Samsung is the laggard by a wide margin: KeyBanc's John Vinh — "[[INTC]] 18A yields saw meaningful improvements over the past quarter improving to 85% FROM 65%… This still trails [[TSM]]'s N2 yields of 90% but remains MEANINGFULLY ABOVE SAMSUNG FOUNDRY SF2 YIELDS OF 50-60%."** ⚠️ **This page has carried "2nm yields are questionable / behind Intel and TSMC" as a qualitative statement; 50-60% against 90% at TSMC is a ~30-40pt gap and it is the concrete reason Samsung Foundry remains unprofitable. It also frames the [[AVGO]] and [[QCOM]] 2nm design wins correctly: those are wins on capacity, second-sourcing and price, not on yield parity — and at 50-60% the economics of serving them are poor until the gap closes. The 18A datapoint is already on the [[INTC]] page from a 07-14 relay of the same KeyBanc work; the SF2 and N2 legs are the new part here.** **Company-sourced foundry colour from the Q2 release, restated: "Earnings for the Foundry Business IMPROVED SIGNIFICANTLY PRIOR TO INCENTIVE-RELATED PROVISIONS, driven by HBM BASE-DIE DEMAND and STRONG ORDERS FROM CUSTOMERS IN THE U.S. Design wins with major customers continued to expand, with 2nm HPC engagements as a notable example."** ⚠️ **Note the qualifier "prior to incentive-related provisions" — the improvement is stated pre-provision, so it is not a statement that the foundry made money. And note WHAT is driving it: HBM BASE DIE, which is a memory-adjacent workload on trailing-edge-relative nodes, not leading-edge logic. Partial offset: HNA single-exposure replaces LNA multi-patterning, so **litho capex/opex ~1.2x but non-litho cost declines**. On 4F², Bernstein reads the 6F²→4F² transition around 0a as **"a temporary easing of scaling requirements rather than a departure from the long-term trend"** — not a break in the EUV-layer trajectory. This would increase HBM WPM from 170K to 250K by the end of 2026"** (Dealsite / Digitimes via Jefferies, 2026-08-17). **The physical reason it has to be a relocation rather than an expansion is the part worth keeping:** conventional DRAM/NAND packaging runs ~700 process steps, while HBM adds ~19 materials-engineering steps on **entirely different equipment** — TSV formation, wafer thinning to ~30µm, precision die stacking, thermal compression bonding — and **"none of the equipment for any of these steps can also perform conventional DRAM or NAND packaging operations."** Samsung has confirmed **Cheonan is at full HBM stacking capacity across C1/C2/C3 with no room to expand without removing commodity packaging equipment**; Onyang has an eight-floor HBM backend building broken ground in early 2026, but those lines also cannot share floor space with commodity packaging. ⚠️ **All four sources are Korean/Taiwanese trade press relayed through a sales desk — "considering" and "weighing" are the operative verbs, and the 170K→250K WPM figure is the reporter's, not the company's.** ➜ **If it happens, it is a ~47% step-up in Samsung HBM back-end capacity inside ~4 months, which is the supply-side answer to the HBM4/HBM5 share debate on this page.** See [[SKHYNIX]], [[MU]], [themes/hbm-memory.md](themes/hbm-memory.md). |

| 08-25 | **Hot Chips 2026 — Samsung memory roadmap** (Jefferies Asia TMT sales · Conor O'Mara relaying Wccftech / ServeTheHome) | produto | bull | **Standard HBM → cHBM → zHBM put on the record.** **cHBM**: advanced-logic base die adds SoC-like function and replaces the HBM PHY with a compact **D2D** interface (PHY/D2D area shrinks materially HBM4→HBM5); **Heat Path Block covers 50% of the PHY area and cuts peak temperature >35%**. **zHBM**: HBM stacked **vertically on the XPU**, no 2.5D interposer, no SerDes — claimed **70% higher power efficiency, 230% greater DRAM bandwidth vs a standard HBM4e stack, and 8.3% more power freed for the XPU**. ⚠️ **Vendor claims, no third-party benchmark, no MP date.** Companion read from BofA's Hot Chips note (Vivek Arya): **"memory, not compute, is now the key constraint"**, core HBM demand intact and extending into **3D DRAM ("zHBM")**, with **HBF / logic-on-DRAM as long-term TAM adders** and **rising WFE intensity** at TSV formation, hybrid bonding and process control. |
| 08-25 | DIGITIMES (via Barclays desk · Jeffrey Rand; MS TMT · Thomas Wigg) — **Samsung Foundry 4nm volumes rise on [[NVDA]] Groq 3** | produto | bull | **A quantified, non-2nm foundry datapoint.** The Groq 3 LPX ramp is adding **~10,000 monthly wafer input at 4nm now, expected above 15,000 in 2027**. ⚠️ Trade-press estimate, not a company figure. **Why it matters: incremental foundry loading from NVDA at a node Samsung already runs — it does not depend on winning the 2nm leading-edge bake-off that the page's foundry bull case usually rests on.** |

| Theme | Q2'25 (Jul 31) | Q3'25 (Oct 30) | Q4'25 (Jan 28) | Q1'26 (Apr 30) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Headline (rev / OP) | KRW 74.6tn / OP 4.7tn; 6.3% margin (trough) | KRW 86.1tn / OP 12.2tn (+7.5tn q/q) | KRW 93.8tn / OP 20.1tn — record | KRW 134tn / OP 57tn; 43% margin |
| Memory / DS | DS +11% q/q; HBM/DRAM the 2H engine | Best memory sales ever; HBM3E + server SSD | Strongest ever; AI the driver | DS 81.7tn (+225% y/y); memory 74.8tn (+292%) |
| HBM | Positioned as 2H recovery engine | HBM3E demand; HBM4 pre-production | HBM sales to triple y/y in 2026 | HBM4 mass production Feb; "fully booked," >3x y/y |
| DRAM / NAND pricing | — | — | — | DRAM ASP +low-90s%; NAND ASP +high-80s% q/q |
| Foundry | — | Utilization up; 2nm GAA ramp in Q4 | — | Double-dgt y/y; Taylor TX move-in; 1.4nm on track |
| Capex / returns | KRW 3.9tn buyback final tranche | — | — | Q1 capex 11.2tn; 2026 "substantial increase"; div 372/sh |

_Source: SAMSUNG earnings calls (dates above); management commentary, paraphrased._

## Changelog
- **2026-09-09 (/wiki-ingest) — NOTHING SUPERSEDED (no rating, PT, guide or house number on this page changed; SIG · Hosseini re-marks Samsung "Positive" at KRW269,500 on 09-08, consistent with the standing marks), BUT FIVE NET-NEW ITEMS AND ONE OF THEM IS A ROADMAP FIRST.** 🔴 **(1) HIGH-NA EUV INTO DRAM PRODUCTION FROM 2028 — the first memory maker to date it, announced jointly with ASML, together with joining the industry 12'' photomask platform that replaces the decades-old 6'' reticle standard. Samsung is now AHEAD of [[SKHYNIX]] (committed with no timeframe) and well ahead of [[MU]], which is absent from the announcement and which BofA describes as "far behind in EUV adoption (will start next year in new Idaho megafab)". (2) Taylor, Texas reportedly FULLY BOOKED AT 2NM by [[TSLA]], [[AVGO]] and [[ARM]] before pilot operation begins — trial ops late Sept/early Oct, mass production 2027, initial capacity 50,000 wafer starts per month, Fab 2 prep accelerating. (3) DIGITIMES headline that MORE THAN HALF of 4nm foundry capacity is going to HBM4 base dies. (4) The silicon-photonics plan is now dated: FormFactor + MPI probe cards (the same CPO-qualified vendors TSMC uses) bring PIC testing in-house by year-end replacing imec, with SILICON PHOTONICS FOUNDRY SERVICES OPENING IN 2027 and [[AVGO]] named among prospective customers. (5) Samsung LED Mistral AI's EUR 3bn Series D at a >EUR 21bn valuation alongside [[ASML]], and Reuters reports OpenAI "deepening cooperation with Samsung on in-house chips".** ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED: the Taylor booking (trade press, no company statement, and the two relays name DIFFERENT customer sets — TrendForce adds ARM where the Korean version names only Tesla and Broadcom), the 4nm/HBM4 allocation (headline only, no volumes), and the OpenAI chip cooperation (no scope, node, volume or dates). Also carried but not adopted: TrendForce's TrendForce 2Q26 DRAM ranking showing [[SKHYNIX]] as the only share decliner in a +59.5% quarter (filed on the SKHYNIX page), and DIGITIMES' "US chip tariffs raise stakes for Samsung, SK Hynix's US memory plans" / "US chip investment push puts ownership at center of Korea's $200 billion deal".** ⚠️ **STANDING CAVEAT REPEATED (it bit this wiki before): place Samsung at NET INCOME, never EPS — broker headline EPS is common-only while BBG is preferred-inclusive — and never hard-code the CY-sum-vs-annual wedge, which inverts by year on this name.** Cross-filed [[ASML]], [[TSM]], [[INTC]], [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[AVGO]], [[OPENAI]], [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md), [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

Consecutive rungs, not competing accounts.
  🔴 **WHAT IT DOES TO THE DE-SPEC ROW: the cut landing at RUBIN ULTRA rather than at Rubin is exactly what lets BOTH of management's 08-25 positions stand — the BofA-relayed *"still no HBM de-spec for high-end GPUs"* about the current platform, and the DS spokesperson's *"it IS being considered… supply constraint"* about the next. **Neither is confirmed; the row does not resolve and the Sinal does not move.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW AND SAMSUNG-POSITIVE, FROM A THIRD-PARTY RELAY: *"Samsung's improving HBM4 yields point to intensifying competition into 2027"* and *"Nvidia is broadening its HBM supplier base and product mix"* — corroboration of the two houses that put Samsung #1 in HBM in 2027 and of the SemiAnalysis price/product mechanism (09-01).** ⚠️ **A sales-desk relay of trade press is the weakest evidence class here; it corroborates, it does not confirm.**
  🔴🔴 **THE BULL INVERSION, AND IT IS THE MOST SAMSUNG-FAVOURABLE FRAMING OF THE DE-SPEC ON THE WIKI: @jukan05 argues stack heights *"were lowered to make supply more elastic"*, scarcity relocates from stack height to **PIN SPEED**, and per-layer value RISES. The page already holds the complement from the same channel a week earlier — *"SK hynix and Micron have struggled to achieve the highest HBM4 speeds… SAMSUNG CURRENTLY HAS THE BEST HBM4 TECHNOLOGY"* (SemiAnalysis via @jukan05, 08-30). Fewer layers, faster pins, speed leader takes the marginal socket.** ⚠️ **NEITHER ADOPTED — an X post and a relayed third-party assessment; no benchmark, no qualification disclosure.**

Samsung is the only memory maker that also owns a foundry, so a base-die offset need not be pure value leakage here. THIS WIKI HAS NO EVIDENCE Samsung Foundry captures any of it and MS offers none.**

SemiAnalysis's **+98% for Nvidia-bound Samsung HBM4** lands beside UBS and ~2.3x JPM. **The spread stays open; nothing is netted.**
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE DE-SPEC ITEM GOES FROM THREE DATED POSITIONS TO FOUR, AND THE NEW ONE CARRIES A DIFFERENT DRIVER.** The page holds three 2026-08-25 positions (BofA relay: *"still no HBM de-spec for high-end GPUs"* / BTG-hosted IR primary: de-spec IS considered but the driver is SUPPLY not weak demand / SemiAnalysis-via-Mizuho: cuts extend to Feynman). **Morgan Stanley's Shawn Kim (09-01) now states significant de-spec from customers on BOTH HBM AND SOCAMM with no supply qualifier, and attributes it to AFFORDABILITY — with next year's iPhone going 12GB → ~9GB "because things have become so expensive."** ➤ **The affordability driver is a DIFFERENT claim from the supply driver and cuts the opposite way for the bull case. DO NOT COLLAPSE THE FOUR. The Signal verdict on that row is unchanged, because management's own two-sided record is what makes it unresolvable.**
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE ATTACK MOVED FROM EARNINGS TO THE MULTIPLE, AND IT DIRECTLY CONTRADICTS TWO BULLISH VALUATION FRAMES ALREADY ON THIS PAGE.** Kim: China's entry is *"a derating element to these stocks"* — forced capex/R&D, less operating leverage, lower through-cycle margin, lower sustainable ROIC — anchored on **December 2017, when the industry earned >70% operating margins and the Korean stocks still never traded above 2x book**, versus **~4x this cycle**, and he argues the next upturn may not get there. **Against: KB Securities via Jefferies (08-20) — *"at just 4x P/E, the stock looks set to enter a full-fledged phase of valuation normalization and re-rating"* — and JPM (09-01) at *"<4x P/E with demand profiles pointing to an upside risk."* NEITHER IS ADOPTED. This is now the sharpest live valuation disagreement on the page.** Competitive clock behind it: **CXMT above [[MU]] on wafer capacity by 2028 (potential #3 DRAM), YMTC probably #2 in NAND above SK hynix by 2028-29, China top-3 in memory within ~3 years**, and the reason offered is the **"AI paradox"** — that the incumbents, this company included, conceded the traditional end markets to chase HBM.

- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — the post-2Q-call detail, and the single most reusable finding is that LTAs are asymmetric collars, not price locks.** ⚠️ **THESIS-DRIFT logged on the valuation basis: BofA raised 2026-28 EPS by 3-5% (on 2-3% higher memory volume/price assumptions and more favourable LTA terms) but CUT the target 2027-28E P/E from ~~10x~~ → **9x**, leaving the PO unchanged at W550,000** — near the top of the previous peak-cycle band of 7-10x (Woo, 2026-07-31). **The OP path is net-new and large: 2Q OP W89.5tn with DRAM OPM ~80% / NAND ~70% even after special bonuses; DRAM ASP guided +23% q/q in 3Q and +8% in 4Q (after +91%/+45% in 1Q/2Q), taking quarterly OP above W100tn (annual W400tn+) in 3Q/4Q26; then ASP flat-to-slightly-down through 2027-28 under LTAs with annual OP still climbing to W500tn+ on volume and yield. Capex W78-100tn p.a. 2026-28; FCF W200tn+ (2026) → W300tn+ (2027-28).** ⚠️ **The trade being described deserves flagging: Samsung is deliberately giving price back "per AI server content increase" rather than defending ASP — a volume/share strategy, not price maximisation.** **THE LTA MECHANICS, which invert the common bear reading: 60-70% of Samsung memory sales go under LTA, but with a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against price increases broadly open at 10-20%+, on mostly 5-year ROLLING tenors where a 6th year can be added as the 1st completes. High LTA coverage is therefore a downside collar with uncapped upside — bullish for realised ASP stability, not a cap on it.** Spot corroboration: **TrendForce July DRAM contract price +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q, FLAT for NAND** (note the divergence); China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming at **17mn units in June vs a normal 25-30mn**; and minimal spot impact from China local capacity because **"China-branded memory [is] not commonly traded in the spot market."** Capital return: buyback framed as **"when, not if"** on low treasury holdings, with 50% of FCF committed (Edward Kim, Head of Korean Equities); **no immediate ADR plan**; **foundry near breakeven in 2H ex-bonus on the 2nm ramp and new US customers.** Third-party mark logged: **KIS PT raised to KRW 650,000**, above BofA's W550,000 (@jukan05, 07-30). Stock **+21%** on the print; **KOSPI +18% overnight with SK Hynix +30% limit-up and Samsung +27%** as the Situational Awareness book cleared. Adjacent corroboration filed: **SEMCO 2Q OP more than doubled y/y on AI MLCC/substrates, annual OP W2tn in 2026 → W5tn+ in 2027, with prepayments funding capacity — and all three top MLCC makers (SEMCO, Murata, Taiyo Yuden) have raised prices.** Cross-filed [[SKHYNIX]], [[KIOXIA]], [[MU]], [[AAPL]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

Sources: AP via GS TMT Today 07-30; GS desk (GS TMT Today) 07-30; Rothschild & Co Redburn · Ben Denney via Rowan Adley 07-30; JPM · Matthew See 07-30. **SUPERSEDED (prior values retained here):** ① **operating profit KRW 57tn (Q1-2026, margin 43%) → KRW 89.5tn (~$62bn) in Q2-2026**, a >19x y/y increase; ② **revenue KRW 134tn → KRW 171.5tn (~$119bn)**, an all-time high; ③ Q1-2026 as "latest quarter" (the DS KRW 81.7tn / memory KRW 74.8tn / DRAM ASP +low-90s% / NAND ASP +high-80s% marks stay in the body as history). ⚠️ **Caveat on the headline: the AP report is a summary of the results release, not the earnings call — the segment-level DS/memory/DRAM/NAND bit-and-ASP detail this page normally carries for Samsung is NOT yet available at this granularity. No rating, PT or estimate superseded.** **The useful item is a timing correction on the page's single loudest near-term catalyst.** This page has been carrying BofA's expectation of a **"massive buyback + earlier dividends" at the print** (logged 07-23/24 and restated 07-26). MS's Sean — who is *bullish* on the buyback case itself ("they can basically buy the entire business in 35 years… I'm on that camp on buybacks that could potentially re-rate these stocks") — lays out why the announcement window is structurally year-end: **the three-year, 50%-of-FCF shareholder-return policy expires this year, but "historically, they do announce these buybacks at the end of the year, so towards the fourth quarter results, because they do need a board to actually look at the amount of free cash flow at year end before making that decision."** Two constraints net-new to the page: **~₩40tn of employee bonus is to be paid in stock and "should not be canceled,"** and **Samsung "historically… tends to buy back stocks when the stock hits book value"** — a trigger far below spot. Same direction as BofA, different quarter; both readings kept, with the Catalysts section now flagging **October-to-January** as the structural window and any 07-30 announcement as upside surprise. **Second, a dispersion worth flagging rather than smoothing: Sean's "close to 100 trillion" ex-provision 2Q OP implies a ~₩11tn labour provision, against the ~₩15+tn provision / ₩110-120tn underlying-OP figures the page carries from JPM (Jay Kwon) and BofA (Simon Woo) on 07-07.** Unreconciled; resolvable at the print, and now a named watch item. We came in at 89") also sits slightly above the ₩84.2tn Street figure on file from Vital Knowledge (07-06) — immaterial, logged for consistency; the actual prelim OP on the page remains **₩89.4tn**, and the **-7% reaction ✓ corroborates** what the page already recorded. **Third, on supply: "Samsung P4 second floor clean room" is named as a driver of TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth moving from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year (~24% next).** Logged as a nuance to — not a reversal of — the standing StoneX/TrendForce cleanroom-constraint thesis, which concerns 2027-28 (Samsung ~740K wpm exiting 2026, +45K in 2027, P5L phase-1 mid-2028); P4's second floor is near-dated bits. **Fourth, print date confirmed as Thursday 2026-07-30**, which places the BofA desk's "7/29 print" phrasing on [SKHYNIX.md](SKHYNIX.md). Also logged: Sean's three-prong memory debate resolving onto **the cycle** (3Q y/y peak, 3Q DRAM +15-20% vs a 4Q needing +60-70% sequential), and his deflation of the mega-capex plan headlines as "not quite important" versus what companies actually see "in the next year or so" — relevant given this page carries three separate plan scopes (₩2,000tn MS/govt, ₩2,450tn Samsung-domestic, $3.1tn JPM-combined).
