- **2026-07-27 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook). 4 intra-quarter rows + Foundry Signal cell updated. No rating, PT or house estimate superseded — but this run materially NUANCES yesterday's headline read and that nuance is the point.** **The 07-26 entry below framed the $200bn [[AVGO]] MOU as, first and foremost, "a foundry win… the standing bear on this page has been precisely that Samsung Foundry cannot land a marquee leading-edge logic customer at scale." JPM (Harlan Sur, 07-27) is the first to decompose it, and on his math that framing is too strong: ~90-95% HBM / 5-10% foundry wafers, implying only ~$40-50B of cumulative Samsung foundry chip sales through 2030, and he believes those wafers are for Broadcom's datacenter-networking, broadband and telco silicon — not leading-edge XPU logic.** The 07-26 text is left standing verbatim (it was correctly hedged as a partnership value, not backlog) and this entry is the correction layer: **the memory leg is the deal; the foundry leg is real but roughly a fifth of the headline and, per JPM, not 2nm logic.** Sur adds the supplier-concentration figure that is unambiguously good for Samsung: **it will supply ~75-85% of ALL Broadcom HBM requirements going forward** — i.e. Samsung is Broadcom's primary HBM source, which is the harder-won position given the page's standing HBM qualification/yield bear. **Not settled — two counterweights logged rather than netted:** UBS (Arcuri, 07-27) says the deal *does* give AVGO **access to Samsung's 2nm process** alongside HBM and advanced packaging (packaging folded into AVGO's Singapore site), which would be a leading-edge read JPM's framing misses; and Bernstein (Li/Rasgon, 07-27) says the announcement **still does not disclose wafer-vs-advanced-packaging** and calls the [[TSM]] impact **negligible**. **Management's 7/29 call remains the resolution point, and the question is now sharper than it was yesterday: not "how big" but "how much of it is logic, and at what node."** **Fourth row is a genuine offset the memory melt-up narrative on this page has been under-weighting: Bernstein's June Apple Tracker has global smartphone shipments -11% YoY in 2Q26 (from -6% in 1Q26), explicitly BECAUSE of memory shortages, with [[AAPL]] taking share to 20% from 17%.** Samsung sits on both sides of that trade — the shortage that is repricing its semiconductor division is compressing its own MX handset TAM and handing units to Apple; partial offset from **DRAM content per phone +27% YoY**. Cross-filed [[AVGO]], [[TSM]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[AAPL]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).
- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch into the 7/29 print). 5 intra-quarter rows. No rating, PT or estimate superseded** — but the page now carries what is, on its face, **the most consequential single datapoint of the quarter: Samsung announced a $200bn / 5-year chip supply contract with [[AVGO]] covering memory, foundry AND advanced packaging** (Reuters, 2026-07-25: "Samsung Elec wins $200 billion Broadcom AI chip partnership, boosting foundry push"). It matters for three reasons, in order: **(1) it is a foundry win**, and the standing bear on this page has been precisely that Samsung Foundry cannot land a marquee leading-edge logic customer at scale — Broadcom is the largest merchant custom-ASIC house there is; **(2) it bundles DRAM/HBM + foundry + advanced packaging into one counterparty**, which is the "turnkey" pitch Samsung has been making against the TSMC-plus-separate-OSAT model, now with a reference customer; **(3) ~$40bn/yr against Samsung's current foundry run-rate is transformational and, if it converts, forces the capex line materially higher** — BofA's desk drew that inference the same day ("bodes well for Samsung capex"), which makes it a WFE read-through as well ([[KLAC]] / [[LRCX]] / [[AMAT]] / [[ASML]], filed to [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md)). **Deliberately NOT treated as a booked number:** as reported it is a supply-contract/partnership *value*, not backlog; no node, product or phasing split has been disclosed; and BofA's own weekend note hedged it as "AVGO **possibly** doing $200B deal with Samsung foundry," i.e. the desk was reading the same press we are. **Management gets its first chance to size or qualify it on 7/29 — that is the thing to watch, not the headline.** One cross-read worth holding: Hock Tan told JPM on 07-07 that Broadcom keeps majority TPU volume with Google, so this plausibly attaches to non-Google ASIC programs and/or HBM/packaging supply rather than implying a Google-share shift. **Second thread — the AMD content position is now explicitly two-sided and both sides stay on the page:** Jefferies reports Samsung "currently supplies HBM4 for AMD's MI455 Instinct GPUs and serves as the dedicated supplier of DDR5 solutions for the 6th-generation EPYC CPUs deployed in the Helios platform," while SemiAnalysis (07-21) documented a quantified HBM-content cut on a Meta-specific cut-down MI part and (07-24) that MSFT "dropped AMD after MI300X due to unreliable Samsung 2023 HBM memory." Named flagship supplier and carrier of the quality stigma on the same account — the **dedicated DDR5 sole-source language on 6th-gen EPYC is the cleaner win**. **Third: an [[AAPL]] System-LSI content gain — Samsung (with Novatek) takes iPhone 18 display-driver-IC share from LX Semicon, whose iPhone DDI share goes from 50%± (2022-25) to ~10%± on iPhone 18 (so ~90%+ to Novatek/Samsung), on Pro / Pro Max / foldable**, and for a technical reason (iPhone 18 "requires more advanced power/heating control") rather than price — which makes it more durable (BofA/Simon Woo, 07-26; LXS downgraded to Underperform, PO W63,000 → W25,000). Also logged: **South Korea's ~$950bn of new AI initiatives** announced around President Lee's US tour (Reuters, 07-25), and BofA reiterating both its **memory-bull stance into "a big week for memory — CXMT IPO, Kioxia, Samsung"** and its expectation of a **massive buyback + earlier dividends at the 7/29 call** (already on page from 07-24). Cross-filed [[AVGO]], [[AMD]], [[AAPL]], [[MU]], [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md), [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).
- **2026-07-25 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook). 2 intra-quarter rows; additive, no rating, PT or estimate superseded** (Susquehanna Positive at ₩252,500 recorded as the standing SIG mark). **(1) "US Negotiating Leverage Bears Fruit" (Mehdi Hosseini, Technology Hardware weekly, 2026-07-25)** — adds a **policy-driven bear case on memory pricing that is independent of demand**, which the page did not have: >50% of Korean memory revenue comes from US customers; **CXMT/YMTC are barred from US OEMs** and sit on the **Pentagon Section 1260H list** (YMTC also on the Commerce **Entity List**); the administration can trade access for US fab commitments, and Korean makers are expected to announce them. **Net-new sizing: CXMT and YMTC at 17% and 25% of DRAM and NAND supply respectively by YE28**, with **US greenfield memory fabs impossible before 2029** — so unlocking Chinese supply is the only fast fix to tightness. Speaks directly to the 2027-28 durability question the 07-24 Rothschild row says the tape is already trading. **(2) Two trade-press items (07-23), both first-time on this page:** **Samsung reportedly eyeing a major investment in Mistral AI** as a "Korean sovereign AI" move (BusinessKorea — unconfirmed, no size; logged because a memory maker taking a stake in a model developer mirrors the AMD/Anthropic and NVDA/OpenAI structures and would create a captive customer); and the **Galaxy Z Fold8 launch running TSMC 3nm silicon with price hikes of up to US$100** (TrendForce) — the 3nm sourcing is a negative read on Samsung Foundry's own leading-edge credibility and consistent with the SF2 yield gap already tracked, while the $100 hike is a concrete instance of the memory-cost pass-through UBS says Samsung and Apple are absorbing better than Chinese Android OEMs. Confirmed print date: **Wed 2026-07-29, 9:00 PM ET**.
- **2026-07-24 (/wiki-ingest — 3 sources into the July 30 print; additive, no figure/rating/PT superseded).** **BofA "Global Memory Tech" weekly + "Simon's tech check" (Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya, 07-23/24)** contributes the strongest fundamental check of the run and three named catalysts for the **July 30 2Q call**: (a) **DRAM contract prices beating** — Tier-2 OEMs/module makers paying **15-20% higher PC DRAM contract prices for July vs June, implying +30-40% q/q vs consensus of +13-18%** (TrendForce, ex-HBM); spot +2-3% w/w; **Korea semis exports +181% y/y in the first 20 days of July**; **China memory imports at a record US$32bn (+~250% y/y)** despite rising domestic output, which BofA reads as evidence that **CXMT's capacity expansion "has had only a limited impact"** — a direct counter to the CXMT bear leg already on the page (cf. the Wells Fargo CXMT deep-dive, 07-14). (b) **Google capex → memory demand quantified:** Justin Post models Alphabet capex **US$200bn/300bn in 2026/27 vs US$91bn in 2025**, implying **Google needs 50%+ more memory in 2027 than 2026**, with the mechanism spelled out — memory's rising share of Big Tech capex, **LTA-governed DRAM/NAND prices unlikely to rise much from 4Q26/1Q27 (higher bits at stable prices)**, and **external TPU sales pulling more Asian memory including HBM** (ties to the [[GOOG]] TPU-aaS fold this run); Post has Alphabet FCF at only **-$16bn/-18bn in '26/'27 before +$37bn**, which BofA frames as supportive of memory-capacity expansion. (c) **Three expected call catalysts — a massive new buyback, earlier-than-normal dividends, and more bullish 2H/2027 guidance** — with the buyback mechanic newly explained: the **treasury-share-based special bonus (10.5% of semiconductor OP, likely W30tn+)** requires treasury shares and **SEC currently holds none**, so a buyback is *necessary* to fund the January special bonus and must be disclosed early given the time to execute; the dividend pull-forward (**40-50% of 2026 dividends in 4Q26**) splits taxable income across years for its **4mn+ mostly-retail Korean holders**. **@jukan05 (07-23):** Samsung Electro-Mechanics disclosed an **MLCC supply agreement with an unnamed "large global company"** — read as **LTAs spreading beyond memory into passives**, corroborating the whole-BOM lock-up pattern (memory → CPU → components) that underpins the pricing-durability bull leg; the standing Capstone-expert caveat that LTAs are "essentially unenforceable" if conditions turn still applies. **Rothschild (Adley, 07-24):** logged the **07-23 tape — Asian memory very weak, KOSPI -5.7% on no specific newsflow**, which Adley attributes to the US session "or even the Anthropic datapoint" (his unverified read that Anthropic ARR growth has slowed — see [[ANTHROPIC]], where it downgraded that page's ARR Sinal row). Flagged as a **mechanism worth watching: the Korean memory complex now trades off frontier-lab ARR perception, not only DRAM pricing** — a positioning vulnerability precisely when the fundamental check is beating. Edits: 3 new Intra-quarter Full-log rows (07-23/24). Cross-filed [[GOOG]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[ANTHROPIC]].
- **2026-07-24 (/run-inbox — SemiAnalysis Advancing AI 2026 + 2 dedup passes; all additive, nothing superseded).** Three distinct threads, kept separate and dated.
  - **NET-NEW (thread 1) — HBM4 pin-speed bake-off (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** AMD MI455X **12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s at 7.6 Gbps** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks / 288GB**, Rubin **22 TB/s at 10.7 Gbps (40% faster)** — near-tie despite AMD's 50% wider bus, because "Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification… precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X," a change that "was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin but **as of today these issues have been finally resolved**," and which means **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD**. Framed as a **two-tier HBM4 market** that reframes the page's core "HBM4 price/yield" question as a **top-bin-yield** question — two-sided for Samsung (missing Nvidia's spec locks it out of the *premium* bin, not out of HBM4 — softer than the HBM3E qualification-failure bear, and it lends the already-confirmed MI455X socket more weight; but tighter binning also lands on the live 1c-yield debate). **No bin winner asserted — the source names none**, and the "Memory Mania" bears (1c test yields "extremely low"; HBM4 ES higher jitter / weaker package execution than SK hynix) stand unchanged. Added as a Current-state bullet, a Risks bullet, an Intra-quarter Full-log row (07-24, produto/mixed) and an augmented **HBM** Signal cell.
  - **NET-NEW (thread 2) — the 2027 HBM-pricing call and the offsetting de-spec (same note).** Expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," modelled as strong enough that AMD cannot pass it all through ("at a lower incremental margin than their corporate margins, dragging down gross margins on the MI455X package"); baseline "HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025." **Flagged as two-sided for this page specifically:** if HBM reprices toward commodity-DRAM margins, the bull leg SemiAnalysis itself introduced ("turned bullish" on Samsung *for* its commodity-DRAM mix, since Samsung's HBM margin is the lowest of the three) narrows. Against it, the same BOM shows content being cut: original 2025 MI455X estimate of **138TB DRAM across LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM**, now with **LPDDR5X entirely removed** (the roadmapped **up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR** tier gone on tight memory supply) plus a significant DDR5 RDIMM de-spec — "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs." **Noted explicitly that this now points the opposite way from the LPDDR5X SO-DIMM/SOCAMM demand pull already on the page (NVIDIA Vera CPU) — the two must be netted, not stacked.** Added as a Current-state bullet + a de-spec Risks bullet + folded into the 07-24 log row.
  - **NET-NEW (thread 3) — the MI300X reliability precedent, now with its commercial consequence (same note).** The page already carried the MI300X sole-supplier episode in full (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11: "a big mistake," above-average GPU failure rates, "damaged AMD's relationship with Microsoft") in Debate, Risks and the Signal table — **so the episode itself was NOT re-stated.** What is net-new is the consequence, quoted precisely: "**In 2023, Microsoft dropped AMD as a customer after MI300X due to unreliable Samsung 2023 HBM memory & poor software quality & skipped both MI325X and MI355X**," with Microsoft only now returning via an MI455X Helios commitment (believed mainly for OpenAI as the end customer on Azure). **Two full AMD generations skipped** — the hardest evidence on this page that HBM reliability failures carry multi-year commercial tails. Attribution kept precise: SemiAnalysis assigns blame to Samsung 2023 HBM **and** AMD software quality, not Samsung alone. Added as a sub-bullet under the existing MI300X Debate bullet + folded into the 07-24 log row and Signal cell.
  - **DEDUP — SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset" (2026-07-21).** Already cited by [themes/hbm-memory.md](themes/hbm-memory.md) but **absent from this page**; the quantified content cut was net-new here — Meta's custom MI450/MI455 **halves the compute silicon (8→4 dies), cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 per package, and downgrades HBM4 from 12-Hi to 8-Hi**, on a program SemiAnalysis says will "nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin" (doubly negative: fewer stacks on a shrinking program). | 07-28 | Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean | capital | **mixed** | 🔴 **The buyback the desk consensus is waiting for at Thursday's print probably is not coming at Thursday's print — and the reason is mechanical, not fundamental.** Sean is on the bull side of the argument itself ("Why are they so cheap? Why don't they announce massive buybacks? They can basically buy the entire business in 35 years. That's kind of the bull case, and I'm on that camp on buybacks that could potentially re-rate these stocks, especially when you have visibility on LTAs" — the Apple-over-the-past-decade analogy). But the timing: Samsung's **three-year shareholder-return policy — 50% of free cash flow — expires this year**, and "**historically, they do announce these buybacks at the end of the year, so towards the fourth quarter results, because they do need a board to actually look at the amount of free cash flow at year end before making that decision**." Two further constraints, both net-new to this page: **"in the near term they could be funding this around 40 trillion worth of employee bonus, which will be paid in stock. That one should not be canceled because it will be left for the employee bonus"** — i.e. a large block of treasury stock is spoken for; and **"historically, the company tends to buy back stocks when the stock hits book value,"** a trigger nowhere near current levels. Read against **BofA's standing expectation of a "massive buyback + earlier dividends" at this call (logged 07-23/24), this is a direct temper — same direction, wrong quarter.** On the prelim, restated: "**you had 85 trillion operating profit as a consensus. We came in at 89. But we have to factor in the provision from labor cost. They could have been close to 100 trillion if that was the case. Didn't matter. The stock sold off 7% that day.**" ⚠️ Note the dispersion: **"close to 100tn" ex-provision implies a ~₩11tn provision, against the ~₩15+tn / ₩110-120tn underlying-OP figures this page carries from JPM (Jay Kwon) and BofA (Simon Woo) on 07-07** — unreconciled, and resolvable at the print. Print date confirmed as **Thursday 2026-07-30** ("we do have earnings for Hynix tomorrow. And we have Samsung on Thursday"), which places the BofA desk's "7/29 print" reference on [[SKHYNIX]]. On supply, Sean deflates the mega-capex headlines — "those things to me are more something that is not quite important… companies are more focused on what's visible in the next year or so" — and points instead at bits: **TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year and ~24% next, with "Samsung P4 second floor clean room" named as a driver** (alongside SK Hynix's HBM3E-over-HBM4 mix freeing wafer to commodity DRAM); NAND high-teens → **20-25%** next year on YMTC's third line at **50,000 wafers**. Sector frame: his three-prong memory debate resolves by elimination onto **the cycle** — y/y DRAM pricing peaks in 3Q, 3Q hikes ~15-20%, and 4Q would need **+60-70% sequential** to sustain the second derivative, "a bit tough to achieve." Investor tone: "**generally remained quite bullish on AI demand, while incrementally less optimistic on the non-AI consumer end market**," with "valuations… clearly an attractive entry point for memory with the potential buyback as near-term catalyst" — and the setup risk that **"recent good news [is] being sold for leading stocks like Samsung, TSMC and ASML on really good numbers and guide."** (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html) |
| 07-27 | JPMorgan · Harlan Sur | supply | mixed | **The first sell-side decomposition of the [[AVGO]] MOU — and it is mostly a memory deal, not a foundry deal.** Sur estimates AVGO's Samsung purchase mix at **~90-95% HBM / 5-10% foundry wafers** against the **>$200B over 5 years through 2030**, implying only **~$40-50B of cumulative Samsung foundry-based chip sales** — and he believes those wafers are for **AVGO's datacenter-networking, broadband and telco service-provider chips**, i.e. mature/mid-node merchant silicon rather than leading-edge custom XPUs. (Reiterates AVGO **OW, PT $580**; JPM covers Samsung via Jay Kwon, **OW, ₩252,500 at 24-Jul close**.) (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-27) |
| 07-27 | UBS · Timothy Arcuri ("SemiBytes") | supply | bull | Counterweight to the JPM decomposition: UBS reads the **Samsung/AVGO MoU ($200B+ over ~5 years to C2030, memory + foundry)** as AVGO **securing memory supply and potentially reducing its reliance on TSMC**, because the agreement gives AVGO **access to Samsung's 2nm foundry process, HBM, and advanced packaging** — with the **advanced-packaging leg being added to AVGO's own Singapore packaging facility** (i.e. Samsung supplies the capability/capacity, AVGO internalises the step). If the 2nm access is used, that is a leading-edge logic read JPM's networking/broadband framing does not capture. Same note flags the parallel **NVDA / SK Group $500B+** deal (SK Telecom up to 2GW on Vera Rubin + [[SKHYNIX]] HBM4, ~$70-80B of NVDA revenue) — so both Korean memory houses signed multi-hundred-billion AI supply anchors in the same weekend. | 07-27 | **Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai — "Global Memory, NVIDIA & Broadcom: Quick thoughts on strategic partnerships" (PRIMARY PDF, 16pp; ingested 2026-08-11)** | supply | **bull** | 🔴 **THE PRIMARY BEHIND THE DESK RELAY IMMEDIATELY BELOW, ingested two weeks late (file locked on P: across three runs). The relay's content was verified and is NOT repeated. The primary partially answers the question this page logged on 07-27 as unresolved — "not how big, but HOW MUCH OF IT IS LOGIC, AND AT WHAT NODE."** 🔴 **① THE ANSWER, verbatim: the [[AVGO]] MOU covers "US$200B worth of memory (including HBM) & foundry service over the next 5 years through 2030. THE FOUNDRY SERVICE WILL FOCUS ON 2NM & BELOW FOR PRODUCTS SUCH AS WIRELESS BROADBAND COMMUNICATIONS SOLUTIONS (but how about AI ASIC?) as well as 2.3D (Cube-E & -R, similar to TSMC CoWoS-L and -R) & 2.5D (Cube-S, similar to TSMC CoWoS-S) advanced packaging technology for 'AI & networking' silicon."** ⚠️ **Read that carefully, because it cuts BOTH ways and the page should not take only the half it likes. It CONFIRMS JPM's 07-27 decomposition on product (networking/broadband, not leading-edge XPU logic) while CONTRADICTING the implication that the foundry leg is therefore mature-node: Bernstein says the node is 2nm AND BELOW. So Samsung is landing 2nm volume — just not, on this evidence, for accelerators. And the parenthetical "(but how about AI ASIC?)" is Bernstein's own, flagging that the announcement does not say. This is the most precise public characterisation of the foundry leg the wiki has, and it does not settle the AI-ASIC question; it sharpens it.** **② The packaging leg gets its product names and its TSMC equivalents for the first time: Cube-S ≈ CoWoS-S (2.5D), Cube-E and Cube-R ≈ CoWoS-L and CoWoS-R (2.3D) — i.e. Samsung is fielding a full CoWoS-equivalent line, not a single alternative. Bernstein's separate 08-10 TSMC note (see [[TSM]]) calls Samsung's entry into this field "INEVITABLE given the resources that Samsung commands" and expects it to make CoWoS margins "structurally lower" industry-wide.** **③ THE RATING MARKS, absent from the relay: Outperform, PT KRW 440,000 common / KRW 374,000 preferred (005935.KS) / US$7,350 GDR (SMSN.LI)** — px KRW252,500 / KRW177,100 / US$4,296 at the 24-Jul close — on **6.2x forward 5Q-8Q EPS of KRW71,172**, the identical multiple applied to [[SKHYNIX]], so Bernstein's Korea preference is EPS-driven, not multiple-driven. **EPS KRW48,393 (2026E) / 77,273 (2027E) = 5.2x / 3.3x.** Rating history in the note shows the PT at KRW225,000 (18-Mar-2026) → KRW440,000 (22-Jun-2026) — a near-doubling inside three months. **④ The complex-level logic: Outperform on Samsung, SK hynix and [[MU]], Underperform on [[KIOXIA]], discriminated by product — China is a long-term NAND threat but "the threat is much lower in DRAM as China eventually will find it difficult to compete in DRAM market WITHOUT EUV." For Samsung specifically that is a two-sided argument this page should hold in tension: it protects the DRAM franchise, and it is the same litho-access logic that underwrites Samsung Foundry's own High-NA positioning.** **⑤ Risk, as stated: "an earlier end of favorable pricing environment… weaker demand or higher supply," investor sentiment, and "China's progress in memory, especially in NAND."** (Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai / Alrick Shaw / Arpad von Nemes / Eva Zhang, 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260726_Bernstein_NVDA_Global_Memory-_NVIDIA_-_Broadcom-_Quick_thoughts_on_stra.html) |

(BusinessKorea / TrendForce, 2026-07-23, via Susquehanna · Hosseini weekly, 2026-07-25) | the debate has shifted from "can Samsung qualify HBM4?" (resolved — already in mass production and shipping) to the still-open HBM4 price/yield and, above all, to the *durability* of the DRAM cycle — with the thesis of becoming #1 in HBM in 2027 and the foundry angle gaining traction against top-of-cycle skepticism (GS/ex-Micron). **06-30/07-01 sharpens the capital-cycle leg — headline number revised up ~2.3x in 48 hours:** the Korea AI mega-plan first sized at Samsung ₩2,100tn/~$1.36T (see Changelog 2026-06-30) has been superseded by a **combined Samsung+SK Group figure of ₩4,755-4,800tn (~US$3.1 trillion)** through 2040 for semis/AI datacenters/robotics (JPM/Joshua Meyers citing Jay Kwon; FundaAI, both 2026-06-30) — JPM sizes 60-70% to front-end wafer spend, 20-30% to infra/cleanroom (Jay Kwon, 2026-06-30), implying a doubling of installed DRAM capacity within 5 years and a 7-12yr pull-forward of the Yongin ramp, with memory execs citing supply at only ~50% of demand and worsening (JPM/Joshua Meyers, 2026-06-30) — a US$12-20tn cumulative revenue opportunity for Korean memory makers. Separately, DIGITIMES sizes Samsung's own domestic commitment (anchoring a new Gwangju fab) at ₩2,655tn (~$1.72T) (DIGITIMES, 2026-06-29). Market reaction was mixed: Samsung shares +5% in Asia on the news, though Rothschild's desk read it as largely "political repackaging" (Rothschild & Co, 2026-06-30); the stock then fell -4.3% to -5.6% on 07-01 on NPS rebalancing, weak June DRAM export data and an unconfirmed "memory efficiency breakthrough" X post — even as US semicap names (AMD/AMAT/LRCX/KLAC) hit 12-month relative highs and JPM's Korea capex-beneficiary basket (JPG1KCPX) rose +5%, the "hyperscaler curse" divergence (Jefferies/Cristina Titu; Rothschild/Rowan Adley, both 2026-07-01). KB/Jeff Kim reiterates BUY (TP W550,000) on a memory shortage seen persisting 2+ years, forecasting DRAM +308%/NAND +256% ASP in 2026 with further 2027 upside, and memory's share of AI capex rising from 14% to 50% by 2027 (Jefferies/KB · Jeff Kim, 2026-07-01). A skeptical counterpoint from a Capstone expert call (former Samsung EVP): LTAs for memory/foundry capacity are "essentially unenforceable" if conditions turn, flagging risk that Nvidia-style customers could use LTAs to induce oversupply (Capstone expert call, 2026-06-30) — a direct challenge to the supply-tightness bull thesis underpinning the mega-plan narrative. TPU V10ax at Samsung Foundry and the SF1.4-with-High-NA restart (MP 2029) firm up the foundry-optionality angle, while a US DRAM price-fixing class-action is a new (non-fundamental) overhang. **Backfilled from two SemiAnalysis deep-dives** (Memory Mania 02-06; Scaling the Memory Wall 08-11, added 2026-06-30): these sharpen both sides of the HBM debate with hard detail rather than changing the overall read. Bull leg: SemiAnalysis "turned bullish" on Samsung specifically for its commodity-DRAM exposure (margins there now rival/exceed HBM margins, especially for Samsung given its lower HBM margin) — a genuinely new angle distinct from the HBM-catch-up thesis already on the page, plus a quantified Rubin HBM share estimate (~30%) and a power-efficiency edge on HBM4's 1c front-end. Bear leg: the AMD MI300X episode is now fully specified (sole-supplier "big mistake," GPU failure contribution, damaged AMD-Microsoft relationship) and SemiAnalysis states its own channel view that Samsung has **not yet passed full Nvidia HBM3E qualification** — a materially harder read than the market-chatter "more optimistic" framing already on the page. **07-07:** UBS's SpaceX/SPCX-initiation "Terafab" note adds a double-edged read-through — Musk names Samsung among Terafab's existing suppliers (wanting faster expansion), Terafab explicitly targets memory, and UBS flags Samsung's ample Taylor, TX land as capacity headroom while noting Terafab could prompt Korean US front-end memory investment (more likely DRAM); a long-dated competitive-risk monitor rather than a near-term thesis-mover. **07-08/07-09:** the prelim-print aftermath dominated the tape — MS TMT (Thomas Wigg, 07-08) framed 2Q expectations as MISSED overnight with **Samsung -9%**, dragging the AI-tech beneficiary basket (MSXXAIB -19% off ATHs) and memory -7% on the day (the desk's read, harder than the page's earlier "beat-but-sold-off-on-the-hurdle" framing) — even as the earnings-power bulls got louder: @Mayhem4Markets notes Samsung's chip division will out-earn its entire 40-year semiconductor history in 2026 alone, and @QQ_Timmy pegs memory-business OP margin ~80% in 2Q26. Industry pricing/demand corroboration held (Bernstein tracker mild-MoM-rises + major 2QCY26 increase, Newman & Li 07-08; Goldman ~$1T 2027 hyperscaler capex ~40-50% to memory via @ParadisLabs; @QQ_Timmy +~50% over two quarters, HBM→general DRAM/NAND; BofA HBM $/rack ~quintupling Blackwell→Rubin via @firstadopter), and Newman published a Memory-LTA deep dive + structure call (07-09). Offsetting: CXMT's ~CNY29.5bn (~US$4.1bn) STAR Market IPO (China's biggest 2026 IPO) funds 17nm/DDR5/HBM with HBM3 MP targeted end-2026 (still in qual — @zephyr_z9). **A hard cap on the foundry-optionality bull leg (07-08/09):** JPM's Gokul sizes TSMC at ~90-95% of 2nm in the first two product waves (2028-29), leaving Samsung+Intel combined only ~5-6% "if they can execute" — TSMC's 2026/27 capex ramp largely closes the 28-29 availability gap, and even at lower sticker prices Samsung stays "adjusted more expensive"; GS's TSMC preview corroborates the dominance backdrop (strong N2/N3 demand, CoWoS to ~220k/mo by end-2027). So the AMD/Google-TPU/Tesla/Meta/Anthropic engagement is real availability-driven interest but bounded — Samsung's foundry win-rate on the leading node remains an overflow/second-source story, not a share-shift. Separately, Bernstein's "Memory LTA effectiveness" note (07-09) expects Samsung to announce more LTA deposits (aligning with UBS's 3Q-DDR5-LTA framing) while capping industry LTA coverage at ~50-67% and flagging the ~$3.33B secured so far as <1% of the protective amount — the skeptic's bound on the memory-durability bull thesis. **07-13→07-15 — a capital-markets leg opens and the foundry-win list lengthens.** (1) **US ADR:** Bloomberg reported Samsung is exploring a US ADR/share sale after SK Hynix's 07-10 Nasdaq listing ($26.5bn); Samsung officially denied but Korean media (@jukan05, 07-15) says it has internally launched an ADR-scenario review and even requested SK Hynix's playbook — a potential liquidity/re-rating catalyst (SK Hynix distanced itself the same way pre-listing), though Cramer read it as more supply to absorb. (2) **DRAM capacity adds:** a new **~100K-wpm DRAM fab at Giheung** (converting the historic SR5 R&D site, construction possibly 3Q26) plus a **P4 100K-wpm HBM4-DRAM line** (@jukan05, 07-14) — demand-driven, but incremental supply for the 2027-28 durability debate, and now facing **labor-union opposition** to the Honam mega-project (84% opposed, tabled as a 2027 CBA item — @zerohedge, 07-13). (3) **Foundry:** the Anthropic engagement firmed from "in talks" to a **reported agreed 2nm win**, with **DeepX** added and TSMC overflow "flowing to Samsung"; the **Taylor fab completed Tesla's A15/AI5 tape-out** (2nd US 2nm chip); and Samsung will **fab the I/O die of Google's 2nm "Icefish" TPU** (outsourcing back-end design only because 2nm orders outstrip internal staff — a demand tell, not a loss). The accumulating wins are real but still sit under JPM/Gokul's ~5-6% 2nm-share cap (overflow, not leadership). (4) **Brokers turn constructive into the July 30 print:** Jefferies' Silk Road desk (Cristina Titu) upgraded Samsung to BUY (07-13, reiterated 07-15) and BofA/Simon Woo called the correction overdone (DRAM ASP +50-60% QoQ; 2Q low-mid-40% ASP is mix-specific, not a 3Q trend). Positioning damage lingers — Korean single-stock leveraged ETFs on the memory names are down ~45% since May (@QQ_Timmy, 07-14). **Added this run:** named sources now anchor the 07-13 positioning washout — **Citi "Dr Mo" (Apabhai, 07-13)** on SEC −9.9% / ~$878m LETF selling / entry ~W230k → bounce toward ~W270k, and the **Goldman-panel PSI (07-13)** on foreign ownership at a 2009 low (46.6%) — plus **Didier's ASML post-2Q call (07-15)** flagging Samsung's just-announced fab likely already in ASML's EUV backlog, the **Wells Fargo CXMT deep-dive (07-14)** sizing the low-end China threat (~8% DRAM rev share, HBM 2-4yr behind), a first-party **CMM-D KV-cache benchmark** (~92% of DRAM at TP=8) firming the CXL optionality, and the **Atlas Peak HBM4 primer** (Samsung commercial HBM4 from early 2026 / MP Mar-2026 on a 4nm in-house base die, NVIDIA Vera Rubin + AMD MI455X, custom-HBM samples 2027). Net: additive across the capital-cycle, foundry, CXL and HBM legs; no house rating/PT superseded. **07-24 (SemiAnalysis, AMD Advancing AI 2026) + a 06-14 teardown backfill — three genuinely separate threads, and the useful one reframes the page's core HBM4 question.** (1) **The HBM4 pin-speed bake-off** turns "can Samsung get HBM4 price and yield right?" into a **binning** question: AMD's MI455X runs **12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s at 7.6 Gbps** while Rubin runs **8 stacks / 288GB / 22 TB/s at 10.7 Gbps** — Nvidia having pushed deliberately above the JEDEC HBM4 spec "precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X," which forced every supplier to rework HBM4 (delay now stated as **resolved**) and means Nvidia "must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD." Two-sided for Samsung and worth holding as such: falling short of Nvidia's spec now locks a supplier out of the **premium bin rather than out of HBM4**, which is a materially softer bear case than the HBM3E-era "fails qualification, discounts unqualified inventory" episode and lends the already-confirmed **MI455X socket** more strategic weight — but an above-JEDEC target also means tighter binning and lower top-bin yield, landing straight on the page's live 1c-yield debate. SemiAnalysis names no bin winner, and the "Memory Mania" bears (1c test yields "extremely low," HBM4 ES with higher jitter and weaker package execution than SK hynix's) are unchanged. (2) **The 2027 HBM-price call cuts against one of the page's own bull legs.** SemiAnalysis expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," strong enough that AMD cannot pass it through and takes a GM hit on the MI455X package — but if HBM reprices *toward* commodity-DRAM margins, the specific reason SemiAnalysis "turned bullish" on Samsung (its heavier commodity-DRAM mix being an advantage precisely because its HBM margin is the lowest of the three) narrows. Against that, the same BOM shows content being cut to pay for the price: MI455X DRAM falling from an original 2025 estimate of **138TB across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM** to a far lower DDR5 RDIMM figure with **LPDDR5X removed entirely** (the up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached tier gone), summarised as "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs" — sharpest here because Samsung is the largest LPDDR supplier and the page separately carries the *opposite* LPDDR5X/SOCAMM demand pull from NVIDIA's Vera CPU; those two now need netting, not stacking. Meta's custom MI450/MI455 (**HBM 12 stacks → 6, 12-Hi → 8-Hi**, at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers, on a program the same house says will "nuke AMD's volume at Meta"; 07-21/07-24) is the sharpest instance. (3) **The MI300X episode finally has a price tag:** "In 2023, Microsoft dropped AMD as a customer after MI300X due to unreliable Samsung 2023 HBM memory & poor software quality & skipped both MI325X and MI355X," with Microsoft only now returning via MI455X Helios — the page had the episode and the damaged-relationship line, but not the consequence, and **two skipped generations** is the hardest evidence on this page that HBM reliability failures carry multi-year rather than single-quarter commercial tails (blame attributed by SemiAnalysis to Samsung HBM *and* AMD software, not Samsung alone). (4) **Backfilled from the 06-14 STEEL teardown:** the **Kirin 9030 Pro carries Samsung's K4L2E165YD (12Gb LPDDR5X-9600, 1a node, "current memory rather than older-node inventory")**, but the **16GB Pro Max variants were found with both CXMT and Samsung packages** — CXMT marked CXDD7JEDM, packaged week 45 2025, at ~0.3 Gib/mm² G4 density ≈ others' 1z. That is **dual-sourcing inside a flagship Chinese handset with CXMT one node generation behind**, the first *measured* China-substitution datapoint on this page and one that sits above the low-end-consumer tier the BofA/WF/roadshow framings imply — a mobile-share watch item, still not a server/HBM threat. Net across all four: additive; no house rating, PT or estimate superseded. |

The 70%/2031 figure is the bull's visibility argument; the 5x-below-spot figure is the bear's ASP-cap argument. Neither is adopted — this is trade press relayed by a broker weekly, NOT a Samsung disclosure and NOT confirmed by the company. Do not model off the 70%.** ➤ **The same three-way LTA argument ran on the corpus the same day (@ParadisLabs vs an unnamed institutional bear vs @BenBajarin) and is carried as an open debate on `themes/hbm-memory.md`.** Also relayed: **Yongin Semiconductor Complex groundbreaking set for OCTOBER.** |
